Chickxel guide

You don't need to read from start to finish. Follow the first-time path, or choose the task you want to do and read only what you need.

First-time path

  1. Install and launchChoose the right download for your operating system and handle the first-launch warning.
  2. Learn the screenFind the canvas, toolbar, timeline, and other main areas.
  3. Draw an imageLearn the basic controls for colors, brushes, and selections.
  4. Save and exportSave your project and export sheets and GIFs.

Find a task

  • Turn an image into pixel art
  • Draw with AI
  • Animate with layer effects
  • Switch skins
  • Export a sprite sheet

In the app, press F1 and click any button or field to open its explanation. If you know the feature name, type it in the search box on the left.

Common controls

Basic controls that work the same everywhere in the app. Each part of the screen is covered in the Screen layout chapter. To follow the main features step by step, open the tutorials with Help > Tutorial… or the graduation-cap button at the right end of the menu bar.

Changing number fields

Size, coordinates, FPS, zoom, effect strength: every number field in the app changes in the same three ways.

  1. Click and typeClick the field and type a number. You can also type arithmetic such as 256*2 (add, subtract, multiply, divide). Press Enter or click elsewhere to confirm.
  2. Up/down buttonsClick the buttons at the field's right end to step by one (Ctrl steps by 10). Drag up or down on them to keep changing the value (Shift is 10× slower); while typing, the wheel works too.
  3. Right-click and pickRight-click the field for a list of common values. Recent: … is the value before the last change, handy for switching back and forth.

Details such as how expressions are calculated and the up/down buttons are in Number fields and menus.

Drawing with AI

Claude Code or Codex on your own computer drives Chickxel with your own account (no API key). Say something like "draw a 32×32 wooden barrel" and it draws right into the open image.

  1. Open AI chatCtrl+L or the AI button in the top bar. Pick the Claude or Codex tab and log in once.
  2. Describe what you wantInclude size, subject and colors for the best results. Progress shows while it draws.
  3. Undo if you don't like itEach reply is one undo step. Use Ctrl+Z or "Undo this step" under the reply.

In our testing, Codex's Astra model (gpt-6-astra) drew the best results. See the AI chat chapter for details.

Pixelate

Turn AI-generated art or any image into clean pixel art. Settings and scoring are explained in detail in the Pixelate AI images chapter.

Comparing cell by cell in the enlarged view

See the cell grid Chickxel actually used laid over the source, then check the result and the source side by side at the same size, one cell at a time.

  1. Show gridThe source view draws the cell borders the selected candidate used, so you can see right away whether the cells line up with the color blocks.
  2. EnlargeClick the result or the source in the large preview to open both at the same size.
  3. Inspect a cellClick a result pixel or a source cell to highlight where that color is used and which result pixel the source cell became.

Every enlarged-view control is described in Source and preview.

Turning images into pixel art

Drop in an image and Chickxel finds the pixel size, snaps to the grid, removes the background and reduces the colors. It builds one candidate per preset and shows them side by side with their scores, so you just pick the one you like.

  1. Add an imageOpen AI > Pixelate AI image… and drag an image in or paste it with Ctrl+V.
  2. Pick a candidateOne candidate is built per checked preset, each shown with its score. The enlarged view compares the result and the source cell by cell.
  3. InsertInsert it as a new layer in the current document or in a new tab.

Settings and scoring are covered in the Pixelate AI images chapter.

Removing and adding colors

Check each color in the result, remove the ones you don't need, and pick missing ones from the source.

  1. Show where a color is usedClick a color swatch or a result pixel to highlight where that color is used.
  2. Remove a colorRight-click to remove it; its pixels take the nearest remaining color. Right-click again or use Restore all to bring it back.
  3. Add a colorClick the + at the end of the swatches, then click a source cell to add that color to the result as a pinned color.

Pinned and excluded colors are described in detail in Source and preview.

Animation effects

Layer effects on without touching your pixels to create motion. Effect types and settings are in Layer effects.

Drag effects from the effect list

Add an effect without opening the effects window: drag it from the effect list onto a timeline cel. It goes onto that layer with its default settings and applies only to the frames you dropped it on.

  1. Open the effect listClick the Effects button at the top of the left toolbar (below the Files button) to open the effect list in the left column. Click it again to close it.
  2. Choose the framesDrag across the cels of the layer that should get the effect. If you don't select any, the effect goes only on the frame of the cel you drop it on.
  3. Drop the effect on the celsWhile you drag over the selected cels, the canvas already previews the effect. Dropping adds it for that frame range and opens the effect row in the timeline.

Double-click an effect to add it to the current cel. Drag the ends of its box in the effect row to change the range, and double-click the effect row to change its settings. Details are in Effect list.

Stacking effects

Stack as many effects as you like: outline, shadow, shine, shake, explosion, fade and more. Your pixels stay untouched, so you can toggle or reorder them anytime; saving bakes the result into the sheet.

  1. Open the effects windowRight-click the layer name in the timeline and choose Effects….
  2. Pick and stack effectsDouble-click an effect card or drag it into the list. Effects stack from the top of the list down, with a before/after preview.
  3. Set the frame rangeDrag in the frame strip under the preview to set each effect's range. Frame extension can add the animation frames for you.

Effect types and settings are covered in Layer effects.

Effect clipping

An effect with Clip to effect above on applies only to the pixels the effect above it made, not to the sprite. For example, you can paint just the outline with a gradient.

  1. Add a base effectStart with an effect that creates new pixels, such as outline or reflection.
  2. Add an effect right below itPut the effect you want on top of it — color overlay, shine, afterimage and so on — directly below the base effect.
  3. Turn on Clip to effect aboveIn the lower effect's settings, turn on Apply to > Clip to effect above. It shows indented in the list and the timeline.

Save a base effect with its clipped effects in the Custom tab to reuse it. The Apply to setting is described in Layer effects.

Using palettes

Import palettes and fit your sprite's colors to them. Every palette panel feature is in Palette.

Importing Lospec palettes

Browse the Lospec palette list of palettes by pixel artists worldwide inside the app, and import them. Each palette shows example art.

  1. Open Palette webChoose Palette web (Lospec)… from the menu button on a palette header.
  2. BrowseSort by popular or newest, and filter by color count, tag (e.g. gameboy, pico8) or name.
  3. ImportUse as palette replaces it, or Add to current palette adds only the missing colors.
  4. FavoritesStar a palette; choose Favorites in the sort box to browse and import it even without internet.

Reference site: lospec.com/palette-list (internet required — favorites work offline; palette copyrights belong to their authors)

Recoloring with a palette

Replace every color in a sprite with colors from a chosen palette in one step. Use it to try a different mood or to bring a whole game onto one palette.

  1. Pick a paletteOpen the palette with the colors you want — palettes imported from Lospec work too.
  2. Open Convert to palette colorsChoose Convert to palette colors… from that palette's menu button.
  3. Choose a target, rule and dithering, then OKPick the target (current cel, all frames of the current layer, or all layers) and Color matching. Nearest color maps to the closest colors; Brightness order spreads the shading evenly over the palette. Check the before/after preview; one undo reverts it.

Every rule and the dithering options are described in Convert to palette colors.

Skins

Draw several looks per layer and swap them. The layer tree and skins are explained in detail in the Layer tree · skins chapter.

Swapping skins

Split a character into child layers like head, body and arms, draw skins for each, and swap them in one click. A skin preset switches several layers at once.

  1. Split into child layersUse a new empty layer (Ctrl+Shift+N) as the parent and add new child layers (Shift+P)
  2. Add skins and drawRight-click a layer and choose an empty skin or a duplicate of the drawing under Add skin, then draw. With children… on a parent adds a same-named skin to all children and a skin preset.
  3. SwapPick a skin from the timeline skin chip or the skin row under the preview, or step with Alt+[ / Alt+]. In the Skin settings window, a preset card switches several layers together.

Empty cels in a skin stay empty, so duplicate a skin to start from the same art. Skins export as separate files per skin or as a Unity Sprite Library. See Layer tree · skins for details.

Tile and item skins

Put a tile and the items it drops under one parent and make each material (wood, dirt, ore) a skin. Every variant lives in one file at the same size and position.

  1. Nest them as child layersPut the block tile and its items under one parent layer as child layers (e.g. tile, item_1, item_2, item_3).
  2. One skin per materialOn the parent, make plank, dirt and ore skins with Add skin > With children…. One same-named skin preset switches every child together.
  3. Export in one goSaving exports per-skin sheets or a Unity Sprite Library, so the game only needs to switch the skin name.

Adding and editing skins is covered in Skins.

Unity integration

Values you set in Chickxel carry straight into Unity. The exported files are covered in the Files · saving · export chapter.

Pivot sync

Set an image's anchor point (pivot), such as a character's feet or a weapon's grip, in Chickxel, and saving carries it into Unity. No adjusting in Unity needed.

  1. Pick the Pivot tool (K)Drag the anchor on the canvas, or use the 5×5 buttons and X/Y fields in the tool settings.
  2. Choose the targetThe pivot of the layer selected in the timeline changes. For the anchor of the whole image (the canvas pivot), click Canvas W×H at the top left of the timeline.
  3. SaveSaving writes the pivot into the Unity settings file (.meta) next to the PNG sheet.

Every layer has its own pivot, so rotation and transforms center on it. Pick a Representative layer to use its pivot as the canvas pivot; layer atlas exports write each layer's own pivot.

Every Pivot tool setting is in Pivot tool.

Export

Use it to make sprite sheets (PNG) for your game. Set it up once, and every save rebuilds the sheets.

  1. Open the export windowCtrl+E. A preview of the sheets and the list of files to be written appear on the right.
  1. Set layout and skinsChoose the cell layout and spacing, and whether skins share one image or get their own files. You can split files by tag, layer or skin, and set the Unity import settings (including Sprite Library) here. Save settings you use often as presets.
  1. SaveEvery save rebuilds the PNGs and the Unity settings file (.meta) with these settings. The file browser shows only the .chickxel, and searching by an exported file name finds it.

Every export setting is in Export dialog.

Getting started

Chickxel is a pixel art editor for games (especially Unity 2D). Projects are saved as .chickxel, and saving also writes a PNG sprite sheet and a Unity .meta. Nothing else needs to be installed.

This chapter covers installation through your first project, plus help, updates, network use, feedback and accounts.

PageRead it when

First launchA security warning appears on first launch, or you want to know what to choose on the start screen
TutorialsYou want to learn the key features by following along on screen in the app
New projectYou start a new drawing from an empty canvas or a clipboard image
Getting help (F1)You want to know what a button or panel does right away, or want to search the guide
UpdatesA new version was announced, or you want to turn off or run update checks
Network and privacyYou want to know when Chickxel goes online
FeedbackYou want to report a bug, share an idea or support development
Chickxel accountYou sign in to or switch the account used for gallery and asset uploads
Account levelYou want to know how to earn XP or what the level badge means
Level rewardsYou want to see or choose the profile decorations you unlock by level

Installation

Download the ZIP for Windows, macOS or Linux and unzip it to run. You can also install with one terminal line; after that the app updates itself (see Updates).

Which file to download

OSDownload (latest version)Run
Windows 10/11Chickxel-windows-x64.zipUnzip and double-click Chickxel.exe
macOS 12 or later, Apple Silicon (M1 and later)Chickxel-macos-arm64.zipUnzip, move Chickxel.app to the Applications folder, then double-click it
macOS 12 or later, IntelChickxel-macos-x64.zipSame as above
Linux, x64 (Intel or AMD)Chickxel-linux-x64.zipUnzip and run Chickxel. If it doesn't start, turn on Allow executing file as program in its properties, or run chmod +x Chickxel
Linux, ARM64Chickxel-linux-arm64.zipSame as above

Your Mac's chip is shown in Apple menu → About This Mac. On Linux, uname -m prints x86_64 for x64 and aarch64 for ARM64. Changes are listed on the releases page.

Installing from the terminal

macOS and Linux — paste the line below into a terminal to download the build for your CPU, install it and launch it. macOS installs to /Applications/Chickxel.app; Linux installs to ~/.local/share/Chickxel and adds it to the applications menu (no sudo needed).

curl -fsSL https://raw.githubusercontent.com/1000Pro-1997/Chickxel/main/install.sh | sh

Windows — paste the line below into PowerShell to install to %LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\Chickxel with a Start menu shortcut. This way the 'Windows protected your PC' prompt doesn't appear.

irm https://raw.githubusercontent.com/1000Pro-1997/Chickxel/main/install.ps1 | iex

File associations and Open in Chickxel

These files and website links open in Chickxel. If Chickxel is already running, they open in that window.

What you double-click or clickWhat Chickxel does
.chickxelOpens it in a new document tab in the editor.
.chickxel-assetImports the assets inside it in the Asset Manager.
Open in Chickxel on an asset page of the websiteShows that package in the Asset Manager store (you click Install yourself).
  • Windows: start Chickxel once to register the associations and the Explorer preview (see Opening files). If you move the exe, start it once more.
  • macOS: the associations work once Chickxel.app is in the Applications folder. If another app opens these files, choose Chickxel in the file's Open With setting.
  • Linux: file associations and Open in Chickxel links aren't available yet.

First launch

On Windows and macOS, you need to get past your operating system's security warning the first time (Linux shows no warning). Once the app starts, the start screen appears with no document open; choose the folder or Unity project you want to work in.

Getting past the security warning

The app isn't developer-signed, so Windows and macOS show a warning the first time.

  • Windows "Windows protected your PC": More info → Run anyway.
  • macOS "unidentified developer": right-click → Open on Chickxel.app. If that doesn't work, go to System Settings → Privacy & Security → Open Anyway.
  • Linux: there's no warning. If it doesn't start, turn on Allow executing file as program in the file's properties, or run chmod +x Chickxel in a terminal.

The homepage download button opens the illustrated download guide. On Windows, open the ZIP file's Properties and select Unblock before extracting to avoid the protected-PC warning. If Edge stops the download as 'not commonly downloaded', choose More options → Keep → Show more → Keep anyway. PCs with Smart App Control enabled may block the app without offering a Run button.

Start screen

  • ① Open folder… picks a folder with your images. New project… next to it opens the New project dialog.
  • ② Double-click a row in the Recent folders or Recent files list, or select it and press Enter, to open it.
  • ③ Once you choose a folder, the file browser on the left shows its images.
  • ④ Double-click a project registered in Unity Hub to open its Assets folder. If the list is empty or can't be read, a note appears; Refresh project list reads it again.

After opening a document you can return to the start screen with File > Start screen.

The Tutorials window also opens at every launch. It lets you learn the key features by following along on screen, and you can turn it off in that window.

Display language and macOS keys

On first launch the display language follows your computer's language (Korean or English; English if there's no match). See Changing the display language to change it.

On macOS, read Ctrl in this guide as ⌘ (Command) (e.g. ⌘+S to save). The eyedropper is ⌥+click, and holding ⌘ switches temporarily to the Move tool. Menus and tooltips also show ⌘.

Tutorials

Tutorials teach a feature by having you follow along on the real screen. Only the spot to use stays lit, and a bubble next to it tells you what to do, step by step.

Opening it

The Chickxel tutorial window opens every time you start the app. After closing it, reopen it with ① the graduation-cap button at the right of the menu bar or Help > Tutorial….

Tutorials window

  • ② The four key features are large cards with an example clip. Click a card or Start to start that tutorial.
  • ③ Learn more holds the other seven tutorials. Ones you haven't done come first.
  • ④ A locked card has tutorials to finish first. Effect Maker opens after Draw with AI (MCP) and Use effects; the buttons under Finish these first: start the remaining ones.
  • ⑤ A finished tutorial shows a green Done, and its button becomes Do again.
  • ⑥ Turn on Don't show again and close the window to stop it opening at startup. Reopen it, clear the checkbox and close it to bring it back.

When you finish a tutorial, this window opens again so you can pick the next one. Progress and Don't show again are stored only on this computer.

Following along

  • The bubble shows the step number (1 / 6), the explanation and Back/Next (Finish on the last step). The X at its top right stops the tutorial.
  • A required step has no Next; do what the bubble says and it moves on by itself.
  • An optional step moves on with Next.
  • Windows without a bubble (such as the AI status check or the account window) stay usable during a tutorial.

The tutorials

TutorialWhat you do
Turn an image into pixel artLoad an example image into the AI image pixel cleanup window, change the quick settings, run one preset, and remove or add result colors
Turn 3D into pixel artLoad the example chick model into the 3D model window, rotate it, recolor it, set the output, camera, light and outline, then import it as a new tab
Use effectsAdd an outline effect to frames 3–5 of the example wolf, then look at the effect settings, the timeline effect row and the effect list's store download
Draw with AI (MCP)Open AI chat and learn the Claude and Codex sign-in, drawing settings, writing a request, layer locks, and connecting outside AI apps through MCP
Effect MakerIn the Effect Maker window, ask the AI for an effect in words, then learn the code box, preview, your effect list, inserting into a layer and uploading
Working the canvasZooming and panning, the temporary move with Ctrl and brush size, changing the canvas size and transforming the whole layer
Layers and framesAdding a layer, adding and deleting frames, and linking cels with the same picture
Layer groups and skinsGrouping with child layers, adding a skin and swapping it with the skin chip, and the stacking order of layers
TilemapsCreate a tilemap layer, pick tiles from the tileset, lay them on the canvas and show the cell grid
ExportingIn the Export window, add a row, switch it to GIF, and use the export-on-save checkbox to export several files at once
LevelsAccount level and how to earn XP, gallery uploads, and the level rewards in the account window (finishing it gives the sprout rewards; see Level rewards)

New project

The New project dialog creates a one-layer drawing from a canvas size and a background. You can also start from a clipboard image.

Opening the dialog

Use File > New project… (Ctrl+N), + on the tab strip, or New project… on the start screen. The document opens in a new tab; you choose its name and location when you first save. Created from the file browser, the dialog also has a file name field.

Settings

ItemDescription
① Paste the clipboard imageShown only when the clipboard holds an image. Details are under Starting from a clipboard image below.
② CanvasEnter the canvas size as width × height in pixels. Turn on the chain at the end to keep the ratio. The buttons below (16×16, 32×32, 16×32, 64×64, 128×128, 256×256) pick common sizes. You can change it later in Canvas size mode.
③ Transparent backgroundOn: the canvas starts transparent. Off: the canvas is filled with the secondary color.

Starting from a clipboard image

If the clipboard holds an image, a thumbnail and a ① Paste the clipboard image (width×height) checkbox appear at the top. Turning it on sets the canvas to the image size and puts the image on the first layer.

If you change the size, the image stays at the top left and anything outside the canvas is cut off. With transparent background off, it sits on the secondary color. Without an image copied in another program, an image copied inside Chickxel is used; images over 16384px wide or tall aren't offered.

Getting help (F1)

To find out what a button or panel does, press F1 and click it. For a feature you know by name, search in Help > Guide….

Choosing help on screen

  1. Press F1 or choose ① Help > Choose help on screen.
  2. The pointer turns into a question mark and ③ a note appears in the status bar. A yellow box surrounds the control under the mouse, showing whose help will open.
  3. Click the area you want to know about. The guide window opens at the place that explains it and briefly outlines that place in yellow.

While choosing, clicking a button doesn't run its usual action. Esc, pressing F1 again, a right-click or switching windows ends it.

Clicking an area with no linked page ends choosing and shows a note in the status bar. Search for those areas in ② Guide….

Choosing help in other windows

You can also choose help in the Pixelate, Effects, AI chat, Effect maker, Export, Skin settings, Preferences and Asset manager windows and in small dialogs.

  • In a window without a status bar, ④ a yellow-outlined note band appears at the top center. When the mouse reaches it, the band moves to the bottom edge.
  • Clicking an area with no linked page changes the text in ④ the band and keeps choosing.
  • A guide window opened from a dialog stays open after you close the dialog.

Guide window

  • ⑤ Type words in the search box to search the whole guide. Choose a result with ↑/↓ and open it with Enter. Matches are highlighted in yellow on the page.
  • ⑥ With an empty search box this is the contents by chapter. Expand a chapter and click a page name to open it.
  • ⑦ The page text. Links open in the same window, and Previous and Next at the bottom go to the neighboring pages.
  • ⑧ Open on website opens the current page in the website guide, and ⑨ Send a coffee opens the support page.
  • Guide… has no default shortcut (assign one in Customizing shortcuts). Only one guide window opens, and it remembers the last page you viewed while the app runs.
  • Esc clears the search, or closes the window when the search is empty. Shortcuts in the window are the keys you currently use, including changed ones.
  • Animated clips loop in the window without internet; Watch larger on the website shows them larger on the website.
  • The guide is in Korean when the display language is Korean, otherwise in English.
  • Drag over the text to select it and copy it with Ctrl+C. A selection stays within one paragraph.

Updates

Chickxel checks for a new version at every start and tells you when one is out. Click Update now in the update dialog and the app downloads, installs and restarts by itself.

New version notice

When a new version is available, the status bar says so, an ① Update button appears in the top bar and the update dialog opens. If another window is open, only the button appears. Click it to open the dialog again.

Update dialog

ItemWhat it does
② Update nowDownloads and verifies the file for your OS, then closes the app and restarts in the new version. Cancel stops the download. You're asked about unsaved work first, and if anything fails your current version stays.
③ LaterCloses the dialog; you're reminded the next time you start the app.
④ Don't show until the next updateThe automatic check skips this version and tells you about the next one. A manual check still shows it.
⑤ VersionsThe new version, your current version and the release date.
⑥ ChangesAll changes from the version after yours up to the new one, in your display language, or English when there are no notes in it. If they can't be downloaded, only the release page link is shown.
Open release pageOpens the release page in your browser without closing the dialog.

For development or test builds, when the install folder isn't writable, or when macOS runs the app from a temporary location, Update now is disabled and the dialog shows why. Then download it with Open release page and replace the app yourself. On macOS, moving Chickxel.app to the Applications folder and restarting fixes the temporary location.

On Linux, keep Chickxel in a folder your account can write to. If replacing it fails, the previous version stays and a notification tells you.

Checking manually and turning off automatic checks

  • ⑦ Help > Check for updates… checks any time. It tells you when you're up to date or why it couldn't check, and shows skipped versions too.
  • ⑧ Clear this checkbox in Edit > Preferences… > General to stop checking at startup.

Network and privacy

Chickxel has no usage tracking (analytics) or ads, and your images and files leave your computer only when you choose to upload them. It goes online only in the cases below, and an account is needed only for gallery and asset uploads.

When Chickxel goes online

WhenWhere and what
At startup and Check for updates…Asks GitHub for the latest version and its changes. The automatic check can be turned off (below).
When you open the Palette web (Lospec) windowDownloads palette lists and sample images from lospec.com. Favorite palettes are stored only on your computer.
When you use the AI chat or AI status windowThe Claude Code or Codex CLI installed on your computer connects to Anthropic or OpenAI with your login. The images and conversation the AI sees go to that service.
When you use the Chickxel account, gallery or asset uploadsSigns in to chickxel.com and uploads the picture or asset you chose (plus the editable .chickxel file if you turn that on when uploading to the gallery). Just starting the app doesn't connect (see Chickxel account).
The Store and Updates tabs of the Asset Manager, and the list of your uploaded effects in Effect MakerDownloads the public asset list, previews and author profile pictures from chickxel.com, and the packages you choose to install.
When you click links such as the website, Discord or supportOpens the address in your default browser.

The connection AI tools use to control Chickxel (MCP) is opened only on this computer (127.0.0.1) and can't be reached from outside.

Discord activity status only goes to the Discord app running on this computer. Your profile shows just the chick picture, "Playing Chickxel" and the elapsed time; nothing about your work is sent. To turn it off, clear Show activity on Discord ("Playing Chickxel") in Edit > Preferences… > General > Discord.

Turning off update checks

  • ① Clear this checkbox in Edit > Preferences… > General and Chickxel no longer checks for updates at startup. Manual checks still work (see Updates).

Feedback

Report bugs, annoyances and feature ideas on Discord or GitHub Issues. Including your version and the steps to reproduce helps get them fixed quickly.

Where to report

Please post bugs, annoyances and feature ideas on the Chickxel Discord. In the app, ① Help > ② Report a bug or idea (Discord)… opens it directly; in the ③ Help > About Chickxel… window, the ④ Discord button does the same. If you have a GitHub account, you can also use GitHub Issues (⑤ Report an issue in the About window).

What to include in a bug report

When reporting a bug, including the following helps get it fixed quickly:

  • Version: shown in ③ Help > About Chickxel… at ⑥.
  • OS: Windows 10/11, your macOS version (Apple Silicon or Intel), or your Linux distribution and version (x64 or ARM64).
  • Steps: what you did, what happened, and what you expected.
  • Error log: if an error dialog appeared, its log file (When errors occur). A screenshot helps too.

Supporting development

To support development, use Support Chickxel development on the website, the Send a coffee button at the top right of the in-app guide (F1), or ⑦ Buy the developer a coffee in ③ Help > About Chickxel…. The Website button in the same window opens the Chickxel website in the app's language.

Chickxel account

A Chickxel account is needed only to upload pictures to the chickxel.com gallery and share assets. Drawing, saving and exporting don't need it.

Signing in

  1. Open the account window with Help > Chickxel Account….
  2. Click the sign-in button for Google, GitHub or Discord. The chickxel.com sign-in page opens in your browser.
  3. Finish signing in in the browser; the app window switches to your account. You can close the browser tab.

Your account is created the first time you sign in. While waiting you can use Open Browser Again or Cancel Sign-in; after 5 minutes you're asked to try again.

Account window

  • ① The signed-in account's picture, display name, title, level badge and @handle, shown with your equipped level rewards (card, frame, effect and nickname effect).
  • ② Level N, the XP bar and the XP left to the next level (see Account level). The info box next to it explains how to earn XP.
  • ③ Level rewards: this level's reward (gold border and star until seen) and the XP left to the next one. Equipped rewards show a check; Change… lets you choose (see Level rewards).
  • ④ Profile: edit the display name and handle and click Save Profile. The handle is 3–32 lowercase letters, digits and hyphens, used in your gallery address and as your asset author ID. A taken handle can't be used, and the handle is locked once you publish an asset package.
  • ⑤ Open My Gallery Page opens your page on chickxel.com. Sign Out removes only this account, and Add Another Account… shows the sign-in buttons while keeping the current account.
  • ⑥ Other accounts signed in on this computer: each other signed-in account has Switch and a sign-out button.

Account button in the menu bar

The account button at the right end of the menu bar shows your picture, name and level badge when signed in, otherwise Sign In. With no saved accounts it opens the account window; otherwise it opens the account menu.

The account menu lists your signed-in accounts (the current one checked; click another to switch), Add Another Account…, Manage Account… (Sign In… when signed out) and Sign Out. The gallery button next to it is covered in Menu and top bar.

Good to know

  • Gallery and asset uploads use the current account. An account whose sign-in the server ended is removed from the list; switching to it opens the account window with "This account's sign-in has ended and it was removed from the list. Sign in again.".
  • If the browser goes straight to an account that's already signed in when you add another, switch accounts on that service or use another service. Signing in again with the same account doesn't add a duplicate.
  • Wherever an account or author appears (the account button, menu and window, Asset Manager, Effect Maker, Upload to Gallery and more), you see the picture, name, level badge and equipped frame and nickname effect. Hover over a cut-off name to see all of it.
  • With a saved account, Chickxel contacts chickxel.com at startup, when you first open the account menu, when you switch accounts or use the account window or uploads, and after each hour of signed-in app time. Sign-ins are kept in community-session.bin in the settings folder (see Preferences).

Account level

Your account level rises as you share pictures and assets on chickxel.com and stay active. Read this page to see how to earn XP (experience points) or what the Lv badge next to your name means.

Earning XP

You earn XP for each of these activities.

HowXP
Upload a picture to the gallery+10
Also share the editable .chickxel file with the picture+5
Publish a new asset package+10
Upload a new version of a published package+1
Each like from someone else on your art or assets+2
Like someone else's art or assets (3 a day count)+1
Daily check-in (your first app start or site visit of the day)+10
Each hour the app is open while you're signed in+1
  • You get the check-in the first time each day you start the app or visit chickxel.com while signed in. The status bar shows "Daily check-in +10 XP".
  • App time counts only while you're signed in. Even on several computers you get it once an hour.
  • A new day starts at 00:00 UTC (09:00 in Korea).

Levels and badges

Each level needs 1 XP more than the one before. Level 2 starts at 10 XP, level 3 at 21 XP and level 100 at 5,841 XP; there's no maximum level.

The badge color depends on your level.

LevelDefault level badge
1–4Dim gray
5–9Bronze
10–19Silver
20–29Gold
30–49Cyan-to-purple crystal
50+Rainbow

The badge appears on the account button and window, in the Upload to Gallery window, next to authors in the Asset Manager, and next to names on chickxel.com. Hover over it to see the level and how to earn XP.

Good to know

  • When you level up, the status bar shows "Level up! Lv N".
  • Moving badges are badge looks from level rewards. The default badge doesn't move.
  • If the server doesn't report a level (an older server, for example), there's no badge.
  • If a like is withdrawn, or a picture or asset package is deleted (by you, an admin or a report), the XP it earned (likes included) is removed. Its storage quota comes back right away.
  • Deleted pictures and assets stay on the server, so an admin can restore them.

Level rewards

Each level from 2 to 100 unlocks one reward that decorates your profile (99 in all). Rewards come in six slots, and you wear one per slot.

Reward slots

SlotWhere and how it shows
Title (9)Text under your name by the large picture. Higher levels get rarer colors; from level 71 they flow in rainbow colors.
Level badge (20)The look of the badge next to your name, moving with flowing colors, glowing borders, sparkles or embers.
Frame (30)A pixel frame around your picture: a small look on small pictures, and a larger look with ornaments such as wings or crowns on large pictures (the account window and your site profile).
Effect (20)An animation around the large picture (sparkle dust, petals, fireflies, planets, runes and more).
Card (10)An animated background for your profile header (the top of the account window and your site profile).
Nickname effect (10)Colors and motion for the letters of your name (neon, wave, glitch, fire, galaxy, rainbow and more). The text keeps its width.

Choosing rewards

  1. Click Change… in the account window, or use the account menu on chickxel.com, to open the level rewards page.
  2. Click a reward or None in any slot. It changes right away and looks the same wherever your name appears in the app and on the site.

Every slot is on Auto by default: it wears the highest-level reward you've unlocked and switches by itself as you level up.

Good to know

  • When you unlock a reward, the badge on the account button sparkles and Manage Account… in the account menu shows how many you haven't seen (2 new). Opening the account window marks them as seen.
  • If your level drops because something you shared was deleted, that level's rewards lock again and slots that had one chosen go back to Auto.
  • Finishing the Levels tutorial gives you the title Pixel Sprout and the frame Sprout Frame at any level. Auto treats them as the lowest rewards.

Screen layout

This chapter covers the areas of the main window and the controls shared by many windows. Click a name in the table to open that area's page.

⑨ PreviewPlay the animation, switch skins, copy or save frames

The timeline, effects, scripts, assets and export have their own chapters: Timeline, Layer effects, Making script effects, Asset manager, Export and sharing.

Drag the borders between panels to resize them; the sizes are kept for the next launch.

Menu and top bar

Look at the top of the window to find a command, see how the current tool works, or zoom. The menu bar has every command; the top bar below it has the buttons you use most.

  • ① Tool name: the current tool and its shortcut
  • ② Usage hint: the tool's mouse actions and modifier keys
  • ③ View options: turn grids, pivots and other overlays on or off (View options)
  • ④ Zoom box and ⑤ Fit: canvas zoom (below)
  • ⑥ AI chat: open or close the AI chat window (Ctrl+L)

Menus

Each menu item shows its shortcut on the right. You can change shortcuts in Preferences.

MenuOpen it to
_FileOpen, save and export files, or upload to the gallery (Files chapter)
_EditUndo, copy and paste, flip, transform and canvas size, convert to palette colors, Preferences
_ViewZoom, view options, switch tabs, file browser, movement test
F_rame·LayerPlay, and work with frames, layers, skins and tags (Timeline and Layer tree · skins chapters)
_ToolsPick tools, drawing options, Asset Manager, Effect Maker (Tools chapter)
_AIAI chat, connect other AI apps, references, pixelate (AI and Pixelate chapters)
3_DOpen 3D model… (3D models chapter)
_HelpChoose help on screen (F1), the guide, the shortcut list, updates, bug reports (Getting started chapter)

Zoom and Fit

The canvas zooms from 1× to 64× in whole steps. The zoom is kept for the next launch (800% at first).

ControlResult
Type in ④ the zoom boxRounds to the nearest whole zoom (150% → 200%).
Right-click the zoom boxPick ⑦ Fit to window or a value from 100% to 6400%.
Minus and plus buttonsZoom out or in one step (Ctrl+-, Ctrl+=).
Turn on ⑤ FitFits the canvas to the window whenever you open an image (on at first).
ShortcutsCtrl+0 fits, Ctrl+1 shows actual size. Wheel zoom is on the Canvas page.

Other buttons

  • ⑫ Gallery button: uploads the current drawing to the gallery. It sparkles briefly after you save, inviting you to share.
  • ⑬ Account button: shows your picture and level and opens the account menu (Chickxel account).
  • Update button: appears on the top bar only when a new version is out (Updates).

View options

Turn on a view button on the top bar when you want drawing aids such as grids or neighboring frames on the canvas. They show only on screen, never in the artwork.

View buttons

ButtonWhat you see when it's on
⑧ Pixel grid
Ctrl+G		A line around every pixel when zoomed in enough (on at first)
⑨ Tile grid
Ctrl+Shift+G		A line every tile
⑩ Tiled mode
Ctrl+Shift+T		The image repeated around itself, to check seamless patterns
Show pivotThe pivots of layers and the canvas: the points they rotate and line up around (on at first)
⑪ Onion skin
Ctrl+J		Neighboring frames faintly overlaid, for spacing motion
Focus
`		Only the current layer clear; the other layers faded
Effects
Ctrl+Shift+E		The canvas with layer effects applied (see Effects view below)

Settings

For buttons with settings, click the small triangle on their right or right-click the button.

  • ⑧ Pixel grid: line color and the zoom it appears from (6× at first)
  • ⑨ Tile grid: tile size (16×16 at first) and line color
  • ⑩ Tiled mode: how many tiles repeat (Auto, 3×3, 5×5, 7×7) and dimming the surroundings
  • ⑪ Onion skin: how many frames before and after (1 each at first), intensity (30%), and red/blue tint for previous/next
  • Focus: how visible the other layers are (30% at first)

The same commands are in the _View menu, where Grid settings… sets the grid colors and tile size in one place.

Effects view

When effects get in the way of editing the drawing itself, turn off ⑫ Effects. ⑬ The canvas shows the drawing without effects, while ⑭ the preview keeps showing them.

Same as View > Show effects on canvas (Ctrl+Shift+E).

Good to know

  • Views and their settings are kept for the next launch, except the effects view, which turns back on.
  • With Show pivot off, the Pivot tool still shows the pivot you're editing.
  • With effects off, onion skin, previous-frame peek, the eyedropper and fill also use the drawing without effects. Exports don't change.
  • Layers drawn entirely by effects, such as text layers, still show with effects off.

To choose how artwork beyond the canvas is shown, see Outside-canvas view.

Outside-canvas view

Use the Outside button on the top bar to choose how artwork beyond the canvas is shown. Saved artwork and exports don't change.

Click ① Outside: view state to cycle Opaque → Translucent → Hidden, or open the list with its arrow or a right-click. The choice is kept across launches.

Tilemaps, floating selections, resize and transform previews, and the child layers of an empty layer follow this view outside the canvas too.

Number fields and menus

The ① number fields throughout the app work the same in every window: click to type, use ③ the up/down buttons, or pick from ② the right-click list.

Up/down buttons

Click or drag up and down on ③ the up/down buttons to change the value. Dragging the number field itself doesn't change it.

ActionResult
Click a buttonSteps by one; hold Ctrl (macOS ⌘) to step by 10.
Drag up/down on the buttonsUp increases and down decreases, one step every 3 pixels (stops at the minimum and maximum).
Shift+dragChanges 10× slower (one step every 30 pixels).
Ctrl (macOS ⌘)+dragChanges 10 steps at a time (works with Shift too).
Drag to the top or bottom of the screenThe cursor wraps to the other side so you can keep going.

Changing modifiers mid-drag doesn't make the value jump. One drag is one undo step.

Typing and expressions

Click a number field to type with the whole number selected. While typing, each wheel notch changes it by one step. Press Enter or click elsewhere to confirm.

  • Integer and decimal fields accept arithmetic such as 256*2 (512 once confirmed).
  • Use +, -, *, /, parentheses and a minus sign; multiplication and division come first.
  • Integer fields drop the fraction (7/2 → 3). Decimal fields round to the digits shown.
  • Invalid expressions, division by zero and values too large are rejected; the previous value stays.

Right-click value list

When not typing, right-click a number field for ② a list of values suited to it.

  • Values show their unit (px, °, %, ms), and the current value has a dot.
  • Some fields have an action at the top (for example Fit to window in the zoom box).
  • The last value used in fields of the same kind during this session appears as Recent: value.
  • Right-clicking while typing opens the cut, copy and paste menu instead.

Menus and popups

Only one menu or popup is open at a time across the app. It closes when you click or scroll outside it, press Esc, move or switch windows, or press the button that opened it again.

Size fields

Every size input has the same [width] × [height] (unit) [chain] shape. While the chain (lock ratio, on at first) is on, changing one side makes the other follow the ratio it had when you turned the chain on.

Info badges and collapsible groups

Hover the small square with an i (info badge) for a longer explanation, or click it to show it right away. Groups with an arrow next to their title collapse and expand when you click the title.

Buttons and menu items with a shortcut show it at the top of their tooltip in a green box.

Toolbar

Pick tools from the vertical bar on the far left. Hover an icon to see its name and shortcut. If the window is short, only the tool slots scroll.

Tool slots

The slots in order from the top. Click a tool name for its page.

SlotTools (shortcut)
1Pencil (B), Gradient pencil (Shift+B)
2Eraser (E)
3Fill (G), Gradient (F)
4Eyedropper (I)
5Shape (R)
6Text (T)
7Rectangle select, Ellipse select (M), Lasso select (Q), Magic wand (W)
8Move (V)
9Dodge/burn (D)
10Dithering (no default shortcut)
11Replace color brush (C)
12Pivot (K)
139-slice (S)

Switching tools in a grouped slot

A slot with several tools has ② a small triangle in its corner. Click to pick the tool shown; hold for over 0.4 seconds or right-click to expand ③ the list. The tool you pick becomes the slot's icon.

M picks the selection tool you used last; pressing again within 10 seconds cycles Rectangle → Ellipse → Lasso → Magic wand.

File browser, Effects, Save and Revert buttons

  • ① The folder button collapses and expands the file browser (F8).
  • ⑥ The Effects button opens and closes the effect list in the same column. With both on, the column splits into files (top) and effects (bottom).
  • ④ Save is enabled only with unsaved changes, and ⑤ Revert to saved only when there is a file on disk to go back to (Revert to saved).
  • Whether the file browser and effect list are shown is kept for the next launch.

File browser

Use the file browser on the left to find and open the files you work on. Click ② a thumbnail to open that file in a new document tab.

Choosing a folder

  • Open a folder with Ctrl+Shift+O. The last folder opens again next launch.
  • Click a folder in ① the folder tree to see its files.
  • With ④ Include subfolders on (the default), files in folders below show too.
  • Open Unity project (Ctrl+Shift+U) lists the projects registered in Unity Hub and opens the chosen project's Assets folder.

List and search

  • Images, videos and 3D models all appear. The list updates as soon as the folder changes.
  • ⑥ A blue badge marks a Chickxel project (.chickxel), a film badge a video, a cube badge a 3D model.
  • Type in ③ the search box to filter by file name.

Project bundles

Images and videos exported from a project are bundled into the project's tile, which keeps the list short.

  • ⑧ Click the arrow (or press Ctrl+→) to expand; click again or press Ctrl+← to collapse.
  • ⑨ Expanded files appear with the project inside a black outline. Open, drag and delete them like any file.

New file and new folder

⑤ The icons on the right of the title row are New pixel file, New folder and Refresh.

New pixel file creates and opens a new drawing in this folder after you set its name and size (New project). It never overwrites a file with the same name.

Selecting and dragging

  • Select several: Ctrl+click, Shift+click, or drag a rectangle
  • Add a picture as a new layer: drag the file onto the canvas (Dropping in a layer)
  • Copy to another app: drag the file to Explorer, Finder or another app

Unsaved changes (red dot)

⑦ A red dot means the file has changes you haven't saved. Closing its tab asks whether to save.

Good to know

  • Hidden folders and folders whose names start with a dot don't show in the tree.
  • Video thumbnails need the ffmpeg video tool installed.
  • If another app changes an open file, you're asked whether to reload it.
  • Hold Ctrl (macOS ⌘) while dragging a rectangle to add to the selection.
  • Turning on the effect list too splits this column in two.

File menu and details

Right-click a file in the file browser when you want to rename it, delete it or see its details. Select ⑩ several files first to get ⑪ the commands that apply to all of them.

Right-click menu

ItemWhat it does
New project…Creates a new drawing in this folder.
Pixelate AI image…Cleans up an AI-made picture into tidy pixel art (Pixelate).
Merge…Combines several files into one project (Merging files).
RenameRenames the file (F2).
Open in Explorer/FinderOpens the file's folder in your computer's file manager.
Select image folderGoes to the subfolder that holds the file.
Delete (trash)Moves the files to the app trash (Delete, macOS ⌘+Backspace).
Delete .chickxel files only (trash)Deletes the projects but keeps the pictures exported from them.
Restore last deletedBrings back the files you just deleted (Ctrl+Z).
View app trash…Opens the app trash.
Details…Shows the file's information (Details below).

Renaming

  1. Select one file and press F2.
  2. Type the new name and press Enter (Esc cancels).

You can leave out the extension; it's kept. Renaming a project renames its exported files too.

Details

Choose Details… when you want a file's size, dates and similar facts. Values can be copied, and each kind of file adds more:

  • Pictures: pixel size and frame count
  • Videos: size, length and frames per second (with ffmpeg)
  • Chickxel projects: canvas size, frame and layer counts, and the export list (below)
  • Folders: how many files and folders are inside and their total size

Export list

Use it to see which files a project makes when saved, without opening the project. The Export list is at the bottom of a project's details.

  • Check box: whether that file is made when you save.
  • Settings…: opens the project in the export window to change it.
  • Under each row: the files made and their sizes, with open-folder and trash buttons. Nothing made yet reads “None yet — made when you save”.
  • At the bottom: files made earlier that the current settings no longer make. You can delete them here.

Merging files

Select two or more .chickxel or PNG files and choose Merge… from the right-click menu to combine them into one .chickxel. Other formats and videos can't be merged.

  • ⑫ Order: the order you selected the files in at first. Drag rows or use the up/down buttons (top = first).
  • ⑬ Name: suggested automatically from the words the file names share (potato_growing, potato_mature → potato).
  • ⑭ Mode: see the table below (As layers at first). With Under one layer, Empty thumbnail creates it with all child layers hidden.
  • ⑮ Canvas size (at first the largest width and height), anchor (9 directions) and Delete originals (off at first; originals go to the app trash).
  • ⑯ A preview (played if there are several frames) and ⑰ How it will look in the timeline show the result and notes on what is kept.

Modes

ModeResult
As layersOne layer per image file; a .chickxel comes in whole with its layer tree and skins (first file on top)
Under one layerOne empty layer with a child layer per file (sizes kept). If a .chickxel has a layer with children, they go under its first parent.
As skinsOne skin per file on a single layer (skin name = file name), for tile or item variants swapped in one spot.
As framesFrames appended in file order.

Layer trees, skins, effects and tags in .chickxel files carry over in every mode. The result is saved as <name>.chickxel in the first file's folder and opened. If that name is taken, nothing is merged and you're told.

Deleting and the app trash

Deleted files (Delete) and originals removed by merging go to the app trash without asking. Press Ctrl+Z in the file list to restore the last deletion.

  • ⑱ In the window opened from the right-click menu or File > View app trash…, see each item's deletion time, days left, size and a preview.
  • ⑲ Restore (double-click), delete permanently or empty the trash. An item isn't restored if its original place has a file with the same name.
  • ⑳ Retention policy: Time limit (off at first; 1–3650 days, 30) and Size limit (on at first; 0.1–1000 GB, 1 GB; oldest go first). Also in Edit > Preferences….

Their PNG and .meta files are kept too. The app trash is separate from the Windows or macOS trash and lives in the app settings folder.

Palette

Use the palette at the top of the color panel to pick the color you paint with and to keep colors you use often. Click a swatch to paint with it.

Below ① the primary/secondary slots you can stack ② several palettes and add one with ③ New palette (DawnBringer 32 at first).

Primary and secondary colors

  • ① The left mouse button paints the primary color, the right button the secondary (transparent at first).
  • Click a slot to edit that color in the color picker below.
  • Swap the two with the button beside them or X.

Stacked palettes and the active palette

  • ④ Each palette's header has a collapse arrow, its name (double-click to rename), the color count and a menu button.
  • ⑤ The blue line marks the active palette: the one you clicked last. The Edit menu and the AI use this palette.
  • The palette list is kept for the next launch.

Picking swatches

  • Click for the primary color, right-click for the secondary. Hover to see the color code (HEX).
  • ⑥ A white border marks the current primary color, ⑦ a blue border the current secondary.
  • To remove a color, make it the primary color and choose Remove primary color from palette from the menu.

Palette menu

⑧ The menu acts on that palette only, and can be undone.

ItemWhat it does
Add current colorAdds the color you're editing to the end.
Remove primary color from paletteRemoves the swatch matching the primary color.
Sort by hueGroups colors by family (red, yellow, green…) and orders each family by brightness.
Load palette (.hex/.gpl)…Replaces the colors with those of a palette file (.hex, .gpl).
Palette web (Lospec)…Finds and imports palettes from the web (Palette web).
Save palette (.hex)…Saves it as a .hex file.
Create palette from imageFills the palette with the colors in the drawing (most used first, up to 256).
Convert to palette colors…Changes the drawing's colors to this palette (Convert to palette colors).
Rename…Renames the palette.
Move upMoves it up one place.
Move downMoves it down one place.
Clear paletteRemoves every color.
Delete paletteDeletes the palette (not the only one).

Palette web (Lospec)

Open Palette web (Lospec)… from the palette menu when you want a palette someone else made. It shows the lospec.com palette list inside the app (internet required).

Finding a palette

  • Filter by sort order (popular, downloads, newest, name) and by tag (e.g. gameboy). The sort is remembered.
  • Filter the color count with All, Max, Min or Exact. Scroll to the end or press Load more for more.
  • Selecting a palette shows its colors and example art made with it.

Importing

Use as palette (same as double-click) replaces the palette; Add to current palette adds only new colors. There's also Save as .hex and View on Lospec.

Favorites

  • Favorites: click the star next to a palette to add or remove it. Favorites stay on your computer.
  • Choose Favorites in the sort box to show only favorites. No internet needed.
  • If Lospec can't be reached, click View favorites next to the error.

Palette copyrights belong to their respective authors.

Convert to palette colors

Convert to palette colors replaces the image's colors with palette colors. Open it from the palette menu or the _Edit menu.

  • ⑨ Choose the target from current cel (inside the selection, if any), all frames of the current layer or all layers and frames (locked layers skipped).
  • ⑩ Color matching decides which palette color each image color becomes (table below; the last rule is remembered).
  • ⑪ Dithering is No dithering (default) or Ordered dithering (Bayer 4×4), with any rule.
  • ⑫ A before/after preview and ⑬ the change in color count help you choose.

Alpha is kept and fully transparent pixels are left alone. The conversion is one undo step.

Color matching rules

RuleColor it picks
Nearest colorThe most similar color by color distance. Pick RGB (redmean), OKLab or CIEDE2000 in Color distance right below; the last two are closer to how differences look.
Brightness orderRanks the image's colors by lightness and spreads them over the palette sorted by lightness, so light and dark survive even with different hues.
Palette orderRanks by lightness but maps onto the palette's listed order (first color = darkest). Suits palettes laid out as shade ramps.
Hue firstAmong the closest hues, the closest lightness. Grays stay gray.
Lightness onlyThe closest lightness, ignoring hue. Suits black-and-white or 4-color Game Boy-style palettes.

Brightness order and palette order rank all target cels together, so a color maps the same way in every frame.

Dithering

Without dithering, colors form bands; ordered dithering mixes two palette colors in a pattern to show the shade between them.

Colors almost equal to a palette color aren't dithered. With the nearest color rule, a pattern is used only when it's closer to the original than a single color.

Color picker

The color picker below the palettes changes the color you're editing (primary or secondary). Switch picking modes with ⑥ the tabs.

It has ① an alpha bar, ② RGB, ③ HSV and ④ alpha (A)/HEX inputs and ⑤ recent colors, with Layer colors at the bottom.

Picking modes

ModeLayout
⑦ Square (default)Saturation/value box + vertical hue bar
⑧ Honeycomb127 hexagonal cells (white in the center, richer and darker outward) and 13 gray cells
⑨ Color wheelOuter ring = hue, inner square = saturation/value
⑩ SlidersR, G, B, H, S, V bars

Input fields

  • R, G, B and A are 0–255, H is 0–360°, S and V are 0–100%. HSV, sliders and honeycomb keep the current alpha.
  • The HEX field accepts #RGB, #RGBA, #RRGGBB and #RRGGBBAA (the # is optional).
  • Press Enter or click elsewhere to confirm; invalid input reverts.

Recent colors

⑤ Recent colors keeps the last 16 colors you painted with, newest first (not eraser, dodge/burn, gradient or transparent). Click for primary, right-click for secondary.

Collapsing

Collapse the “Color” header and ⑤ “Recent colors” separately with their chevrons. Collapsing “Color” leaves the header and recent colors and gives the palettes more room. The state is kept for the next launch.

Layer colors

⑪ Layer colors shows the colors used in every frame of the selected layer, most used first (no transparent). The number by the header is the count.

Selected layerColors counted
Normal layerThe art in every frame, including outside the canvas
Layer with childrenIts own art and all its descendant layers
Layer with several skinsThe art of the skin currently shown
  • Click a swatch for the primary color, right-click for the secondary. Up to 1024 colors are shown.
  • Click the header to collapse it. While collapsed it doesn't count colors, and the state is kept for the next launch.

Color pickers in other windows

Clicking a color box in other windows, such as effect settings or Replace color, opens a color popup with the same four modes. The palettes are on its right, with two buttons below for picking a color.

  • ⑫ Pick from image: shows the current frame enlarged in a separate window and picks the pixel you click (picture below). Hidden with no document open.
  • ⑬ Pick from screen: captures the whole screen and picks the color where you click, other apps included. A magnifier and HEX by the cursor show the color; Esc or right-click cancels.
On macOS it needs the Screen Recording permission (allow Chickxel in System Settings → Privacy & Security → Screen Recording, then restart the app). On Linux it works only in an X11 session; under Wayland the button is disabled.

⑭ In the Pick a color from the image window, the wheel zooms and dragging pans. Click to pick that pixel and close; Esc closes without picking. ⑮ The bottom row shows the hovered pixel's color, HEX and coordinates.

Canvas

The canvas shows the current frame (all visible layers combined), and tools draw on the current layer's cel.

Zoom and pan

ActionResult
Mouse wheelZooms around the point under the cursor (1–64×).
Ctrl (macOS ⌘)+wheelChanges the brush size.
Space+drag, middle-button dragPans the view.
Ctrl+= · Ctrl+- · Ctrl+0 · Ctrl+1Zoom in · zoom out · fit · actual size. For the zoom box, see Menu and top bar.
Hold HShows the previous frame while held (with a note at the top left).

Editing layers with their own size and empty layers

  • Selecting a layer with its own size (such as a child layer) shows ② the whole frame with ① that layer's area outlined. Coordinates are relative to the layer's area.
  • Selecting an empty layer with only children shows a dashed outline and name over each child layer; drag them to arrange (Arranging).
  • A layer's 9-slice border appears as dashed guides.

View overlays

These overlays are switched with the view options and never become part of the image.

OverlayWhat it shows
③ CheckerboardTransparent areas
④ Pixel gridPixel borders (from the chosen zoom up)
⑤ Tile gridA line every tile (16×16 and light blue at first)
⑥ Symmetry guideThe symmetry drawing axis (canvas center, or the pivot with Mirror around pivot). The brush outline shows faintly on the mirrored side.
⑦ PivotThe reference point for rotation and placement: a large circle and cross for the current layer, small dots with name labels for other layers and the canvas (blue for layers with their own size, orange otherwise).
⑧ SelectionThe dashed outline of the selected area
⑨ Onion skinNeighboring frames overlaid translucently

⑩ In tiled mode you edit the center tile; the surrounding tiles are copies.

Shown only while editing

  • A hidden layer you select shows translucently on the canvas only, so you can keep drawing.
  • While using the Move tool, pixels pushed outside the canvas show dimmed (Move).
  • If the current layer is locked, a note at the bottom left says you can't draw on it.
  • Brush tools outline the brush shape at the cursor.

Computing effects

Heavy effects never freeze the window. A slow frame shows a gray veil and progress bar until it's done. While you draw, new strokes appear first and effects catch up.

To hide effects on the canvas, turn off the effects view.

Document tabs and split view

The tab strip above the canvas has a tab for each open document. Click a tab to switch documents, or split the canvas into left and right panes.

Opening, switching and closing tabs

  • ① The title is the file name, ② a red dot means unsaved changes, and ③ hovering shows the full path.
  • The file browser, Open and New project open new tabs. A file that's already open, even in the other pane, goes to its tab.
  • Zoom, position, current frame, layer, selection and undo history are kept per tab.
  • Close with ④ X, a middle click or Ctrl+W. ⑤ + creates a new project in a new tab.
  • After closing with X, the next tab's X lands in the same spot so you can keep closing. When tabs overflow, scroll the wheel over the strip.
  • Closing every tab shows the start screen.

Tab menu and tab order

  • Drag tabs sideways to reorder them. Ctrl+Tab / Ctrl+Shift+Tab cycle through the focused pane's tabs.
  • ⑥ Right-click a tab for Close, Close other tabs and Move to the other pane.
  • A never-saved new project's tab also has Temporary title…. The name shows on the tab and is suggested as the file name when you first save.

Splitting the view

  • Choose Move to the other pane (tab menu, ⑦ the button at the end of the strip, or the View menu), or drop a tab on the right 40% of the canvas, to split it into ⑧ a right pane.
  • Drag the divider to share the width. Each pane has its own tab strip.
  • The timeline, preview and panels work on the focused pane (⑨ the accent line). Click the other pane to focus it.
  • Closing or moving the last tab of a pane merges the view back into one.

Preview tab

⑩ Open as tab in the preview header opens the document you're editing in a green preview tab on the other side (splitting the view if needed).

  • It shows the same playback, background and skins as the preview. The wheel zooms, dragging pans, double-click fits and right-click opens the preview menu.
  • There is only one preview tab; clicking the preview image brings it forward. It closes when all document tabs close.
  • You can move it like other tabs, but it never becomes the editing document and isn't reopened next launch.

Reopening at the next launch

Tabs open when you quit reopen next launch with their split, order and focus. Missing files, never-saved documents and the preview tab are skipped.

Preview

The preview at the top right plays the animation, effects included, at real speed, separately from the frame you edit.

NumberItem and use
①Playback view. It plays the tag selected in the timeline, or loops all frames. Click it for the enlarged view.
②Preview FPS (0–60, 0 at first). 0 uses each frame's own duration.
③Click to cycle Fit → 1× → 2× → 4×, or pick from the list with the small arrow or a right-click.
④Background. Click to cycle checker → black → white → gray → green → custom, or open the list with the small arrow or a right-click.

Playback

  • Play or stop with the play button or Shift+Enter.
  • A short tap of Space (under 0.4 seconds) in the main window also plays or stops; holding it while dragging pans. A focused text field gets the key instead.
  • If effects fall behind, playback holds the current frame until the next is ready.
  • FPS, zoom and background are app settings, so they carry over to other documents and the next launch.

Collapsing the preview

⑤ Click the arrow at the right end of the header (Collapse preview) to leave only the header; Tool options takes the space. Click again to expand. The state is kept for the next launch.

Copying and saving

⑥ Right-click the preview image for copy and save commands. During playback they use the frame shown when you right-clicked.

CommandWhat it does
Copy imageCopies the shown frame as a transparent PNG, without the background.
Copy enlarged (×4)Copies a crisp 4× PNG (handy for chats).
Save frame…Saves <document>_<frame number>.png.
Save as GIF…Opens the Export dialog with the first GIF row selected (adding one if needed).

Background

⑦ Pick a background from the list; choosing the custom color opens the color picker. The background is for the preview only, never in copies, saved frames or exports.

Skins

With layers that have several skins or with skin presets, a Skins row appears below the preview. Pick a preset first, then the skin to show for each layer. Click a layer name or Settings… to open the skin settings window.

Enlarged view and preview tab

Click the preview image to open ⑧ the enlarged view, which shows the same playback, background and skins at the largest whole zoom that fits.

  • Only one opens, and you can resize it. Space plays or stops, Esc closes and right-click opens the preview menu.
  • In the enlarged window and the preview tab, the wheel zooms around the cursor (up to 64×) and dragging pans. Double-click fits again (0 too in the enlarged window).
  • While a preview tab is open, clicking the image brings that tab forward instead.

Movement test

Movement test (F5) lets you move a character with the keyboard, as in a top-down or platformer game, using your document's tag animations.

Check the animation for each state, the pivot, body size and collisions before going to a game engine. Edits show up shortly after; click the view on the left to control it.

  • ① Mode: Top-down walks in 8 directions; Platformer is a side view with running, jumping and climbing.
  • ② Character: the document to move. It starts as the tab active when you opened the window; pick another open tab from the list.
  • ③ Show colliders: draws the collision boxes of the character (green), terrain (red) and ladders (blue).
  • ④ Map: lays down a tilemap layer from an open tab to move on. None (empty room) gives a practice area.
  • ⑤ Terrain floors: tile cells on the chosen floors become solid walls and ground (several floors, hidden ones too).
  • ⑥ Ladder floors: in platformer, cells on the chosen floors become ladders (terrain wins where they overlap).
  • ⑦ Climb speed (px/s): how fast the character climbs ladders and walls.
  • ⑧ Allow wall climbing: when on, the character can cling to terrain walls and climb.

Controls

KeyWhat it does
WASD / arrow keysTop-down: move in 8 directions. Platformer: move sideways and climb ladders and walls.
SpacePlatformer jump; release early for a lower jump. With nothing to grab, W/↑ jump too.
RReturns to the start (same as Reset position (R)).

View and movement settings

SettingWhat it does
Zoom (integer)View zoom (1–16×), set to suit the character at first.
Show grid (1 cell = PPU)Draws a grid of PPU-sized cells (tile cells with a map).
Show pivotDraws a cross at the pivot.
Undirected sprites face rightTreats animations without a direction (idle, run) as facing right. Turn off if they face left; they then flip when moving right.
Move speed (px/s), Gravity (px/s²), Jump force (px/s), Max fall speed (px/s)Movement values for each mode. Press Enter or Esc in a field to return the keyboard to the view.

Map and terrain

  • Terrain floors start chosen by name (top-down: Walls, Objects, Water; platformer: Ground). Changing them returns the character to the start.
  • Top-down collides with a box around the feet, platformer with the whole body box: the opaque area of idle's first frame.
  • The start is the free spot (in platformer, footing) nearest the map center. With no terrain floor, a platformer character falls and a notice appears.
  • With a tileset drawn by position, the character hides behind upright tiles and covers them in front. With floor order, it's drawn above all tiles.

Ladders and wall climbing

In platformer, ladders are ladder-floor cells and walls are terrain sides. Walls can be climbed only with ⑧ on.

ActionLadderWall
GrabW/↑ in front of it, or S/↓ on topIn the air, the arrow toward the wall (on the ground, with W/↑)
ClimbW/S or ↑/↓; moving off sideways lets go.W/S or ↑/↓
Release the keysHangs in place.Hangs in place.
Climb to the topStands on top (a platform you can land on only from above).Pulls up onto the top if there is room.
Let goSpace jumps off; reaching the ground also lets go.The opposite arrow drops off; Space kicks off the wall.

Animation names in the movement test

Check this when you want to know which animation plays while the character stands, runs or climbs. Name your animation tags with the set names, such as idle and run, and the movement test picks the right one.

  • ⑨ Playing: the current state, the animation playing, and the names tried (play icon = used, X = missing)
  • ⑩ Check animation names: for each name, a check means it exists, a triangle that another animation stands in, an X that it's missing

Names tried for each state

Use this table when naming tags. For each state the leftmost name is tried first, then the next.

StateNames tried (first to last)
Top-down idleidle (idle_down if there is none for the facing)
Top-down movingwalk → run → the idle names
Platformer idleidle
Runrun → walk → idle
Jump (rising)jump → fall → idle
Fallfall → jump → idle
Climbing a ladderclimb → idle
Hanging on a ladderhang → climb → idle
Climbing a wallwall_climb → climb → idle
Hanging on a wallwall_hang → wall_climb → hang → climb → idle

Names with a direction

Add a direction to a name (walk_left) to use it when facing that way. If you drew only one side, the other side is flipped from it.

  • Top-down: _down, _up, _left, _right
  • Platformer: _left, _right

Good to know

  • If no name matches, the first animation plays; with no animations, the first cell shows as a still picture.
  • Ladders add the names climb and hang; wall climbing adds wall_climb and wall_hang.
  • If a climbing animation stands in for a missing hang, it stays paused while the character hangs.
  • When flipped, the character turns around in place instead of jumping sideways.
  • These names also appear in the suggestions when you name a tag.

Tool options

Change the current tool's settings below the preview on the right. ① The title row shows the tool's name and ② its settings appear below.

Per-tool settings are in the Tools chapter. Settings aren't undoable and are kept for the next launch (except whether symmetry is on).

For the Pivot tool (K) settings (5×5 buttons, X/Y, From current frame on), see Pivot.

Collapsing

Click ① the title row to collapse the whole panel. Groups such as Brush and Options also collapse from their titles; the state lasts while the app runs.

Settings of the selected effect

Select ③ one effect in the timeline to show ④ its settings below Tool options. Changes apply at once; quick consecutive edits are one undo step.

  • Change the frame range, clipping, script or permanent application in the Effects dialog with Open in Effects dialog….
  • Expand canvas… opens a window that grows the canvas to fit the effect's result.
  • The group disappears when you select something else or several effects.
  • If a script can't render, the group shows why as a warning.

During transform and resize modes

During transform, canvas size and transform whole layer, the panel shows that mode's fields and Apply/Cancel buttons instead.

Status bar

The bottom line of the window shows the current state in cells, with ⑦ the latest message at the right end. The cells are for reading only.

CellWhat it shows
① Cursor coordinatesThe pixel under the cursor (x, y). Empty outside the canvas.
② ColorThe combined image's color under the cursor (HEX), or “Transparent”.
③ SizeThe canvas size (width × height).
④ Frame/layerFrame current/total · L number layer name, plus its size for a layer with its own size.
⑤ ZoomThe canvas zoom (%).
⑥ Save stateA dot and “Modified” with unsaved changes, otherwise “Saved”.
⑦ MessagesThe last action's result and hints (such as what's selected in the timeline and what Delete will do). It may end with “N unsaved files”.

Progress for long tasks appears in a box in the middle of the window (see Export).

With the external AI connection on, an External AI cell appears at the right end and briefly highlights each tool another AI app calls (Using other AI apps).

Timeline

Use the timeline at the bottom of the window to select and edit frames, layers and cels, and to mark animation ranges with tags.

PageWhat it covers
TimelineReading layer rows, frame columns and the tag strip, adding frames and layers, compact view
Right-click menusThe menu for each place you right-click, layer opacity
Frame durationSetting duration, FPS or speed in the frame duration dialog
Selecting in the timelineSelecting frames, layers and cels by clicking, dragging and keyboard
Linked celsMaking several cels share one image, and unlinking them
Editing the timelineDeleting, reordering, moving, copying and pasting, nesting layers
TagsNaming frame ranges and playing tags

Timeline

The timeline at the bottom of the window is where you see and pick your animation's frames and layers. Rows are layers, columns are frames, and each cell (a cel) is that layer's drawing in that frame.

The frame and layer you click become the current frame and layer you draw on in the canvas.

Layer rows

  • ① Eye: shows or hides the layer.
  • ② Lock: locks the layer so you can't draw on it.
  • ③ Opacity: a percentage follows the name when it isn't 100%.
  • ④ Triangle: collapses or expands the child layers.
  • ⑤ Child layers: indented and joined by orange lines, with their own size next to the name.
  • Alt+click an eye to show only that layer, or a lock to lock all the others. Alt+click again to undo it.
  • fx: the layer has effects. Click it to expand or collapse its effect rows.
  • Diamond badge: the layer's children use auto layout. Click it to open the auto layout settings.
  • Skin chip: with two or more skins, the current skin's name follows the layer name. Click it for a menu to switch or manage skins (see the skin settings window).
  • Small crown: the layer that represents the whole canvas's center point (canvas pivot; see Pivot).
  • Grass-block icon: a tilemap layer.
  • Below the sixth level, a depth number before the name replaces the lines.
You can't draw on a locked layer, but you can draw on a hidden one. A hidden layer shows semi-transparently on the canvas only while editing and doesn't appear in the preview or exports.

Frame columns and durations

⑥ Each frame header shows its number and duration (ms), and each cel shows ⑦ a thumbnail. Double-click a number to open the frame duration dialog.

  • Thumbnails show the drawing without effects, all frames cropped at the same scale so you can compare movement.
  • A collapsed parent row's thumbnail combines it with its visible children.
  • Orange diamond at a cel's top left: that frame has its own center point (pivot; see Pivot).
  • The diamond always shows for layers with their own size, otherwise only on the current layer's row. Diamonds on frame headers appear only while you edit the canvas pivot.

Tag strip, canvas size chip and playback marker

  • ⑧ The tag strip shows groups of frames as colored brackets (Tags).
  • The canvas size chip in the top-left corner: click its icon for the canvas size mode (Ctrl+Alt+C).
  • Click the chip's Canvas W×H text to edit the whole canvas's center point (canvas pivot) with the pivot tool. Click a layer name to edit layer pivots again.
  • While the preview plays, the frame column being shown gets a ⑬ green outline.
  • When effects take a while to compute, those thumbnails get a gray veil.

Adding frames and layers

  • ⑨ Plus after the last frame: adds an empty frame.
  • ⑩ Copy below it: adds a copy of the last frame.
  • ⑪ Name cell below the last layer: adds an empty layer at the bottom.
  • ⑫ Frame area next to it: copies the bottom layer with its children.
  • Drag ⑲ a button to the right to add as many as the cells you cover (Esc cancels).
  • New frames go outside tags, so tags don't grow; copied linked cels join their original group.

Compact view and scrolling

Switch off frame previews with ⑳ the image button at the right end of the title bar to make ㉑ the cels smaller, with only a white dot where there is art (on by default; remembered).

  • Drag the border between layer names and frames to set the name width (140–600 px); double-click it to reset to 270 px. The width is remembered.
  • The wheel scrolls layer rows; Shift+wheel or Ctrl+wheel scrolls frame columns. The horizontal scrollbar sits right below the title bar.
  • Dragging a selection or a move to an edge scrolls automatically. Timeline cells can't be zoomed.

Right-click menus

To find what you can do with a frame, cel or layer, right-click it. The top of the menu shows ⑭ what's selected (for example, "Frames 1–3 selected").

By where you right-click

Where you right-clickMain menu items
⑮ Frame headerCreate tag, Empty frame (Alt+N), Duplicate (Alt+D), Delete, Move forward/back, Duration…, Copy/Cut/Paste, Pivot, Export cell name…
⑯ CelClear, Copy, Cut and Paste cels, Copy image from previous frame, Copy as image, Link/Unlink cels, Add effect…, frame items
