⑰ Layer nameRename, Opacity…, Effects…, Show/Lock, Canvas pivot representative, New (layer, empty, tilemap, child), Duplicate, Move out of parent, Move up/down, Merge down, Delete
⑱ Empty area (such as the top-left cell)New layer (Shift+N), New empty layer (Ctrl+Shift+N), New tilemap layer (Alt+Shift+N), New child layer… (Shift+P), Empty frame at end, Copy last frame to end
TagTag properties…, Play this tag, Delete (Tags)
Effect rowEffect settings…, change the range, Turn on/off, Delete, Apply permanently, Expand canvas…, Copy/Paste (see effect rows)

Good to know

  • The layer menu gains items depending on the layer: expand/collapse effect rows, layer size and position, collapse children and auto layout, convert tilemap to layers, and skin items (see Layer tree and skins).
  • Merge down works only when both layers are normal layers with a single skin.
  • The pivot and cell name items at the end of the header and cel menus apply only to the right-clicked frame, not the whole selection.
  • The pivot items change the center point you're editing now (the current layer's or the canvas pivot).
  • Items that don't apply are grayed out.

Layer opacity

To make a layer semi-transparent, choose Opacity… in the layer menu. Use the slider or a number, in % (0–100) or 0–255. The 100%, 75%, 50% and 25% buttons set common values in one click.

Frame duration

The frame duration dialog sets how long the selected frames are shown. Double-click a frame number, or choose Duration… from the header or cel right-click menu.

Methods

The top shows how many frames are selected. Pick a method under Set by and enter one value (the info badge at the end of the row explains each).

MethodWhat it does
Duration (ms)Gives every selected frame that duration (1–60000; quick picks 50, 80, 100, 120, 150, 200).
FPS (frames per second)Same duration for all (duration = 1 second ÷ FPS).
Duration (seconds)Same duration for all, in seconds.
Game frames (60 fps)Same duration for all, as a number of frames in a 60 fps game.
Speed multiplierEach current duration ÷ the speed (2× is twice as fast). Keeps the differences between frames.
Total durationSets the total of the selected durations to that many ms, split in the current proportions.

Good to know

  • The Result group below shows each frame's new duration, fps and game frames and the total length in seconds, before → after.
  • Switching the method carries the current result over as the new method's value (80 ms becomes 12.5 FPS). The chosen method is remembered while the app runs.
  • Frames in a new project are 100 ms, and newly added frames take the duration of the frame before them.

Selecting in the timeline

In the timeline you select one kind of thing at a time (frames, layers, cels or effects). What you click becomes the current frame and layer, and the selected kind decides what Delete, copy and paste do.

How to select

Click a ① frame header to select that frame, a layer name for that layer, or a ② cel for that cell; the selection gets a blue border. Click an effect in an expanded effect row to select that effect.

ActionResult
DragSelects everything the rectangle touches.
Shift+clickExtends the selection to a range up to the clicked item.
Ctrl (macOS ⌘)+clickAdds or removes one item at a time, even when not adjacent. Pressing inside selected cels with Ctrl held and dragging copies them instead (see Editing the timeline).
Click an empty areaClears the selection.
Click the eye, lock, collapse triangle or fxOnly does the icon's own job; doesn't select. Alt+clicking an eye or lock does select that layer.

The ③ status bar shows what's selected and what Delete will do.

Keyboard

After clicking the timeline, ←→ go to the adjacent frame and ↑↓ to the adjacent layer (stopping at the first and last). Moving left/right with frames selected, or up/down with layers selected, keeps that kind; moving the other way switches to cel selection. Shift+arrow extends the selection.

F2 = rename the current layer, Delete = delete what is selected (also Backspace on macOS; if the canvas has a selection, only its pixels are cleared), Esc = cancel a drag. For frame navigation shortcuts see Timeline shortcuts.

How multiple selections look

④ The clicked anchor has a dark border; ⑤ the neighboring and ⑥ separately selected cels have pale blue backgrounds.

Touching selected cels share one faint border. Cels picked across gaps with Ctrl/⌘+click can't be copied or cut; the status bar explains why.

Linked cels

Use cel links when the same drawing repeats across frames and you want editing one cel to change them all. Select the cels and choose Link cels from the right-click menu.

Each cel in ① the link group has a chain; the same-color strip continues under adjacent linked cels.

Linking cels

Right-click ② chain-marked cels to use ③ Link cels or Unlink cels.

  • You can include cels that aren't next to each other; each layer gets its own group.
  • The group takes the drawing of the cel you clicked (dark border). If that cel is empty, it uses an already linked cel in the selection, or the first cel with a drawing.
  • Drawing, moving or resizing in any cel changes the whole group (one undo step).
  • Each group gets its own strip color (the next color not yet used in that row).
  • The AI can also link, unlink and recolor groups (link_cels, unlink_cels, set_link_color).

Unlinking and what breaks a link

  • Unlink cels gives each selected cel its own copy of the drawing so you can edit it separately.
  • Pressing Delete on a linked cel keeps the link and clears the whole group.
  • Pasting and Copy image from previous frame take the cel out of its group. To copy without links, use Ctrl+Shift+C (see Editing the timeline).
  • Unlinking a cel that showed an effect's end look through its link puts that look into its drawing (except moved positions).

Links on parent layers and skins

  • Links are separate for each layer row. Linking a parent's row doesn't link its children's cels, so select their cels too.
  • A new blank skin copies only the link frames and colors; a skin made with Duplicate current art copies the drawing and links. After that, each skin's links are separate.

Editing the timeline

See this page when you want to reorder, move or copy frames, layers or cels. Select them in the timeline first, then drag or use the shortcuts.

To do thisHow
DeleteSelect, then Delete. Cels only lose their drawing.
ReorderDrag an already selected frame header or layer name.
Move or copy a block of cels① Press inside the selected cels and drag to move them; hold Ctrl when you drop to copy.
Copy and pasteCtrl+C or Ctrl+X, then Ctrl+V. ② They're in the right-click menu too.
Bring in the previous frame's imageRight-click an ③ empty cel → ④ “Copy image from previous frame”
Change duration or nameDouble-click a frame number = frame duration; double-click a layer name or F2 = rename

Good to know

  • Every edit is a single undo step. Press Esc to cancel while dragging.
  • You can't delete every frame or layer, and layers are deleted with their children.
  • A dragged layer moves within the same parent. Every 20 px you drag left takes it one level out of its parent.
  • Holding Alt when you drop copies too. Starting the drag with Ctrl held copies from the start.
  • Cels can't move to rows of a different size or type.
  • When pasting, cels overwrite starting at the clicked cell, frames go after the selected frame, and layers go above the original.
  • Cels picked across gaps can't be copied.
  • Cels copied from a document with a different canvas size also paste into normal layers, aligned to the top left; parts past the canvas are kept as outside-canvas artwork. Layers with their own size need the same size.
  • New layers made by copying get their trailing number raised by 1 (item_1 → item_2, or _1 if there's no number). Duplicate in the layer menu does the same.

Linked copies and images-only copies

Copying in the timeline copies cel links too.

  • Pasted into the same row, cels join the original link group and take its color.
  • Pasted into another row or document, cels from one group form a new group (a single cel is pasted unlinked).
  • Dragging a block of cels to move or copy it works the same way.

To copy only the images without links, press Ctrl+Shift+C or use the images only, no links copy items in the cel and frame right-click menus. Outside the timeline (for example on the canvas) it works like a regular copy.

Copying cels as an image

Right-click ⑤ a cel to see ⑥ commands to copy the selected cels with effects, or the entire frame, as an image.

Copy cels as image (with effects) copies the selected cels as a canvas-sized image, as shown with effects, opacity and current skins (hidden layers excluded). Copy frame N as image copies the whole composited frame.

Nesting layers and Add skin

  • To put a layer inside another, drag its name, hold Shift and drop it on that layer's row. An indented blue gap shows where it will land, and a not-allowed cursor shows where you can't drop.
  • To take it out, drag left or right-click and choose Move out of parent. The drawing stays in place on the canvas; a canvas-sized layer you nest shrinks to fit its drawing.
  • The layer's right-click Add skin adds a blank skin or one copied from the current art; with children, With children… makes a same-named skin on all descendants too (see Layer tree and Skins).

Tags

A tag is a named frame range that groups an animation (for example idle or run). Tags set the preview playback range, the GIF range, and export cell names and file splits.

Creating a tag

① Select a range of frames and choose ② Create tag from the right-click menu. In the dialog, set the ③ name, ④ frame range, ⑤ playback direction and loop, and ⑥ color.

FieldMeaning (initial value)
③ NameThe tag name; it can't be empty or match another tag. Names such as idle_down, walk_left, run, jump and fall are suggested (initially the first unused one).
④ FramesStart and end frame (inclusive); the end can't come before the start (initially the selected range).
⑤ DirectionForward, Reverse or Ping-pong (bounces back from the end, playing each end frame once) (initially Forward).
⑤ LoopWhen off, the preview plays the tag once and stops (initially on).
⑥ ColorThe tag strip color. Click the swatch or type a HEX value (initially blue, orange, green, red, purple, teal, yellow and gray in turn).

Editing and playing tags

  • Hover near either inner end of a ⑦ tag box until the cursor becomes ↔, then drag to change the range.
  • Right-click a tag for the ⑧ menu with Tag properties… (the same dialog as when creating; double-clicking the tag also opens it), Play this tag and Delete.
  • Clicking a tag selects it (white outline), and the preview plays that tag in its direction (a tag with Loop off plays once and stops). Clicking the frame area outside tags (headers, cels, effect rows or empty space on the tag strip) deselects it, and the preview loops all frames again.

Tag names are used for By tag cell names and per-tag files in Export, and for the range in GIF export. Tags are saved in the .chickxel file.

Layer effects

Add effects such as outlines, shadows, flashes and shakes to a layer. Effects don't change the cel pixels, so you can turn them off, change or remove them at any time.

PageWhat it covers
Layer effectsThe basic workflow, stacking order, applying permanently
Effects dialogParts of the effects dialog, saving a preset, OK and Cancel
Frames and canvas in the effects dialogExtending and deleting frames, expanding the canvas
Effect list panelAdding effects by drag and drop, official effects
Look and color effectsOutline, Shadow, Text and other effects that look the same on every frame, with their settings
Animated effectsExplode, Shake, Hit flash and other effects that change over their range, with their settings
Effect colors and clippingSolid or gradient colors, clipping to the effect above
Effect range and common settingsEffect range, speed and repeat, moving the pivot along, keeping the end look, effect padding
Effect rows in the timelineAdding effects in the timeline, dragging ranges, moving and copying effects
Effect presetsSaving several effects with their values and inserting them in one go
Script effects · Script APIUsing and editing effects written in JavaScript, allowing them to run, the rules for writing scripts
Effect MakerMaking, previewing, checking and publishing new script effects
Asset Manager · Publish onlineGetting effects from the Store, exchanging files, publishing your own effects

Layer effects

Use layer effects when you want to add outlines, shadows, flashes or shakes without changing your drawing. To start, right-click a layer name in the timeline and choose Effects….

Effects only sit on top of the layer, so you can turn them off, change or delete them at any time, and they also apply to anything you draw later.

Basic workflow

  1. In the timeline, right-click a layer name and choose ① Effects…. To cover only some frames, select those cels and choose Add effect… from the right-click menu.
  2. On the Effects tab of the effects dialog, double-click an effect or drag it into the effect list in the middle.
  3. Change values while watching the before/after preview, and drag in the frame strip below it to choose the frames the effect covers.
  4. Click OK. The layer name gets ② fx, and each effect gets an effect row in the timeline.

To skip the dialog, drag an effect from the effect list panel onto a timeline cel (it goes in with default settings). Drag its range in the timeline effect row.

Order of effects

Effects apply from the top of the list down, each on top of the result so far, so changing the order changes the result (see the picture).

  • Change the order with Up and Down in the effects dialog, or by dragging effect names in the timeline.
  • Clear an effect's checkbox to stop drawing it without deleting it.
  • Afterimage is always drawn last, wherever it is in the list.
  • To apply an effect only to what the effect above drew, turn on clipping (for example, rainbow colors on the outline only).

Layers with children

For a layer with child layers, pick the target in the common settings. This layer only applies to that layer's own drawing; Combined with children applies around the shape combined with its children (frame sheets only).

Applying permanently

Use Apply effect permanently when you want to turn an effect into real pixels and edit it like a drawing. Use the Apply permanently button in the effects dialog or the effect box's right-click menu; enabled effects above it become part of the drawing too.

  • Switch a Combined with children effect to This layer only before applying it permanently. Effects on locked layers can't be applied permanently.
  • Duplicating a layer also copies its effects, and Merge down turns the upper layer's effects into part of the drawing.

Where effects show

  • The canvas shows effects when Show effects on canvas is on; preview, frame sheets and layer atlases always include them.
  • While a layer with its own size is selected, the canvas shows that layer's and its parents' effects.
  • Timeline thumbnails show no effects (except cels with only a text effect and no drawing).
  • Layer atlases include only effects set to This layer only, and each sprite grows by the size of outlines or shadows.
  • Effects are drawn pixel by pixel, with no blur.

Effects this version doesn't know

Effects added in a newer Chickxel aren't drawn in this version, but saving doesn't remove them. The effect list shows them as a dimmed Unknown effect · type ID row that you can only reorder or delete.

Effects dialog

Open this dialog when you want to add effects, change their settings and preview the result. Right-click a layer name and choose Effects….

Other ways to open it: select cels and choose Add effect… (adds to those frames only), double-click an effect box in the timeline, or click Open in Effects… in the tool options dock.

Parts of the dialog

PartWhat it does
⑥ Effects tabThe effects you can add. Double-click one to add it at the end of the list, or drag it into the effect list to place it.
Get more · Effect Maker · Check all effects for updatesOpen the store · open the Effect Maker · check received effects for new versions (Update all installs them)
⑦ Presets tabEffect presets (bundles of effects). Insert them the same way as on the Effects tab.
⑧ Add rangeThe frames new effects will cover: All frames or a start–end range.
⑨ Effect listThis layer's effects in the order they apply. Use the checkbox to turn one on or off, and the buttons below to delete, reorder or apply permanently.
⑩ SettingsThe selected effect's settings and the common settings. Hover an i mark for an explanation.
⑪ Before/after previewThe current frame before and after effects. Click it for a larger view (Space play, ←/→ frames, Esc close).
⑫ Frame stripClick a cell to view that frame, or drag to choose the frames the selected effect covers.
⑬ Expand canvas…Grows the canvas when effects get cut off at its edge (see Expanding the canvas).
⑭ Extend framesAdds frames to fit the effect (see Extend frames).

Good to know

  • The Effects tab lists official effects, then Text, then received effects, then your own. Click the question mark at a tile's top right for a description.
  • When an effect from the Store has a new version, a blue Update appears below the ⑨ list. It switches to the new version and keeps your setting values.
  • A script effect that can't draw gets a warning icon.

Saving the list as a preset

⑮ Save Current Effects as Preset at the top of the ⑦ Presets tab saves the whole effect list, with its values, as an effect preset.

OK and Cancel

Pressing Enter in a setting doesn't close the dialog. Changes apply only when you click OK, and everything you changed is a single undo step. Cancel or closing the window restores the original.

Frames and canvas in the effects dialog

See this page when you want to add or delete animation frames for an effect, or grow the canvas when an effect gets cut off. Everything is applied together when you click OK in the effects dialog.

Extend frames

For example, to put a 12-frame explosion on a 4-frame drawing, use Extend frames in the common settings to add frames to the effect's range.

  • Ways to fill the new cells: with original frames · same ratio · first frame · last frame · repeat
  • Dragging the range end past the last cell of the frame strip also adds frames.
  • Added cels share the drawing of their originals (linked cels). Unlink a cel to draw it separately.

Deleting frames in the effects dialog

Drag the dashed trash cell (㉑) at the end of the frame strip to the left: the frames from there to the end turn red, and letting go marks them for deletion. The first frame can't be deleted.

  • ㉒ The red number on the trash cell is how many frames you deleted. Click it without dragging to bring back the last deletion.
  • While frames are marked, the preview shows the result after deletion, and effects only in those frames appear off.
  • If you then add frames, the deleted frames can't be brought back.
  • Frames are really removed only when you click OK, and tags and effect ranges shrink with them. Cancel keeps everything.

Expanding the canvas

Parts of an effect that go past the canvas are cut off (they're dimmed in the preview). To grow the canvas:

  1. Click Expand canvas… at the top of the common settings.
  2. Choose how to expand (minimal / keep aspect / keep center) and check the new size.
  3. Click Expand; the new size appears next to the button (click X to cancel).
  4. Click OK in the effects dialog to grow the canvas. The drawing isn't scaled; only the space around it grows.

To grow just the canvas for one effect, use Expand canvas… in the effect row's right-click menu or the tool options dock. There, Expand grows it right away.

When effects take a while

On large images where effects take a while, cells being redrawn and the preview get a gray veil, and playback waits until they're ready. While you drag a value, everything is redrawn when you let go.

Effect list panel

The effect list panel lets you drag effects onto timeline cels without opening the effects dialog. Click the ① Effects button (sparkle icon) on the toolbar to open ② the panel in the leftmost column.

Adding by drag and drop

  • While you drag a row over the timeline, ③ the target cel turns blue and the canvas and preview show the result right away. Inside a selected block of cels, the effect covers that block's frames.
  • Drop to add it with default settings (one undo step). Moving off the cels restores the preview.
  • Double-click a row to add it to the current layer at the current cel. The status bar shows where it went; drag its range in the effect row.

Finding effects

  • Like the effects dialog, it has Effects and Presets tabs, with a preview and name on each row (animated effects play).
  • Type in the Search effects box above the tabs to filter both tabs by name, description, author and store name.
  • Right-click a row and choose Add to favorites to move it to the Favorites group at the top of the tab; it stays there on the next launch. Remove from favorites undoes it.
  • At the bottom, the blue Get more opens the website store and the green Effect Maker opens the Effect Maker.
  • With the file browser on too, the column splits into files (top) and effects (bottom). What you leave open is kept for the next launch.

Official effects

The effects you have from the start are the 23 official effects and Text. Official effects are script effects the app installs on first run, and they update automatically when the app contains a newer version. Bring back deleted official effects with Restore default effects in the Asset Manager.

What each effect does and its settings are listed under Look and color effects and Animated effects. In the effects dialog, an effect tile's question mark also shows its description.

Look and color effects

Use these effects when you want to add an outline or shadow, or change an image's colors. They look the same on every frame.

Check the table below when choosing an effect or looking up what a setting does. Every color setting can be a solid color or a gradient (see Effect colors).

EffectWhat it doesMain settings
OutlineDraws a line around the image or on its inner edge. Shade from neighbors uses a darker version of the touching color instead of black.Color, Thickness, 8 directions (incl. corners), Inside (edge pixels), Hide original (outline only), Color mode, Shade %
ShadowCasts a shadow of the image's shape behind it, offset to the side.Color (semi-transparent black by default), X/Y offset, Dither
Edge light · shadeLights the edge facing the light and darkens the opposite edge for depth.Light direction, Range, Light color, Shade color
Color overlayTints visible pixels with a color or gradient. At Amount 100% the image becomes a one-color silhouette.Color, Amount, Blend (Overlay / Multiply / Screen)
Inner glowMakes a glowing edge by blending a color inward from the edges.Color, Thickness, Strength, Weaker toward the inside, Blend (Screen by default)
Palette swapSwaps chosen colors for others (for example, red clothes → blue).Source → target (color pair list, up to 64 pairs), Tolerance 0–255
Color adjustmentTurns the image grayscale or sepia, or adjusts hue, brightness, contrast or saturation.Style, Amount, Hue, Brightness/contrast, Saturation
ReflectionFlips the image and reflects it below (water, floor), above or to the side (wall), fading out.Direction, Gap, Height %, Start and End opacity, Fade with dithering, Tint, Ripple
TextWrites text over the image. The Text tool (T) creates a new layer with this effect.Text (up to 1000 characters), Font, Bold/italic, Size, Color, Position, Box size (0 = auto), Scale, Letter and line spacing, Alignment, Smooth, Typewriter (one letter at a time)

Good to know

  • Dither: instead of semi-transparency, dots are spaced out so the area looks lighter. It gives a pixel-art look.
  • Text uses the bundled pixel fonts or fonts you added. Letters outside the image are cut off.
  • On an empty layer the Text effect works like a text layer; Merge down or applying it permanently turns it into pixels.

Outline and shadow settings compared

The default outline only checks above, below and to the sides, so corners look open. 8 directions fills the corners too, and Shade from neighbors is softer than black.

Without dither the shadow is semi-transparent; with dither it becomes a checkerboard of dots (25% and 75% are sparser or denser).

Animated effects

Use these effects for animated touches such as a hit, an explosion or a shake; they change from frame to frame. The more frames an effect covers, the slower it moves.

Pick an effect from the tables below, then tune its motion with the common Speed and Repeat settings (see common settings). Blink and Afterimage don't have these.

Flashing, appearing and changing colors

EffectWhat it doesMain settings
Hit flashThe image flashes one color, as if hit.Color, Strength, Count, Shape (Solid / Fade out)
BlinkRepeatedly shows and hides (for example to show invincibility).Interval, Visible and Hidden frames, Count, Hide style (transparent / translucent / dots), Opacity
Appear / disappearThe image gradually appears or disappears.Disappear (off = appear), Style (opacity / dots / wipe / pixel scatter), Wipe direction, Edge, Seed
Fade in · outThe whole image gradually fades out or in.Fade out (off = fade in)
Light sweepA band of light sweeps across the image (the glint on coins or buttons).Angle, Width, Strength, Color, Softness
Color cycleCycles through chosen colors so they flow like water, fire or neon.Colors (2–8), Steps, Reverse
HologramTints the image like a sci-fi hologram and adds scan lines, blinking and glitchy shifts.Color and strength, Scanlines, Bright band, Opacity, Dither, Blink, Glitch, RGB split, Edge glow, Seed

Moving

EffectWhat it doesMain settings
ShakeThe whole image shakes.Direction (Horizontal / Vertical / Both), Strength, Count, Decay (weaker toward the end), Regular, Seed
BobFloats gently up and down (or side to side) — floating items, idle motion.Height, Count, Direction
Squash & stretchSquashes and stretches like jelly, then returns to its shape.Horizontal and Vertical strength, Bounce, Anchor (feet fixed / center / pivot), Upward first
RotateRotates the image around an anchor, keeping pixel lines clean without adding new colors.Angle (360 = one turn, negative = counterclockwise), Pivot (feet fixed / center / pivot)
AfterimageLeaves faint copies of earlier frames behind for a sense of speed.Count, Spacing (frames), Opacity, Tint

Breaking apart and spreading

EffectWhat it doesMain settings
ExplodePixels shatter and fly outward as they disappear.Fly distance, Center, Random variation, Gravity, Fragment size, Vanish, Flash, Keep original, Seed
MeltPixels drip down, each column at its own speed.Distance, Irregularity, Drip (leaves trails), Vanish, Seed
Shockwave ringA pixel ring spreads out from the center.Center, Max radius, Thickness, Color, Fade out, Behind image

Good to know

  • Seed: a number that fixes a random look. Each click of the dice button gives another look, and the same seed always gives the same look.
  • Afterimage is drawn last, after all other effects.
  • When Shake or Bob moves the image, later effects' center point (pivot) can follow it (see Move pivot along).

Effect colors and clipping

See this page when you want an effect in gradient colors such as a rainbow, or want an effect to apply only to what the effect before it drew, such as color on the outline only.

Picking colors

  • Clicking a ③ color swatch opens a color popup with the same modes as the main color editor, the palette, recent colors and buttons to pick from the image or screen; the canvas updates as you pick (see Color pickers in other windows).
  • Each color setting can be a ④ solid color / gradient. Gradient editing, color space and presets work as in the gradient tool.

Gradient modes

Choose the shape the gradient paints in. Static stays still; Animated flows or turns over the effect range.

ModeHow it paints
Linear (static) · Linear (animated)Paints straight bands along the angle.
Radial (static) · Radial (animated)Paints rings spreading from the center.
Square (static) · Square (animated)Paints square bands spreading from the center (tilted by the angle).
Angular (static) · Angular (animated)Colors change going around the center (angle = starting point).
Whole sprite, one color (over time)The whole image is one color per frame and changes color over the effect range.
  • Depending on the shape, set the Direction, angle and Repeats/rotations (1–16).
  • Even an effect that doesn't move, such as Outline, changes color with an animated gradient.
  • Whole sprite, one color (over time) also has speed and ping-pong settings.

Clipping

Turn on ⑤ Apply to → Clip to effect above and the effect applies only to what the effect just before it drew. Above, the color overlay with clipping off covers the whole image (left); with it on, only the outline is colored (right).

A clipped effect can still spread beyond those pixels. A clipped shadow becomes a shadow of the outline alone, drawn over the picture (right).

  • Several clipped effects in a row build on each other (for example, Outline → Shadow → Color overlay colors the outline and its shadow together).
  • If the effect before is off or not on that frame, the clipped effect isn't shown either.
  • You can't clip to Afterimage, and clipping needs an effect before it.
  • Clipped to an outline with Hide original (outline only) on, an effect applies to the line only.

Effect range and common settings

See this page when you want to set which frames an effect covers, how fast it moves and what remains after it ends. These are the common rows at the bottom of the effect settings.

RowShown forWhat it sets
Apply toAll effectsWhether to apply only to what the effect before drew (clipping)
Speed · RepeatAnimated effects (except Blink and Afterimage)How the motion speeds up and slows down and repeats
Later effects: Move pivot alongEffects that move the image (Shake, Bob and so on)Whether later effects' center point (pivot) moves too
Cels after the range: Keep the end lookAnimated effects (when the range isn't All frames)Whether the final look stays after the range ends

The effect group in the tool options dock shows some of these rows too (range and clipping only in the effects dialog).

Effect range

The effect range is the frames an effect covers: All frames (including frames added later) or a start–end range.

  • Pick the range for new effects with Add range in the effects dialog.
  • Change an added effect's range by dragging in the dialog's frame strip or its timeline effect box.
  • Inserting or deleting frames grows or shrinks the range like a tag, and deleting all its frames deletes the effect.

Speed and repeat

  • Speed: Fast start / Linear / Slow start / Slow start and end. Fast start moves a lot at first and almost stops at the end (see the picture).
  • Repeat: Once / Loop (start over) / Ping-pong (go and come back), 1–8 times.
  • Defaults differ by effect (for example, Explode and Shockwave ring use Fast start).

Later effects, Cels after the range and Expand canvas

For an effect that moves the image, choose ① whether later effects' center point (pivot) moves with it; for an animated effect, choose ② whether its final look stays after it ends (see below).

③ If an effect gets cut off at the canvas edge, use this button to grow the canvas (see Expanding the canvas).

Move pivot along

With Rotate after Shake, ⑤ Move pivot along (on by default) makes the rotation center follow the shaking image. Turn it off to shake only the image and keep the rotation center in place.

  • The row appears only for effects that report movement: the official Shake and Bob (1.0.2 or later) and script effects with ctx.movePivot in the source that are allowed to run.
  • The moved center is used by later effects that work around a center point, such as Rotate and Squash & stretch.
  • With a layer that has its own size selected, the pivot marker follows the moved position, and the original spot is shown as a small circle and dotted line.

Keep the end look

Turn on Cels after the range: Keep the end look when you want an animated effect's final look to stay after it ends. For example, a throw over frames 1–18 stays landed on frame 19. The timeline shows ⑥ a faint tail.

  • Cels after the range that are linked to its last cel look exactly like it.
  • Unlinked cels keep only the moved position, and their tail is fainter.
  • With Move pivot along on, later effects' centers and the pivot marker follow the final position too.
  • It's unavailable when the range is All frames.
  • Unlinking such a cel puts the held look into its drawing.

Effect padding

Effects that draw outside the image, such as Outline, Shadow and Explode, reserve extra space (padding) around it. When exported to a layer atlas, each sprite grows by this padding. Effects that move the image widen it automatically by the distance moved.

Effect rows in the timeline

Each effect gets a thin effect row under its layer. Without opening the effects dialog you can add effects, change their ranges, move them to other layers and copy them.

⑯ Each effect row has the on/off checkbox and effect name on the left and a ⑰ rounded effect box spanning the frames it applies to. Click fx in the layer name cell to collapse or expand that layer's effect rows.

Adding an effect to selected cels

Select cels and choose ⑱ Add effect… from the right-click menu to open the effects dialog with Add range set to those frames.

You can also drag a row from the effect list panel onto a timeline cel to add it with default settings.

Changing by dragging

  • Drag either end of a box to change its range, or drag the body to move the whole range. Double-click a box to open the effects dialog.
  • Drag an effect name up or down to reorder it, or drop it on another layer's row to move it to that layer. Select and press Delete to delete, Ctrl+C/V to copy.
  • Effect ranges follow frame insertions and deletions like tags (see Effect range and common settings).

Right-click menus

Right-click ⑲ an effect box for the menu below. Right-clicking an empty cell outside the box adds ⑳ the extend and add-copies items.

Menu itemWhat it does
Effect settings…Opens the effects dialog with that effect selected (same as double-clicking the box).
To all frames · This frame only · From this frame to end · From start to this frameChanges the effect range relative to the right-clicked frame.
On / OffTurns the effect on or off without deleting it.
DeleteDeletes the effect (Delete).
Apply effect permanentlyBakes the effect into the cel pixels; enabled effects above it are baked too ("including N effects above").
Expand canvas…Opens a small window that grows the canvas to fit that effect's result (see Expanding the canvas).
Copy effect · Paste effectCopies the effect and pastes it onto the current layer (Ctrl+C/V).
⑳ Extend to hereEmpty cells outside the box only: extends the effect range to the right-clicked frame.
⑳ Add copies from hereEmpty cells outside the box only: adds a copy of the effect from the right-clicked frame to the end.

Extending past the end

Drag the right end of an effect box past the last frame and ㉑ chain-marked cells appear. Drop it to add that many frames linked to the last frame; the effect's range and tags or other effects reaching the last frame grow too (one undo step).

Effect presets

Use effect presets when you want to save a combination you use often, such as outline + shadow, and insert it into other layers or projects in one go. Save and insert them on the Presets tab of the effects dialog.

Presets are saved on this computer and can be shared as asset files. To publish an effect online, make it a script effect in the Effect Maker.

Basic workflow

  1. Build the effect list you want in the effects dialog.
  2. Click Save Current Effects as Preset at the top of the Presets tab, choose a name and preview chick, and save.
  3. In the effects dialog of another layer or project, double-click the preset on the Presets tab or drag it into the effect list.
  4. To change the whole bundle with a few values, set up parameters (see Setting parameters below).

Presets tab

  • Build an effect list in the effects dialog and click ① Save Current Effects as Preset to open the save window; saving makes it ② one of your presets.
  • ③ Received presets, downloaded with the Asset Manager or imported from asset files, show on the same tab.
  • Inserted presets are copies, so they stay in the project even if you change or delete the original preset.
  • Right-click one of your presets for Rename, Edit parameters, Delete and Show in Asset Manager. To change a received preset, make your own copy with Copy to My Assets in the Asset Manager.

Saving a preset

Enter ④ a name (it starts as the first effect's name). If a preset with that name exists, you're asked whether to overwrite it.

  • Under ⑤ Preview chick, pick one of 20 chicks (chick 1 by default). Exporting as an asset file makes the preview with this chick.
  • None draws only the effects, without a chick (for effects that draw on their own, such as rain or snow).

Setting parameters

A parameter lets one value change settings of several effects together. For example, tie the outline color and shadow color to one "Color" parameter, and after inserting you change both with one value.

  1. Right-click one of your presets on the Presets tab and choose Edit parameters (your preset's details in the Asset Manager have the same button).
  2. Click Add to create a parameter in the ⑥ list and give it a ⑦ Name.
  3. Under ⑧ Linked settings, check the settings to change together. The first one you check sets the parameter's kind and default; settings of another kind can't be picked.
  4. Depending on the kind, set ⑨ the Default, the Range for integers or the Choices for choices, then click OK.

Invalid links or ranges are listed as problems at the bottom of the window, and OK stays off until they are fixed.

Using a parameter preset

  • ⑩ A preset with parameters appears in the effect list as one row, "preset name · preset, N effects".
  • ⑪ Selecting that row shows only the parameters in the settings; changing a value updates every linked setting together.
  • ⑫ Show effects lists the effects inside under the group row so you can edit them one by one, and Hide effects folds them again. Ungroup turns them into ordinary effects with their current values (the parameters go away).
  • A collapsed group moves, deletes and applies permanently as one unit.

Text parameters

Link the text setting of a Text or script effect at ⑬ to create a text parameter. Write the default text in the multiline field ⑭, and change it in the effects dialog after inserting. The shortest length limit among linked settings applies.

Making script effects

Official effects such as Outline and Shadow are script effects written in JavaScript too. This chapter covers using script effects, the API for writing a source, and the Effect Maker for making and publishing new effects.

Script effects

Use script effects to add looks such as outlines, glows or shakes to a layer. They are layer effects written in JavaScript, added and edited in the effects dialog.

Official, Store and your own effects are all script effects. An effect's code (its source) is saved in the project file, so it draws the same on any computer.

In the effects dialog

  • ① After the official effects and Text, the Effects tab has tiles for received script effects and your own. Double-click a tile or drag it into the effect list. New scripts are made in the Effect Maker.
  • ② With a script effect selected, the top of the settings shows its name, author and a status line (time per frame, or an error). The first 12 characters of the source's fingerprint (hash) show too.
  • ③ Edit script… opens the script editor. Open JS file… replaces this effect's code with a JS file (settings with the same name keep their values).
  • ④ Save as asset: saves this effect as one of your script effects, to reuse from the Effects tab and the Asset Manager and to edit in the Effect Maker. Only scripts allowed to run can be saved, and the same effect is saved once.
  • ⑤ The settings come from the declared settings chosen by the effect's author (see Script settings). Animated effects also get Speed and Repeat.

Script effect assets

  • Received and own script effect tiles have a code icon at the top right. The tooltip shows the description and the Store name (or the author), and says so when the script isn't allowed to run.
  • Right-click your own script tile for Edit in Effect Maker, Delete and Show in Asset Manager; received effects only have Show in Asset Manager.
  • Presets and asset files with script effects include their sources.
  • In the Asset Manager they have the kind Script effect under the Effects filter. Details list the author, hash, Script run and setting names.
  • To edit a received effect, make your own copy with Copy to My Assets.

Allowing scripts to run

Opening a project with scripts you haven't seen before shows the Run script effects? window. ⑰ Each script has a checkbox, name, author and hash; ⑱ Run runs only the checked ones and Don't run runs none.

  • Your choice is remembered on this computer for each effect. Effects that don't run aren't drawn but stay in the project; allow them later with Allow to run in the settings.
  • These run without asking: sources written or imported on this computer, your own script effects, effects installed with the Asset Manager, sources written by the AI and official effects (older versions too).
  • Sources set to Don't run stay blocked until you allow or reinstall them.

When a script can't draw

A script effect that can't draw gets a warning icon at the end of its effect list row and on its timeline effect row. Hover the icon in the effect list for the reason.

StatusCause and fix
Missing sourceThe project lacks the source. Open the same JS file with Open JS file… in the settings.
Not allowedThe script isn't allowed to run. Click Allow to run in the settings.
Source errorThe code has syntax errors. Fix them in the editor.
FailedA frame went over the limits or an error occurred while running. Only this effect is skipped on that frame.
StoppedIt failed 3 times in a row and stopped. Fix it, then click Run again in the settings or turn the effect off and on.

Good to know

  • Scripts run in an isolated engine with no file or network access, within time and memory limits per frame.

Script settings

See this page when you want to change a script effect's colors, angles, positions and other values in the effects dialog. The kind of field depends on the settings the effect's author chose.

  • ⑥ Color: pick a solid color or a gradient (a band of blending colors). Some color fields allow only opaque colors.
  • ⑦ Color lists and color pair lists: each item has move up/down and remove buttons; add items with Add color or Add pair. At the count limits that button is off. In a pair, the top row is the color to replace, the bottom row its replacement.
  • ⑧ Angle dial: click or drag to set the angle (0° = right, 90° = up; hold Shift for 15° steps).
  • ⑨ Point picker: drag on the pad to set X and Y together (top left is the smallest value).
  • ⑩ Layer picker: choose the layer the effect reads. It lists (none), then the document's layer names in timeline order (without the effect's own layer and its parents). A saved name not in the document shows as "name (missing)".
  • ⑪ Seed: each click on the dice button gives a different random look. Keep the number to keep a look you like.

Other numbers get a slider and a number field, on/off settings a checkbox, and choices a list.

Text input setting

⑲ Type the text the effect draws in the text field. Newlines are allowed; the length limit (1–1000 characters) depends on the effect.

Font and position settings, and setting groups

  • ⑳ Font: the same font list as the Text effect, with Add font and Open font folder buttons. A font missing on this computer shows as "name (missing)" with a warning.
  • ㉑ Position: pick an anchor (Custom, Canvas center, Pivot, Image center). The X/Y fields and point picker show only for Custom; X and Y are kept when you switch.
  • ㉒ A titled box groups related settings. A box whose settings are all hidden disappears.

Effect authors: how to declare these fields is in Script API: setting types.

Script editor

Use the script editor to change the code of a script effect already on a layer. Open it with Edit script… in the effects dialog settings.

  • ⑫ The hint at the top shows the basic shape of effect code. To make a new effect, use the Effect Maker.
  • ⑬ The code editor is the same as in the Effect Maker: syntax colors, line numbers, and hovering a name shows how to use it and a one-line description.
  • ⑭ Error list: syntax errors show as "Line n, column m: …"; click one to jump there. The error spot in the code gets a red wavy underline. The line below the list is the run status (time per frame, or a run error).
  • ⑮ Apply (Ctrl+Enter) shows the result in the effects dialog and on the canvas right away. Old code no longer used is dropped from the project when you save.

Autocompletion

⑯ Typing a dot, letters or a quote, or pressing Ctrl+Space, opens the suggestion list. The selected suggestion shows its shape and a one-line description; insert it with Enter or Tab.

  • Typing a dot lists what can follow (img., ctx., ctx.source., ctx.pivot., Math.). After s. you get this effect's setting names (r, g, b, a after s.key. for colors).
  • Inside quotes (type: ", area: ", previewChick: ", ctx.color(") you get the allowed values. Suggestions also appear after variables holding a result, such as const c = ctx.color("key"), and at key positions in settings.
  • Elsewhere you get globals, names in this code and keywords. Inside a function call a parameter hint shows, with the current argument in bold. Typing a dot or bracket inserts the selected suggestion first.

Script API

Reference for writing script effect code yourself or fixing code the AI wrote. This page covers the basic shape of the code and the items you put in effect({ … }) (declaration keys).

Shape of a source

  • One code file is one effect. Call effect({ … }) exactly once at the top; besides it, keep only const values and functions (no import or export).
  • render(img, ctx, s) is called for every frame. It changes the pixel values in img.data directly and returns nothing.
  • The same image, frame and settings must always give the same result. Date, Math.random, eval, Function, files, network, timers and console aren't available; use ctx.hash and ctx.random for randomness.

This is the skeleton New script starts from (comments removed). It has one Strength setting and moves the red of each non-transparent pixel toward 255 by that strength.

effect({
  name: "New effect",
  language: "en",
  settings: [
    { key: "strength", label: "Strength", type: "int", min: 0, max: 100, default: 50 },
  ],
  render(img, ctx, s) {
    const d = img.data, k = s.strength / 100;
    for (let i = 0; i < d.length; i += 4) {
      if (d[i + 3] === 0) continue;
      d[i] = lerp(d[i], 255, k);
    }
  },
});

Declaration keys

Use this table to set the effect's name, description and how it animates. All of these go inside effect({ … }).

NameMeaning
nameEffect name (1–100 characters, required)
descriptionShort description (up to 200 characters); the initial short description when publishing
authorAuthor name (shown in the run confirmation window)
languageLanguage of the name, description and setting labels ("ko", "en", "zh-Hans" …)
translationsOther languages as { name, description, labels }; labels translate setting labels, choice labels and group titles
tagsUp to 8 tags of lowercase English letters, digits and hyphens; used as tags when publishing
settingsList of settings (see setting types)
gradientsInitial gradients of color settings (optional). See Writing gradients below
render(img, ctx, s)Called for every frame; changes img.data in place
animated · motion · easinganimated: true = changes over time (adds speed and repeat settings) · motion: false = hides speed and repeat · easing = initial easing ("Linear", "EaseIn", "EaseOut", "EaseInOut")
repeat · repeatCount · movePivotInitial values for animated effects: repeat mode Once, Loop or PingPong (default Once) · repeat count 1–8 (default 1) · whether later effects' centers move along (default true)
paddingMargin for drawing outside the image: [left, top, right, bottom] or padding(s, width, height) { … } (see Drawing outside the image below)
area · radius"global" (default) = once per frame with the whole image · "local" = per tile, with radius px around it
deferredtrue = draws after all other effects regardless of its place in the list (like Afterimage). Only then ctx.renderedFrameSource is available

Writing gradients

  • Write one per color slot: a color setting "key", a list item "key[0]", or a pair side "key[0].from" or "key[0].to".
  • The value is { stops: [{ position, color }, …] }. position is 0–1 and color "#RRGGBB"; only stops is required.
  • Optional keys: alphaStops ({ position, alpha: 0–255 }), interpolation (Smooth, Steps), colorSpace (OkLch, OkLab, Rgb, Hsl), dither, shape (Linear, Radial, Square, Angular, Uniform), angle, reverse, cycles, animated, easing, pingPong. Left out, they take initial values.
  • When the effect is added, that color setting starts with this gradient; the user can change it or switch back to a solid color. A wrong slot or value is a declaration error.
  • The Effect Maker's Set as default writes the chosen gradients here for you.

Preview keys

Use this table to choose the chick and motion for previews in the Effect Maker. These apply only to the preview, Check and the published preview, never to documents.

NameMeaning
previewChick · previewEndChickPreview chick name (default "original") and end chick name. "none" = an empty canvas without a chick (for effects that draw on their own, such as rain or snow)
previewFramesNumber of frames in the preview, the check and the published preview (2–20, default 10)
previewMotionChick movement: "none" (default), "right", "left", "up", "down" (1 px per frame) or one [x, y] per frame (−32 to 32; later frames keep the last one)
previewPivot · previewEndOffset · previewEndPivotOffsets of each frame's pivot · the end chick's position · the last frame's pivot. Each is [x, y] px (−64 to 64, default 0; see preview position and pivot)
  • Chick names 1–10: original, painter, pencil, palette, easel, paint-bucket, designer, heart-canvas, cursor, hatch
  • 11–20: knight, wizard, archer, ninja, pirate, astronaut, viking, king, gamer, healer

Drawing outside the image (padding)

  • img is the source image plus the declared margin (padding). Anything drawn outside it is cut off.
  • Effects that draw outside the source, such as glows, shadows, outlines, shakes, smoke, particles and ripples, must declare padding.
  • When the reach depends on the settings, compute it with padding(s, width, height) { return [left, top, right, bottom]; }. Otherwise give a fixed value of just the distance it reaches.
  • With padding the source starts at (ctx.source.x, ctx.source.y) inside img. Measure source-relative positions from ctx.source, ctx.center or ctx.pivot.

Script API: setting types

Reference for choosing the fields (numbers, colors, layers…) shown in the effects dialog. Each item in settings is one field; read its value in render as s.key.

Keys of an item: key (id) · label (shown name) · type · min, max, step (range) · default (initial value) · choices · alpha (allow transparency) · widget (field style) · pair (paired setting) · group · when (show condition).

Fields and values by type

The field each type creates in the effects dialog, and the value render reads.

typeField in the effects dialogs.key value
int · numberSlider and number field. widget: "angle" = angle dial, widget: "point" + pair = point picker; key seed adds a dice buttonNumber
boolCheckboxtrue / false
colorColor field with solid/gradient (opaque only with alpha: false). default like "#ff8800"{ r, g, b, a } — gradients via ctx.color
choiceDrop-down list (choices: [{ value, label }])The chosen value
colorListColor list (min, max = item count; default 0–16, up to 64)[{ r, g, b, a }, …] — item colors via ctx.colorAt
colorPairsFrom → to color pair list (default [{ from, to }, …])[{ from, to }, …] — ctx.pairColor
layerLayer picker. default = a layer name (up to 64 characters). min, max, step, choices, alpha and widget aren't allowedThe chosen name ("" when none). Read the layer with ctx.layer(key)
textMultiline text field. max is the length limit (1–1000; default limit when omitted)The typed text
fontFont picker. Only key, label, default and group (default is "Galmuri11" when omitted)The font name; draw with ctx.text(text, { font: s.key })
positionAnchor plus X/Y. min and max are the X/Y range; default is { anchor, x, y } (canvas center when omitted){ anchor, x, y }. Get image coordinates with ctx.position("key")

Groups and conditions

Use these to box settings together or hide them depending on other settings.

KeyWhat it doesExample
groupGroup title (1–100 characters). Consecutive settings with the same title share one boxgroup: "Position" — translate it with translations.lang.labels["group:Position"]
whenShows only when earlier choice/bool settings match; all conditions must matchwhen: { facing: "rotate" } · when: { facing: ["rotate", "flip"] } · when: { closePath: true }

Good to know

  • Hidden settings keep their values and can be read as s.key in render. When a condition's setting is hidden, its dependents are hidden too.
  • A when that points to a later or missing setting, a setting that isn't choice/bool, or a choice value that doesn't exist is a declaration error.
  • With more than four settings, group similar ones. Keep color settings in their own "Color" group, since they grow when switched to a gradient.
  • For effects where placement matters, use one position setting instead of a choice plus point settings. Position and font settings aren't exported as recipe parameters.
  • Effects that read a font value run on the slower engine (Jint).

Script API: img, ctx and globals

Reference for when render needs the image size, progress, colors, other layers and so on. img is the image to draw on; ctx holds run info and helpers.

Image (img)

Use these to read and write pixels.

NameMeaning
img.width · img.height · img.dataWidth and height of the padded image and its pixel array: four numbers R, G, B, A per pixel; the red of (x, y) is at (y * img.width + x) * 4. Written values are rounded to 0–255
img.blank(w, h) · img.blendOver(src, x, y)Makes an empty (transparent) image of the same kind · draws src over the image at (x, y), respecting transparency

Frame and position

Use these to change things over time or measure from a reference point.

NameMeaning
ctx.progress · ctx.phase · ctx.tHow far the effect range has progressed (0–1). progress includes loop and easing (usually the one to use), phase only the loop, t neither
ctx.frame · ctx.step · ctx.spanFrame being drawn (from 0) · frames since the first frame of the range · range length − 1
ctx.rangeStart · ctx.rangeEnd · ctx.frameTagFirst and last frame of the effect range (0 and the last frame in previews) · name of the tag containing this frame ("" when none or in previews)
ctx.source · ctx.pivot · ctx.centerWhere the source sits { x, y, w, h } · pivot (the effect's center point) { x, y } · source center { x, y } (always the middle, unlike the pivot)
ctx.movePivot(dx, dy)Reports how far the image moved (px, fractions allowed). Calls add up; returns nothing
ctx.position(key)Image coordinates { x, y } of a position setting. Custom = the source's top left + (X, Y), Canvas center = ctx.center, Pivot = ctx.pivot, Image center = middle of the picture before the effect
ctx.slice9-slice border (the edge margins that stay fixed when stretched) { left, top, right, bottom } of the project or that layer. null when there is none or it doesn't fit

Colors and randomness

Use these to apply the user's colors or make random patterns.

NameMeaning
ctx.color(key, x, y, bounds?) · ctx.colorAt(key, i, x, y, bounds?) · ctx.pairColor(key, i, "from"|"to", x, y, bounds?)Color { r, g, b, a } of a color setting at (x, y). The solid color, or for gradients picked across the opaque area of the image (or the given bounds { x, y, w, h })
ctx.hasGradient(…)Whether a color slot has a gradient: "key", "key[0]", "key[0].from", or (listKey, index, "from"|"to")
ctx.gradientAt("slot", t, x?, y?)Color at t (0–1) of a gradient, for picking a color by a value (a particle's age, progress) instead of position. Ignores the gradient's shape and angle. x, y place the dot pattern (dither; default 0)
ctx.hash(x, y, seed, salt?) · ctx.random(seed)A fixed random number from 0 up to 1. hash depends on position, seed and a purpose number (salt); random on frame and seed. Always the same
ctx.bayer2(x, y) · ctx.bayer4(x, y)2×2 (0–3) and 4×4 (0–15) dot-pattern thresholds (for mixing colors by dithering)

Other frames and layers

Use these when you need pictures from other frames or layers.

NameMeaning
ctx.frameSource(i)The source of frame i before effects (same size). null when missing or a different size
ctx.renderedFrameSource(i)Deferred effects only: frame i with this layer's other effects applied, { image, offsetX, offsetY }
ctx.matchPixels(a, b, mode?)Pairs the opaque pixels of two images (for transform effects). { count, data }, [ax, ay, bx, by] per pair. Mode "color" pairs by brightness (default: by position). null when a or b is null
ctx.layer(key) · ctx.layerWithEffects(key) · ctx.layerEffects(key)The layer chosen in a layer setting on this frame (without / with its effects, aligned to img) and its enabled effect names. null or an empty array when there is none

Text and global helpers

Use these to draw text or do number and color math.

NameMeaning
ctx.text(text, options?)Draws text into a transparent image with the Text effect's fonts and layout. options: font, size, color, bold, italic, smooth, align, letterSpacing, lineSpacing, width
lerp · hsvToRgb · rgbToHsv · rgbToOklab · oklabToRgb · Mathlerp(a, b, t) = the value a fraction t between a and b · HSV and OKLab color conversion · Math (no Math.random). Typed arrays: Float64Array, Int32Array, Uint8Array

One floating damage number

This draws the text from a text setting with ctx.text, places it with img.blendOver and moves it up as the effect progresses.

effect({
  name: "Damage number",
  animated: true,
  settings: [
    { key: "message", label: "Shown text", type: "text", default: "42", max: 32 },
  ],
  render(img, ctx, s) {
    const label = ctx.text(s.message, { size: 12, color: "#ff6666", bold: true });
    const x = Math.floor((img.width - label.width) / 2);
    const y = Math.floor(img.height / 2 - ctx.progress * 12);
    img.blendOver(label, x, y);
  },
});

Script writing tips

See this page when an effect is slow, looks cut off, or needs to read other layers or frames. It collects how to run fast and common patterns.

Fast path and limits

  • When render uses only numbers, booleans, loops, if, Math, lerp, helper functions taking numbers and img.data, it runs quickly on the fast path.
  • Strings, objects, arrays, closures, recursion and the like run on the general engine (Jint) with the same result, only slower.
  • These run on the fast path too: ctx.center, ctx.frameSource, ctx.matchPixels, ctx.position, ctx.movePivot, color functions (ctx.color and the like) with the key written literally, ctx.layer and ctx.layerWithEffects.
  • These run on Jint: ctx.slice, ctx.layerEffects, and reading layer or font setting values. The Effect Maker's status button shows which path is used and why.
  • Limits per frame: 2 seconds, 50 million statements, 256 MB of memory and call depth 256. Bigger images get 100 µs, 500 statements and 6 KB more per pixel.
  • Over a limit the frame is skipped; after 3 in a row the effect stops.
  • Keep the work proportional to the pixel count (no big loop per pixel).

Effects that move the image

  • Effects that move their image report the distance moved this frame (px) with ctx.movePivot(dx, dy). Later effects' centers, the layer pivot marker, the margin and held positions after the range then follow.
  • Effects that don't report it, such as Follow path or Gravity throw, need this call where the movement is computed (in Edit script) for others to follow.

Reading other layers

  • Add a layer setting so the user picks the layer, then read it in render with ctx.layer("key") (without effects), ctx.layerWithEffects("key") (with effects) or ctx.layerEffects("key") (enabled effect names). A layer can't be read by position or by a name in the code.
  • You get the nearest layer with the chosen name (excluding this layer and its parents; hidden layers count), aligned to img. Layer opacity isn't applied.
  • It is null when the layer is missing, so always check.
  • ctx.layerWithEffects skips that layer's effects that read other layers with effects, so layers reading each other never loop.
  • Changing the pixels, effects, name or order of the layer being read, or the 9-slice border, redraws right away. In the Effect Maker you preview it on a preview scene; publishing adds the layer-reference tag.

Effects that use several frames (transform)

  • ctx.frameSource(i) reads another frame before effects. Effects using it also draw on frames without a cel, so put pictures only on the first and last frames and apply the effect over that range to fill the frames between.
  • Preview and check it with the Effect Maker's end chick; publishing adds the frame-reference tag. ctx.frameTag lets an effect draw differently per frame tag.

How a transform effect works:

  1. Read the first and last pictures with a = ctx.frameSource(ctx.rangeStart) and b = ctx.frameSource(ctx.rangeEnd).
  2. Pair the pixels of the two pictures with m = ctx.matchPixels(a, b).
  3. Clear img.data, then draw each pair moved from (ax, ay) toward (bx, by), blending position and color by ctx.progress.

Randomness

  • Random looks come from a seed. Add a { key: "seed", type: "int", … } setting to get a dice button in the effects dialog.
  • ctx.hash(x, y, s.seed, salt) and ctx.random(s.seed) always give the same value for the same seed.
  • Give each random value its own salt (a purpose number, e.g. 0 = direction, 1 = speed).
  • Feed values that don't change per frame, such as a piece number, so each piece follows the same path in every frame.

Following gradient colors

  • Users can switch color settings to gradients. Pick the color for each pixel you write with ctx.color("key", x, y) (lists ctx.colorAt, pairs ctx.pairColor) so gradients are followed.
  • Use s.key only when one color is really needed.
  • Pass bounds { x, y, w, h } as the last argument when the default area doesn't fit the shape. To pick a color by a value such as a particle's age or progress, use ctx.gradientAt("key", t, x, y).
  • The declaration's gradients can make a color setting start as a gradient. The Effect Maker's Check points out color settings that draw in one color only.

Effect Maker

Use the Effect Maker when you want to make a new script effect or change one of yours. Open it with AI > Effect Maker…, preview it on sample chicks, then publish it to the Store if you like.

You can also open it with the Effect Maker button at the bottom of the effect list or Edit in Effect Maker on your script effect. Opened without an effect, it starts on a new script with the AI panel open.

The title, description, tags and preview chick are written into the effect's code too, so one code file is one effect. Effect presets (bundles of effects) aren't made here.

Basic workflow

  1. Start from the skeleton with New script, or pick one of your effects in the list.
  2. Edit the source yourself, ask in the AI panel, or copy the Rules window to another AI and paste its answer.
  3. Check the look in the preview. Edits are saved automatically, so the effect is ready on the Effects tab of the effects dialog.
  4. To share it, run Check on the 20 sample chicks and click Upload….

Window layout

  • ① My effects: effects made on this computer or published with your account. Turn on Show all at the top right to also see received effects and unchanged copies of them (see My effects list).
  • ② New script starts from a short skeleton; Open JS file… opens a .js file as a new effect.
  • ③ Info: title, description, tags and Languages (see Info and languages).
  • ④ Rules (right of the Info header, book icon): shows the rules for writing effects (see Rules window).
  • ⑤ The code editor is the same as the script editor of the effects dialog (see Script editor). Ctrl+Z undoes.
  • ⑥ Preview setting values: pick the setting values used for the preview (see below).
  • ⑦ AI (right of the Preview header, sparkles icon) opens and closes the AI panel on the right.
  • ⑧ The preview is the same image that is made when you publish (0.1 s per frame). Click a frame in the strip to pause on it.
  • ⑨ Preview chick: pick one of 20 chicks, or None in the first slot (an empty canvas, for effects that draw on their own such as rain or snow). End chick (transform effects) is covered separately.
  • ⑩ Play/pause icon button, Show without effect, Frames (2–20), and the motion (gear) and position (crosshair) buttons (see Moving the preview chick).
  • ⑪ The red Undo steps back through source edits one at a time; Insert into layer adds the effect to the current layer in the main window (see below).
  • ⑫ The status button shows the current state by color; click it for details (see Status, check and publishing).
  • ⑬ Check, Upload… and Close.

Preview setting values

  • They appear in the same order as in the right dock: the effect's settings, then easing, repeat and repeat count for animated effects, then 'move later effects' center along' for effects that move the image.
  • 'Cels after the range: Keep the end look' is fixed off.
  • Reset goes back to the effect's defaults.
  • Set as default saves the current values and chosen gradients as the effect's defaults.
  • Easing, repeat and pivot motion left at the app defaults (Linear, Once, 1, true) aren't written into the code.

Saving, undo and inserting

  • There is no Save button. An edit is saved as your script effect once you stop editing for 1.5 seconds (with the preview scene).
  • Ctrl+S saves right away.
  • While the code has errors it isn't saved, and the window title ends with *. Choosing another effect or closing then asks Save, Discard or Cancel.
  • A new script is saved once your first edit has no errors.
  • Undo uses the same history as Ctrl+Z, up to 300 steps. It undoes title, description, tag and default edits too, but not preview scene edits.
  • Insert into layer adds the effect with the preview setting values to the end of the current layer's effects (all frames, one undo step). If the effects dialog is open or the layer is locked, you only get a message.

Info and languages

Use the Info area on the left of the Effect Maker to set the effect's name, description and tags or add names in other languages. Editing it updates the code too.

Title, description and tags

  • The header shows the developer on the right: the author's profile picture and name for Store effects, a person icon and 'Made on this computer' for effects made on this computer.
  • Fill in Title (1–100 characters), Description (up to 200) and Tags. They are name, description and tags in the code.
  • Type tags like #neon and press Space, Enter or a comma to add a chip. Remove one with X or Backspace in the empty field; up to 8, lowercase English letters, digits and hyphens.
  • The language you are editing shows next to the field names. When the UI language differs from the effect's base language, you edit that language's translation (dim text is the base value).
  • Effects without a base language (language) show the UI-language translation if there is one, else the written values (Base (language not set)).

Languages

Languages opens a window for the title, description and setting labels in each language. The name for the app's UI language shows in the effects dialog, the layer effect list and the Asset Manager.

  • Add or remove languages, or type another language code. Empty fields show the base-language value; languages with text are green with a check mark.
  • Setting group titles can be translated too.
  • Base language at the top is the language the title and description were written in. OK writes it into the code as language.
  • Without a base language it is guessed from the letters: Hangul → Korean, kana → Japanese, Han → Simplified Chinese, Cyrillic → Russian, Thai → Thai, Latin → English.

My effects list

Use the My effects list on the left of the Effect Maker to pick an effect to edit, download your published effects or revert an edited effect to the Store original.

  • The list has effects made on this computer or published with your account. Turn on Show all to also see received effects (package icon) and unchanged copies of them.
  • Right-click: Delete (your own only), Revert to original (below) and Show in Asset Manager.
  • Effects made only on this computer show just a code icon and the name.
  • ⑭ Effects published with your account show your profile picture (or a person icon) and name next to their name. Without author details it reads Published, or Published · edited after edits.
  • ⑮ Effects published with the current account that aren't on this computer are grey rows at the bottom. The download button on the right checks the downloaded file, then installs and opens it.

Publishing an edited effect again makes a new version of the same package. While signed in, your published effects are fetched when the window opens and when the account changes.

Effects that differ from the Store

When an effect on this computer differs from the Store (online catalog), its name is orange. Hover it for the reason.

  • A newer version is online: update it from the Store in the Asset Manager.
  • This computer's version isn't in the online catalog yet: a newly published version may be under review.
  • Edited after downloading or publishing.
  • Effects never published, or anything when the catalog couldn't be fetched, aren't marked. The catalog is fetched when the window opens (even when signed out).

Such effects get Revert to original in the right-click menu. It asks first, then downloads the Store's latest package and overwrites the source and preview scene. Your changes are lost.

It isn't shown for effects you made yourself or copies, effects identical to the Store's latest, or when this computer's version is newer than the catalog's.

Received effects are read-only

Effects made by others (received effects) open read-only and aren't saved automatically.

  • You can't change: the code, info, preview chick, frames, motion, position, pivot or scene.
  • Turned off: Undo, Upload… and the AI panel.
  • Still available: preview setting values, Check and Insert into layer.

Editing a received effect

  1. Open the effect (turn on Show all if it isn't listed).
  2. Click Copy to My Assets in the bottom row. The received original stays as it is, and your own copy is created and opened (or the existing one opens).
  3. Edit and save the copy. The copy is your own asset, so you can publish it too.

A received original is replaced without asking when a higher version comes from the Store, so always copy an effect you want to change. Effects already on layers don't change (see Asset Manager).

Moving the preview chick

Use the preview motion, end chick and position options to preview effects that react to movement or turn one picture into another. They apply only to previews, never to documents.

Preview motion

The gear button below the preview opens the Preview motion window (its tooltip shows the current motion).

  • ⑯ None, Right, Left, Up, Down (1 px per frame) or Custom: picking one fills the table (dimmed).
  • ⑰ Edit the table to switch to Custom (−32 to 32 px per frame; right and down are +).

OK updates previewMotion in the effect's code. Effects that react to movement, such as afterimages and trails, become visible (ctx.frameSource reads the moved pictures too).

End chick (transform effects)

  • ⑱ Choose an End chick (first item None) and the last frame becomes that chick. Both chicks stand feet-aligned.
  • ⑲ The frame strip shows the first chick turning into the end chick, so you can see and check it before saving. How to write transform effects is in effects that use several frames.

Preview position and pivot

The crosshair button opens the Preview position and pivot window (its tooltip shows the current non-zero values). The pivot is the center point an effect works around.

RowWhat it does
Chick pivotMoves each frame's pivot. With an end chick, every frame but the last.
End chick positionMoves the end chick from its place (feet aligned with the first chick).
End chick pivotMoves the pivot of the last frame.
  • Enter X and Y in px on each row (right and down are +, −64 to 64).
  • OK writes only the changed values into the code; zero removes the value.
  • Without an end chick the two end rows are off (their values are kept).
  • Pivot values are offsets from the chick's center, so moving only the end chick leaves the pivot in place.
  • With a preview scene, offsets start from the scene's chick spot; without one, the preview widens to fit the moved chick.

With End chick position 0 the picture changes in place; moved 16 px to the right, it travels there as it changes.

Preview scene

Use a preview scene to preview effects that read other layers, such as landing on a ground or following a path. It is a small drawing saved with the effect, with layers like a ground or path next to the chick.

  • ⑳ The layer picker in Preview setting values lists the scene's layer names (without the chick layer). Without a scene it only has (none) and the hint "Add a preview scene to pick one of its layers.".
  • ㉑ Preview scene row: edit and remove buttons and the layer names (bottom to top, with the [Chick] spot). It appears for effects that read other layers, or when a scene exists.

Drawing the scene

  1. Click Edit in main editor. Without a scene it creates a default one and opens it in a new tab of the main editor.
  2. Draw with any tool, and add and name layers. Moving the chick layer changes where the chick stands (by its feet); another chick stands on the same spot.
  3. What you draw reaches the preview shortly after. The scene is saved together with the effect.

㉒ While you edit the scene, the Effect Maker becomes a small preview window in the top-right corner of the main editor. Click the preview or the expand button, or close the scene tab, to restore it.

Good to know

  • Default scene: a canvas of chick width × 3 and height × 2 (48–256 px), an empty ground layer, 1 frame. The tab is named "Preview scene - effect name".
  • Limits: canvas 256×256, 8 layers, 20 frames (layers × frames must fit a 512×512 grid).
  • Without a scene, functions that read other layers return null.
  • Effects and child layers in the scene aren't used. A scene with fewer frames than the preview repeats.
  • The scene tab doesn't ask to save when closed. Check, the AI preview and the published preview draw on the scene too; asset files store it as previews/name.scene.png.
  • Remove scene asks, then removes the scene (closing its tab).

Status, check and publishing

Use the status button, Check and Upload to make sure your effect works and publish it to the Store. Watch the status button, test on 20 sample chicks with Check, then click Upload….

  • ㉓ Status button: green = OK or check passed, yellow = warnings only, red = errors (with the count), dim = rendering, checking or no script.
  • ㉔ Click it for details: time per chick frame, which engine runs the code and why, errors and the check result. Click an error line to jump to that code.

Yellow warnings include: runs on the slower engine (Jint), gradient advice, scene couldn't be read. Engines are explained in Script writing tips.

Check

  • Check: runs the install-time code check and checks the title (1–100 characters), then runs the default settings on 20 sample chicks × the frame count.
  • The scene, end chick, motion, position and pivot are used as set. It lists run errors and the slowest frame.
  • Gradient advice: a yellow line points out color settings that still draw in one color when the user picks a gradient. It is only advice and doesn't block publishing.
  • To fix it, pick colors per pixel with ctx.color.
  • If the image moved but there is no ctx.movePivot call reporting it, you get a warning (publishing still works).

Publishing

  • Upload…: saves, and if the check passes opens the Publish online window. The short description and tags start from the effect's values; the preview is made automatically.
  • If the check fails, you are told to open the status button to see the problems.
  • A published preview over the size limit merges pairs of frames.
  • Effects reading other layers always get the layer-reference tag, and effects reading other frames frame-reference.
  • Using ctx.movePivot adds the pivot-move tag. It can't be removed and counts toward the 8 tags.

Rules window

Rules shows the rules, the answer format and the script API list in a separate window. Turn on ㉕ Include the current source to append the editor's source; Copy puts it on the clipboard.

  1. Click Copy ("Copied the guide." shows briefly).
  2. Paste it into another AI such as ChatGPT on the web and describe the effect you want.
  3. Paste the code block from the answer into the editor as a whole.

The rules cover:

  • one effect({ … }) per code file, always with name, description, tags and language (plus an English translation when not English)
  • the same input always gives the same result (randomness via a seed setting), run limits, the fast path and setting types
  • adding useful settings unasked: a reference point (pivot, center, source bounds) plus direction, speed, loop, range, offset, color, strength, size and so on, usually 3–8
  • picking colors per pixel with ctx.color("key", x, y) so gradients are followed (with worse/better examples)
  • reading other layers only through a layer setting and ctx.layer("key") (handling null), other frames (transform, ctx.matchPixels, end chick), and reporting movement with ctx.movePivot

AI panel

Use the AI panel when you want to describe an effect in words and have the AI make it. Open it with the AI button in the Effect Maker; the AI writes the code and checks it with the preview and Check.

  • ㉖ Send the effect you want or what to change. The AI (the same Claude Code or Codex login as the AI chat) reads the code, writes new code into the editor and checks it with the preview and Check. You can undo it in one step in the editor.
  • ㉗ The three buttons under the input attach an image file, the current canvas or the current preview frame. Ctrl+V and drag and drop work too; click a chip to enlarge or remove it.
  • ㉘ The model list left of Send is the same as in the AI chat. A change applies from the next message; the choice is saved and shared with the AI chat.
