What the AI does

  • It adds useful settings on its own and lists them briefly in its answer. It reads other layers through a layer setting and picks colors per pixel so gradients are followed.
  • It can look at the open drawing, layers, effects and references but can't change them. It can also read your other script effects ("like my X effect, but …").
  • Saving, inserting into the layer and publishing are up to you with the buttons below.
  • Ask and it searches and reads the web (it doesn't otherwise). It can't see a page's pictures or animation, so it works from the descriptions and rewrites any code it saw with the app's effect API.

Message queue

  • Pressing Enter or Send while the AI's turn is running puts the message in the queue above the input; queued messages are sent in order when the turn ends (even when stopped).
  • Each row has a drag handle (change the order), edit (pencil), delete (trash can) and Send now.
  • Edit brings the message back into the input; Send now stops the running turn and sends it right away.
  • New chat clears the queue.

Past chats

The header has the Claude/Codex switch, ㉙ New chat and Close. New chat saves the current conversation (the editor's source stays) and shows the ㉚ Past chats list.

  • Click a row to continue that conversation. The star marks a favorite (pinned on top, never removed automatically); the trash can deletes it.
  • Conversations are saved per AI at the end of every turn; opening the panel continues the last one. They are kept apart from the AI chat (Continuing past chats), up to 20 per AI.
  • Beyond 20, the oldest non-favorites are removed.
  • If the CLI (the AI program) is missing or needs a login, install or log in with Open AI chat, then click Check again.

Asset manager

Manage effects, effect presets, gradients and shape pen sets in one place. Get them from the Store, share them as files, and publish your own.

PageRead this when
Asset Manager and the Installed tabYou want to see, edit or delete the assets you have
Store and updatesYou want to get assets from the Store or update them
Sharing as filesYou want to give or receive assets as .chickxel-asset files
Managing shape pen setsYou want to make, edit or export shape pen sets
Publish OnlineYou want to publish your asset to the Store and chickxel.com

Asset Manager and the Installed tab

Use it to see, edit and delete your effects, gradients and shape pen sets in one place. Open it with Tools > Asset Manager….

Installed tab

  • ① There are Installed, Store and Updates tabs. For Store and Updates, see Store and updates.
  • ② Filter by Effects / Gradients / Shape pen and search by name. Effects shows script effects and effect presets together.
  • ③ Import from File… and Export… share files, Publish Online… publishes, and the last button brings back deleted default assets.
  • ④ The list shows My assets first, then received assets. Each row shows the author, the version and where it came from (From store, Default (store), From file).
  • ⑤ Shows the selected asset's preview, description, facts and buttons; the buttons are in the table below.
Selected assetButtons
My assetsFavorite (gradients), Edit in Effect Maker (script effects), Rename… (not script effects), Edit parameters (effect presets), Publish Online… or Publish New Version… (not effect presets), Delete.
Received assetsFavorite (gradients), Copy to My Assets, Open Web Page (when the author has a website), View License (when there is a license file), Delete. To edit one, copy it to My assets first.

Asset kinds

Where each kind of asset is used and whether it can be published.

Asset kindWhere it is used
Script effectThe Effects tab of the effects dialog (see Script effects). Can be published.
Effect presetThe Presets tab of the effects dialog (see Effect presets). Exchanged as files only, not published.
GradientThe gradient Presets window and everywhere you pick a gradient (see Gradient). Can be published.
Shape pen setThe set list in the Shape pen group of Tool options (see Shape pen). Can be published; needs app 1.14.120 or later.

Other ways to open

  • Asset Manager in the gradient Presets window opens it filtered to gradients.
  • Show in Asset Manager in the right-click menu of an effect box or the Effect Maker list opens it with that asset selected.
  • Manage sets… in the Shape pen tool options opens the Shape pen filter with the current set selected.
  • Get more at the bottom of the Effects tab opens the website store in your browser instead.
  • Open in Chickxel on chickxel.com opens the Store tab (see Store and updates), and double-clicking a .chickxel-asset file opens the Installed tab (see Sharing as files).

Good to know

  • Delete can't be undone. Bring back deleted default assets with the restore button in ③ (edited ones stay). Effects and gradients already in a project stay even if you delete the asset.
  • Received effects and effect presets appear in the effects dialog next to your own, with the Store name in the tooltip. Rename, Parameters and Delete are only for your own.
  • Edit a received gradient in the gradient Presets window and click Save to My Assets to keep the original and make your own copy (see Gradient).
  • On first launch, old effect presets and gradient presets move into My assets; the old files stay in the settings folder as …backup-date.json.
  • If you change one of your published assets, 'modified' appears next to its version.
  • Script effects installed through the Asset Manager draw without asking.

Store and updates

Find and install assets published on chickxel.com on the Store tab, and bring received assets up to date on the Updates tab.

Store tab

  • ⑥ The online list is downloaded from chickxel.com the first time you open the tab. There are the kind filter, search (name, description, author, tags), All tags, Refresh and Open on Website.
  • ⑦ Each row shows the name, author, version, kind and status (installed version, new version, or can't be installed). If it can't be installed, the details say why and Install is off.
  • ⑧ Preview image, short description, version, author, license, required app version, size, review (Official Chickxel, Reviewed or Community), tags, and the Install (or Update to …, Show in Installed) and Open Web Page buttons.

Installing

  1. Pick an asset in the list (⑦). Narrow it down with the filter, search or tags.
  2. Clicking Install shows a progress bar and Cancel in its place.
  3. When it finishes, the asset appears on the Installed tab and wherever that kind is used.

Click Open in Chickxel on a chickxel.com package page and Chickxel comes to the front (starting if needed) with that package selected on the Store tab. Nothing is installed until you click Install. On Windows, this works after you have started Chickxel once (see Installation).

Updates tab

  • Check for updates downloads the online list again and shows, for each asset with a new version, an 'Installed 1.0.0 → new version 1.1.0' row and an Update to … button.
  • New versions are installed only when you click. The tab name shows the number of updates (e.g. Updates (1)), or 'All received assets are up to date.' when there are none.
  • Without an internet connection, an orange note says so and the last downloaded list is shown. Installed assets keep working.

Store versions and local edits

  • Assets from the Store stay linked to their package and are overwritten only by a higher version. Local edits are replaced without asking; favorites stay.
  • To keep your changes, first make a copy with Copy to My Assets; updates never overwrite the copy. Received script effects are read-only in the Effect Maker too.
  • Effects already placed in a project don't change when the asset is updated or deleted.
  • If the downloaded file doesn't match the online list, Chickxel reports an error and changes nothing.
  • The 23 official effects, 8 default gradients and 8 built-in shape pen sets are installed as Store assets on first launch (Default (store), author chickxel).

Sharing as files

Use this to give effects, gradients and shape pen sets to a friend or to receive theirs. Assets travel in .chickxel-asset files, several per file.

Exporting

  1. On the Installed tab, filter by kind and search by name until only the assets to send are shown.
  2. Click Export… and choose where to save. All assets shown go into one file.
  3. Send the file to the other person.
  • Each asset in the file keeps its author. Assets you made get your signed-in account's name as author.
  • If you send a single Store asset unchanged, the other person also gets its Store updates.
  • Asset details have no export button; only your own shape pen sets export one at a time with the Export menu in their details (see Managing shape pen sets).

Importing

  1. Click Import from File… and pick a .chickxel-asset file, or double-click the file.
  2. Chickxel checks the file first. If anything is wrong, each problem is listed and nothing is added.
  3. If it passes, the assets inside are added one by one.
  • An asset that needs a newer Chickxel tells you which version.
  • If Chickxel is already running, a double-clicked file opens in that window. On Windows, it works after you have started Chickxel once (see Installation).

Overlapping assets

  • If the same asset already exists, choose Replace, Keep Both or Skip in the Import Asset window. Turn on Apply to the remaining items to do the same for the remaining overlaps.
  • Importing a file asks for every overlap. Downloading from the Store asks only when an asset with the same content isn't linked to the Store.

Managing shape pen sets

Use this to make, edit and share the shape sets the shape pen stamps. Choose the Shape pen filter in the Asset Manager.

Making a set from a drawing

  1. Draw one shape per frame.
  2. Click New set from canvas….
  3. The new set appears in your sets and can be picked in the Shape pen tool options.

The Shape pen filter

  • ⑨ New set from canvas… makes a new set with one shape per frame of the current document. Restore built-in sets puts back built-in sets you deleted.
  • ⑩ Your sets come first. Each row shows the first shape, the name and the shape count.
  • ⑪ Then come the eight built-in sets and sets received from the Store or files.
  • ⑫ The selected set of yours: its shape grid, facts and shape count.
  • ⑬ Buttons for your set: Edit on canvas opens the set in a temporary document tab to edit it. The others are Rename, Export (⑭), Publish and Delete.
  • ⑭ Set file (.chickxel-shapes)… saves the set as a set file, and Asset package (.chickxel-asset)… saves it as an asset package.
  • ⑮ A received set shows its shape grid, description and facts (author, license, Store ID, version, source).
  • ⑯ To change a received set, make a copy with Copy to my sets. Edit on canvas also makes a copy and opens it. Open Web Page, View License and Delete are there too.

Good to know

  • In this filter, Import from File… also accepts set files (.chickxel-shapes) and PNGs, several at once, as new sets. Export… bundles the sets shown into one package.
  • Only sets with 1–64 shapes, up to 256px per side, can be published (see Publish online).
  • Deleted built-in sets aren't reinstalled; bring them back with Restore built-in sets in ⑨.
  • A set you published stays yours even when received from the Store, so you edit it in place and publish new versions.
  • If you change a published set, 'modified' appears next to its version.

Publish Online

Publishes one of your assets to the Store and chickxel.com with your Chickxel account. Open it with Publish Online… at the top of the Installed tab or in your asset's details.

  • ⑨ Author: the profile picture, display name and @handle of your signed-in account. Change them in Help > Chickxel Account…, not here (the handle is fixed once you upload a package).
  • ⑩ Package: the top line says whether this is a new package or an update of one of yours. The fields are in the Package fields table below.
  • ⑪ License: always CC0-1.0. Anyone may use the asset without conditions (even commercially, without credit); the package includes the text as LICENSE.txt.
  • ⑫ Asset: the name and kind of the asset being published.
  • ⑬ List preview: the picture shown in the Store and on chickxel.com. It starts as an automatic preview made from the asset; Replace with Image File… uses a PNG (up to 512×512, 1 MB) and Use Asset Preview goes back. The automatic preview also includes the original without the effect, compared by hovering on chickxel.com.
  • ⑭ Pre-upload check: checked as you type, with the server's rules. A red X is an error (blocks uploading); an orange exclamation mark is a warning (you can still upload).

Package fields

FieldWhat it does
Name, short descriptionThe name and a description of up to 200 characters shown in the Store. Script effects fill these in from their declaration.
Languages…Adds a name and short description per language, shown to people viewing in that language. Languages with text show in green.
Package IDSet automatically and can't be edited: the ID of your published package if the asset came from it, otherwise 'handle.name'. Rename to publish a different package.
VersionSuggests the next patch version (1.0.0 → 1.0.1). It must be higher than any version already published.
TagsType tags like #neon and add them with Space, Enter or a comma; remove one with its X or Backspace in an empty field. Up to 8, lowercase English letters, digits and hyphens, 32 characters each.

Uploading

  1. With no errors, a blue check says all checks passed and Upload turns on. Click it.
  2. While uploading, the button shows 'Uploading… N%' with a progress bar, then 'Checking on server…' after the transfer. Cancel Upload stops it.
  3. Once uploaded, the package appears right away in the Store and on chickxel.com (chickxel.com/packages/<ID>). Click Open website in the message to open its page. Later uploads become new versions of the same package.

Checks and tags

  • Main errors: wrong package ID, version, name, description, language settings or tag format; no list preview; a name that clashes with another package; a version not higher than one already published. Warning: no tags.
  • Effects that read other layers get the layer-reference tag, effects that read other frames frame-reference, and effects using ctx.movePivot pivot-move, automatically. These tags can't be removed and count toward the 8; if there's no room, an error asks you to remove another tag.
  • If the server rejects the package, the reason appears at the end of the checks as 'Rejected by the server: …'.

Good to know

  • If you aren't signed in, the account window opens first.
  • One asset makes one package. To publish a received asset, copy it to My assets and publish the copy. Effect presets can't be published; publish effects as script effects.
  • When the app language differs from the package's base language, the name and description fields edit the app-language translation. Edit the base-language text at the top of the Languages… window.
  • A package deleted on chickxel.com disappears from the site, the Store and your profile, and its XP and storage quota are returned. Until an admin restores or permanently deletes it, its ID can't be used again.

Tools

Tools sit on the toolbar at the left of the window, and the selected tool's settings appear in Tool options on the right. Click a name in the table to open its page.

PurposeToolsUse it to
BasicsTool list · Drawing basics · Tool groups · Brush settings · Drawing on several frameslearn the mouse and key rules, the brush and symmetry that all drawing tools share
DrawingPencil · Gradient Pencil · Shape Pen · Shape pen sets · Making sets on the canvas · Curve Pen · Eraser · Shape · Textdraw and erase lines, or add shapes and text
ColorFill · Gradient · Eyedropper · Dodge/burn · Dithering · Replace color brushpaint large areas, pick colors, or change the shading, pattern or color of what is painted
Selection and transformSelection tools · Magic wand · Working with a selection · Move · Transform mode · Canvas size mode · Aspect ratio window · Transform whole layerselect part of the picture to move or rotate it, or resize the canvas or a whole layer
Tidying linesClean up linesredraw wobbly or thick lines as clean 1 px lines in one go
UnityPivot · 9-sliceset the reference point and stretchable border of Unity sprites

In the app, press F1 and then click a toolbar slot or the tool options to open that tool's page.

Tool list

Select a tool in the ① toolbar and its settings appear in ② Tool options. For longer explanations, hover the i icon next to a title.

The numbers match the toolbar slots in the picture above. Tools with the same number share one slot (Tool groups).

ToolShortcutWhat it does
③ PencilBPaints pixels in the brush shape
③ Gradient PencilShift+BPaints along the stroke with gradient colors
③ Shape Pen—Stamps small pictures (leaves, flowers and more) from a set
③ Curve PenShift+LBuilds a curve path and paints it as a clean 1 px line
④ EraserEErases pixels to transparent
⑤ FillGFills an area of the same or a similar color
⑤ GradientFDrag to paint a linear gradient
⑥ EyedropperI, Alt+clickPicks a color from the canvas
⑦ ShapeRDrags out 11 shapes such as lines, rectangles, ellipses and stars. L = line, U = toggle Fill
⑧ TextTTypes text on the canvas as a text layer
⑨ Rectangle select / Ellipse selectMDrag to select an area
⑨ Lasso selectQDraw an outline to select
⑨ Magic wandWSelects an area of similar color
⑩ MoveVMoves the selection, or the whole cel when nothing is selected
⑪ Dodge/burnDRub to lighten (left button) or darken (right button)
⑫ Dithering—Paints two colors mixed in a pattern
⑬ Replace color brushCRepaints only the first color it touches
⑭ PivotKSets the pivot (reference point)
⑮ 9-sliceSSets the stretchable border of a Unity sprite

Holding Ctrl over the canvas switches to the Move tool, and holding Alt in a drawing tool switches to the eyedropper, until you release the key.

Drawing basics

Read this when you start drawing. The mouse buttons, keys and where paint goes work the same in every drawing tool.

Mouse buttons

ButtonWhat it does
Left buttonPaints with the ① primary color.
Right buttonPaints with the ② secondary color.
The other button while drawingCancels that stroke. The status bar shows Cancel stroke.
  • With ③ Right button = eraser on, the right button of the pencil, gradient pencil, shape and fill tools erases.
  • Turn it on in the Options group of the tool options or in Drawing options in the Tools menu.
  • Tools where the buttons differ: Dodge/burn (lighten/darken), Dithering (swaps the two colors), the Eyedropper (which color gets the pick) and Gradient (left button only).

Using keys

④ The top bar always shows what the current tool can do.

KeyWhat it does
Shift+clickDraws a straight line from the end of the last stroke to where you click. Shapes keep their proportions; the gradient snaps to 45°.
AltWhile held, the tool becomes the eyedropper.
CtrlWhile held, the tool becomes the Move tool.
Ctrl+wheelChanges the brush size by 1.
[ / ]Decreases or increases the brush size by 1 (1–32).
XSwaps the primary and secondary colors.

Where paint goes

  • With a selection, you paint only inside it.
  • With symmetry drawing on, every tool except the gradient also paints at the mirrored spots (Brush settings).
  • In tile mode, strokes that cross an edge continue on the opposite side.
  • If the layer is locked, nothing is drawn and the status bar shows The current layer is locked and can't be drawn on..

Drawing commands in the Edit menu

Edit menu commands change the whole picture or the selection at once. Each is one undo step.

CommandWhat it does
⑤ Add outline (primary color) (Ctrl+Alt+O)
Add outline (with diagonals)		Adds a 1 px outline in the primary color around the drawing. Add outline (with diagonals) also fills diagonal pixels.
⑥ Replace color… (Ctrl+Alt+R)Changes every pixel of one color to another in the current layer and frame. The window's Color to replace and New color start as the primary and secondary colors. With a selection, Selection only limits it.
⑦ Clean up lines…Redraws outlines and inner lines as clean 1 px lines and arcs (Clean up lines).
⑧ Flip horizontal (selection/cel) (Shift+H)
Flip vertical (selection/cel) (Shift+V)		Flips the selected pixels, or the current layer's picture in this frame when nothing is selected.
⑧ Rotate 90° (selection/canvas) (Ctrl+R)
Rotate -90° (selection/canvas) (Ctrl+Shift+R)		Rotates the selected pixels by 90°; press Enter to drop them. With nothing selected, the whole canvas turns with all frames.
⑨ Canvas size… (Ctrl+Alt+C)Sets the new size and image position on the canvas (Canvas size mode).

Good to know

  • Pressing a button and releasing it is one stroke, and one stroke is one undo step.
  • Colors painted with the pencil, fill, shape, dithering and color replace brush go into the recent colors in the color panel. Transparent paint doesn't.

Tool groups

Similar tools share one slot: the pencil group, ① the fill group and ② the select group. Group slots have a small triangle in the bottom-right corner.

  • A plain click selects the tool shown in the slot.
  • Long-press or right-click to expand ③ the list of tools in the group.
  • ③ Hover a tool in the list to see ④ its usage hint.

A group slot shows the tool you picked last. A tool picked with its shortcut shows up in its slot too.

Brush settings

Read this to change the shape and thickness of your brush tip, or to draw with left-right or top-bottom symmetry. Brush tools such as the pencil and eraser, and the Shape tool, use these settings.

Shape and size

  • ① Pick the shape: Square / Circle / Diamond. ② The box on the left shows the pixels it will paint.
  • ③ Size 1–32: type a number, or change it by 1 with [ / ] or Ctrl+wheel over the canvas.

Size 1 is a single pixel whatever the shape. Even sizes extend one pixel further right and down from the center.

Which tools share a brush

  • The eraser remembers its own brush. The other brush tools and the Shape tool share one brush. Both are kept for the next launch.
  • ④ In the Shape tool the same group sets the line width and pen shape of shapes. The inside of a filled shape ignores the brush.

Symmetry drawing

  • ⑤ Horizontal symmetry (Shift+X) and vertical symmetry (Shift+Y) also draw on the other side of the canvas center axis.
  • ⑥ Around pivot (Tools menu Drawing options > Mirror around pivot): the axes go through the current layer's pivot (its reference point). Mirrored spots outside the canvas are not painted (in tile mode they wrap).

With symmetry on, a gray brush outline shows at every mirrored spot on the canvas.

Drawing on several frames

Use this to draw or fix the same thing on several frames at once. A stroke on the current frame also lands at the same spot on the other frames in the range.

How to use it

  1. Choose a Range in the Apply to frames group of the tool options (table below).
  2. For ranges with frames before or after, enter the counts in Before and After (1–999). The affected frames show below, in orange when there is more than one.
  3. Draw on the current frame. The same spot on the same layer's other frames gets it too.

Ranges

RangeFrames that also get the stroke
Current frame onlyOnly the current frame (default).
Up to N frames beforeThe current frame and N frames before it.
Up to N frames afterThe current frame and N frames after it.
Before and afterThe current frame plus N before and M after.
Back to the first frameFrom the first frame to the current one.
On to the last frameFrom the current frame to the last one.
All framesEvery frame.

How each tool applies

Check this table if you wonder what lands on the other frames.

ToolOn the other frames
Pencil, eraser (100% strength), dither, shape, gradient pencil, curve penPaints the same color on the same pixels.
Lighten/darken, eraser with partial strengthChanges that frame's pixels by the same amount.
Shape PenStamps the same shapes over that frame.
Replace color brushChanges only pixels of the color first pressed on.
FillFills the same spot again based on that frame's drawing.
GradientPaints again with the same start and end points.

Good to know

  • All frames together are one undo step. The status bar shows how many frames changed.
  • Linked cels are changed once. Locked layers and empty layers that arrange their children are skipped.
  • Empty cels get drawn on too. The canvas shows only the current frame.
  • The range resets to Current frame only each time the app starts. The counts are remembered.

Pencil

The pencil (B) paints pixels in the brush shape along your drag. Painted pixels take the chosen color as-is, without blending.

Primary and secondary colors

  • The left button paints in the primary color, the right button in the secondary color. Swap them with X.
  • With Right button = eraser on, the right button erases with the eraser's strength and channels.
  • Press the other button mid-stroke to cancel it. Alt+click picks a color (eyedropper).

Straight lines (Shift+click)

Hold Shift to preview a line from the end of the last stroke to the pointer. Click to paint it, or drag to keep drawing from its end.

Pixel perfect

Turn on Pixel perfect (P) to remove the doubled pixels at stair corners of size-1 strokes as you draw. It is in the tool options and in Drawing options in the Tools menu.

With it off, every turn gets an extra pixel and the line looks thicker in places.

Nothing is removed at size 2 or more. Going back over a stroke leaves no holes, and Shift+click lines have no doubled pixels to begin with.

Pixel steps

  • ① With Pixel perfect on, Pixel steps is dimmed and strokes join diagonally. Turn it off to return to your pixel steps choice.
  • ② Pixel steps: Orthogonal (default) joins pixels only sideways and up/down, so each touches the next by an edge. Diagonal gives the thinnest line, touching at corners.

The pencil, gradient pencil and eraser share this setting. It is separate from the Shape tool's pixel steps.

Brush

Set the shape and Size (1–32) in the Brush group. Details and symmetry drawing are in Brush settings.

Gradient Pencil

The Gradient Pencil (Shift+B) draws like the pencil but colors each pixel from a gradient. It is in the pencil group slot.

  • Straight lines, the brush, pixel perfect, pixel steps, symmetry and the selection work as with the pencil.
  • The right button erases with Right button = eraser on; otherwise it paints like the left button.
  • Painted colors are not added to the recent colors.

Gradient settings

  • ① Mode: Whole stroke (default) spreads the gradient once over the stroke; Repeat repeats it every interval.
  • ② Interval (2–512px, default 16): the stroke length of one repeat. Shown only for Repeat.
  • ③ Presets: pick a preset or a package gradient, or save the current one. Default resets to black → white.
  • ④ The stop bar, colors, interpolation, color space and dithering use the same editor as the gradient tool. The settings are kept separately from the gradient tool.

How colors are assigned

Whole stroke re-spreads the colors over the length drawn so far and fixes them when you release. Length is measured along the mouse path.

Opacity stops replace pixels with their value instead of blending, so a fully transparent part erases what is below.

Shape Pen

The Shape Pen stamps small pictures (shapes) from the chosen set. Use it to draw many similar things, such as leaves, flowers or sparkles.

Stamping

  • Pick it by long-pressing or right-clicking the pencil group slot. It is also in the Tools menu; there is no default shortcut.
  • Click to stamp the next shape centered where you press. The shape's own colors and opacity are layered on top.
  • Drag to keep stamping at each spacing, with the next shape previewed under the cursor. One stroke is one undo step.
  • Symmetry (mirrored stamps are flipped), tile mode, the selection and drawing outside the canvas work as with the pencil. Alt+click picks a color.

Shape pen settings

ItemWhat it does
① SetChooses the set to stamp. Your sets come first, then received sets (Shape pen sets).
② Set buttonsNew set, rename, delete set, restore built-in sets and Manage sets…. Deleting cannot be undone; Manage sets opens the Asset Manager.
③ Shape tilesThe highlighted tile is stamped next; click a tile to stamp it next. Right-click or the trash button deletes it (your own sets only).
④ Add shapeAdds shapes from the selection, the current cel or PNG files (below).
⑤ OrderHow the next shape is chosen (table below). Remembered per set.
⑥ SpacingDistance between stamps while dragging (px, default 1). At 1 every pixel passed gets a stamp.
⑦ Optimized spacingWhen on (default), each stamp lands at the first spot that doesn't overlap the previous one. Spacing is dimmed meanwhile.
⑧ Tint with current colorWhen on, keeps the shading but changes the color to the primary color (secondary with the right button). Off by default.

Adding shapes

The Add shape button's menu adds shapes to the chosen set. The added shape is stamped next.

  • From selection: adds the selected pixels of the current cel.
  • From current cel: adds the whole current cel.
  • From PNG files…: adds one or more PNG files at once.

The transparent border is trimmed. Fully transparent images and images over 256px on a side are not added. If there is no set, one is created.

Order

OrderWhat it does
RandomStamps any shape from the set. The same shape never comes twice in a row.
In orderStamps the shapes in order, starting over after the last.
SelectedAlways stamps only the highlighted shape.

Spacing and optimized spacing

Spacing 1 overlaps the shapes densely into a bush-like trail. Optimized spacing keeps each shape visible and touching, even when sizes differ. To set the distance yourself, turn optimized spacing off and raise the spacing.

Tinting and the right button

With Tint with current color on, the shape's most common color becomes the primary color and the others follow, keeping the shading. Use it to stamp autumn leaves from green ones.

With Right button = eraser on, the right button erases in the shape, by each pixel's opacity.

Shape pen sets

Read this to choose, get or share the groups of shapes (sets) the shape pen stamps. You choose a set in the Set list of the tool options.

  • ⑨ Your sets: sets you made or imported. You can edit them.
  • ⑩ Received sets: the built-in sets and sets from the Store or package files. You can't edit them; the icon before the name shows the source (globe = Store, package = package file).

⑪ With a received set chosen, rename, delete, Add shape and Order are off. To change it, press Copy to my sets in the Asset Manager or edit it on the canvas.

Built-in sets

The built-in sets are Random Chickxel (twenty chicks), Flowers 1, 2 and 3, Sparkles, Bubbles, Autumn Leaves and Leaves. All stamp in Random order.

If you deleted built-in sets, reinstall them with Restore built-in sets among the set buttons. Old versions are updated too; your own and edited sets stay as they are.

Exporting and importing sets

  1. Press Manage sets… to open the Asset Manager and select your set.
  2. Choose Export > Set file (.chickxel-shapes)… in its details and save. You get one .chickxel-shapes file.
  3. Give the file to someone or move it to another computer.

Import a set file with Import from File… in the Shape pen filter of the Asset Manager, or drop it onto the main window. It becomes a new set of yours; PNGs can be imported the same way.

Getting and publishing sets in the Store

  • Get: install a set from the Shape pen filter on the Store tab of the Asset Manager. It gets updates too.
  • To change a received set, press Copy to my sets in its details. The original keeps getting updates.
  • Publish: press Publish Online… (or Publish New Version… once published) in your set's details to open the publish window. Sign-in is required.

Good to know

  • The built-in sets are official Store assets: the Asset Manager shows them with the source Default (store), and you delete them there.
  • Asset package (.chickxel-asset)… exports a .chickxel-asset package, and Export… on the toolbar bundles the sets shown into one package. Sets imported from a package become received sets.
  • Imports never overwrite a set; a name in use gets (2), (3)… appended.
  • A set to publish needs 1–64 shapes, each at most 256px per side. Received sets can't be published.
  • A set you published and then received can be edited, renamed and published again as your own.
  • Packages with shape pen sets need app 1.14.120 or later.
  • Other built-in sets of 1.14.117–1.14.121 (grass, stars and more) were removed and are in the Store. Ones you had stay as your own sets.
  • Your sets are saved in <settings folder>/Chickxel/ShapePen/shape-sets.json. If it is damaged, it is moved to shape-sets.json.bad and Chickxel starts without your sets.

Making sets on the canvas

Draw or edit a shape pen set on the canvas like any document. Each frame is one shape.

Editing a set on the canvas

  1. Select the set in the Asset Manager and press Edit on canvas. The set opens in a temporary document tab.
  2. Draw or change the shape on each frame. Undo works as in any document.
  3. Press Save to set. The Save shortcut Ctrl+S does the same.
  • ⑫ The tab is named 'Shape set: set name'.
  • ⑬ The bar under the tab shows the set being edited and a short note.
  • ⑭ Save to set merges each frame's visible layers, trims the transparent border and writes it as a shape. Empty frames are skipped.
  • ⑮ Close closes the tab. With unsaved changes you are asked Save to set / Don't save / Cancel.
  • ⑯ Adding a frame adds a shape; deleting or emptying one drops it. The frame order is the shape order.

New set from the canvas

New set from canvas… (film icon) in the Shape pen filter of the Asset Manager makes a new set from the current document. It asks for a name, adds the set at the end of yours and selects it.

Each frame becomes one shape, and empty frames are skipped. Use it to turn shapes drawn across one document's frames into a set at once.

Good to know

  • With more than 64 shapes or a side over 256px, you are told which frames are the problem and nothing is written.
  • The canvas is the size of the largest shape; each shape is centered in its frame.
  • A received set is copied to your sets first and the copy opens. If you are its author, it is edited in place.
  • An already open set goes to its tab. This tab never asks about saving a project file.

Curve Pen

The Curve Pen (Shift+L) builds a curve path from anchor points and handles and paints it as a 1 px line. Use it for smooth curves that are hard to draw by hand.

  • Straight segments get even steps, and curved ones get steps that change evenly. It always paints 1 px, whatever the brush.
  • Until it is painted you see ① the pixels to paint, ② the path line, ③ anchor points (squares) and ④ handles (circles).

Curve pen settings

ItemWhat it does
⑤ Drawing modePoints (default) places points one click at a time; Freehand fits a dragged stroke into a curve.
⑥ Fit toleranceFreehand only: how far the stroke may stray from the curve (0.1–4 px, default 0.6). Larger means fewer points and more smoothing.
⑦ Corner angleFreehand only: turns of this angle (5–180°, default 40°) or more become corner points. At 180° nothing is split.
⑧ Pixel perfectWhen on (default), doubled pixels are removed and steps are evened out. Kept separately from the pencil's setting.

With it off, pixels double up at every turn and the line looks thicker in places.

A freehand path you haven't adjusted yet is refitted as soon as you change a setting.

Points

ActionWhat it does
ClickAdds a corner point at the end of the path.
Click and dragAdds a smooth point and pulls out its two handles.
Click the first pointCloses the path.
Click away from the pathOn an open path it adds a point there. On a closed path, or with the other button, it paints the path and starts a new one.

Freehand

ActionWhat it does
Drag and releaseOn release the stroke is fitted with as few points as possible. Releasing near the start closes the shape.
Hold Shift while draggingMakes one straight line from start to end.
Hold Alt while draggingFits without splitting at corners.
Click without draggingPaints a single pixel.
Press outside the pathPaints the previewed path and starts a new stroke.

Adjusting the path

In both modes, adjust the path like this until it is painted.

ActionWhat it does
Drag an anchor point (square)Moves only that point and its handles.
Drag a handle (circle)On a smooth point the opposite handle stays in line. Alt+drag moves the two handles separately.
Alt+click an anchor pointToggles corner point ↔ smooth point. Alt+click elsewhere picks a color.
Double-click the pathAdds a point there without changing the shape.
Double-click an anchor point, or hover it and press Delete or BackspaceRemoves the point and joins the segments around it. Removing the last point discards the path.
Press the other mouse button while draggingUndoes the stroke or point you were dragging.

Painting and discarding

  • Press Enter, or switch tool, layer or frame, to paint the path. Each path is one undo step.
  • Left-button paths use the primary color, right-button paths the secondary color. With Right button = eraser on, right-button paths erase completely.
  • It paints only inside the selection and follows symmetry drawing, tile mode and Apply to frames.
  • Esc or Ctrl+Z discards the path without painting.

To turn lines you already drew into clean lines at once, use Clean up lines… in the Edit menu.

Eraser

Use it to erase what you drew. The Eraser (E) erases pixels along your drag to transparent, with either button.

  • Shift+click erases along a straight line (as with the pencil).
  • The brush shape and size are remembered separately from the pencil's (Brush settings).
  • Pixel perfect and pixel steps are the pencil's settings, and the selection and symmetry work the same way.

Erase strength and channels

  • ① Strength 1–100% (default 100%): 100% erases completely; lower values fade pixels a little with each pass.
  • ② Channels R, G, B: with all on it erases to transparent. With only some on, it removes just those color components and keeps the opacity.
  • ③·④·⑤ Apply to layers: also erases the same spot on other layers (Erasing several layers at once).

One pass at 50% makes pixels half transparent; release and rub again to fade what is left by half.

With only R on nothing turns transparent; only red is removed. Red turns black, white turns cyan and green stays.

Good to know

  • Right-button erasing in the pencil and gradient pencil follows this strength and these channels. The shape, fill and curve pen tools always erase completely.

Erasing several layers at once

Use this to erase something drawn across several layers, such as lines, colors and shadows, in one go. The eraser stroke also lands at the same spot on other layers of the frame.

How to use it

  1. Choose ③ a range in the Apply to layers group of the eraser's tool options (table below).
  2. For ranges above or below, enter ④ the counts in Above and Below (1–999). ⑤ The number of layers in range shows below.
  3. Erase. The same spot on the layers in range is erased with the same strength and channels.

Ranges

③ RangeLayers that also get the stroke
Current layer onlyOnly the current layer (default).
Up to N layers aboveThe current layer and N layers above it.
Up to N layers belowThe current layer and N layers below it.
Above and belowThe current layer plus N above and M below.
Up to the top layerFrom the current layer to the top.
Down to the bottom layerFrom the bottom to the current layer.
All layersEvery layer.
Selected layersThe current layer and the layers selected in the timeline (Selecting in the timeline). With none selected, only the current layer.

⑥ With Selected layers, a note shows instead of the count boxes. Above and below follow the layer stacking order.

Good to know

  • Only the eraser has this; right-button erasing in other tools doesn't use it.
  • Locked layers, empty layers that arrange children, and tilemaps are skipped.
  • With Apply to frames set too, it also erases the chosen frames of every chosen layer. All of it is one undo step.
  • The range resets to Current layer only each time the app starts.

Shape

The Shape tool (R) drags out 11 shapes, from lines and curves to stars and cubes. The result is previewed while you drag.

The left button draws in the primary color, the right button in the secondary color. Line width, pen shape and symmetry work as in Brush settings.

Shape settings

  • ① Pick the shape with the shape buttons. L switches straight to the line.
  • ② The line under the shape buttons explains how to draw the shape and what Shift does.
  • ③ Set Corners (3–32) for the Regular polygon, Points (3–32) for the Star and Turns (1–10) for the Spiral.
  • ④ Fill (U) paints the inside too. Lines, curves and spirals can't be filled.

How to draw each shape

ShapeHow to draw
LineFrom press to release. Shift = 15° steps
CurveDrag both ends, release, then move the mouse to bend. Same button or Enter confirms; the other button or Esc cancels. Shift = 15° steps, or an arc while bending
RectangleThe dragged box. Shift = square
EllipseAn ellipse in the dragged box. Shift = circle
Equilateral triangleThe dragged line is one side (drag right to point up). Shift = 30° steps
Isosceles triangleFits the dragged box; drag down for the tip on top. Shift = equilateral proportions
Regular polygonWith the set number of corners, in the dragged box. Shift = keep it regular
StarWith the set number of points, in the dragged box. Shift = keep it regular
SpiralA spiral winding outward; drag left to wind the other way. Shift = square
CylinderTop ellipse, both sides and the front half of the bottom. Shift = square
CubeThree faces in 2:1 isometric lines. Shift = square

Pixel steps (lines and curves)

  • ⑤ Pixel steps for lines and curves: Diagonal (default) gives the thinnest line, Orthogonal makes every pixel touch the next by an edge.

A fill with Connect diagonally too (8 directions) on leaks through the gaps of a diagonal line. Draw outlines you will fill that way with Orthogonal.

Text

Use it to put text on the canvas. Type right on it with the Text tool (T) and a new text layer appears.

Typing text

  1. Click the T slot on the toolbar to pick the Text tool.
  2. Drag on the canvas to make ① a text box. A plain click makes a single line.
  3. Type. The letters appear as pixels as you go.
  4. Finish with Esc, Ctrl/⌘+Enter or a click outside. Enter starts a new line.

Editing and moving

  • Click existing text to put the cursor there and edit it.
  • The eight handles resize the box; the top and bottom edges also change the letter size. Drag the border to move it.
  • Dragging with the Move tool moves the text (with no selection and an empty current cel).

Text

Changes apply to the text being edited right away.

  • ② Font: the pixel fonts built into the app and fonts you added.
  • ③ Add Font… copies a .ttf, .otf, .ttc or .bdf file into Fonts in the app settings folder. Open Font Folder opens that folder.
  • ④ Size (0 = the font's own size): pixel fonts work at any size, but strokes may drop out below the original size. The buttons next to it are bold and italic.
  • ⑤ Alignment: left, center, right.
  • ⑥ Letter spacing and line spacing (px).
  • ⑦ Width and height scale 25–400%, and Smooth (vector fonts only, semi-transparent edges).

Fonts

⑧ The list starts with the built-in pixel fonts: Galmuri and NeoDunggeunmo for Korean, Unifont for almost every character, Cozette and Spleen for Latin and symbols.

Text layers

  • When you finish, a text layer appears just above the current layer, named after the first line. Empty text makes no layer.
  • The text is stored as a Text effect, so you can change the font, position and letter-by-letter typing in the Effects window.
  • To edit it as pixels, use Merge down or Bake in the Effects window.

Good to know

  • In a text box, lines wrap within its width and the letter size fits the box height. A single line uses the size from the tool options.
  • Inside the text use the arrow keys and Home/End (Shift = select, Ctrl/⌥ = by word) and Ctrl/⌘+A, C, X, V. Double-click = word, triple-click = everything.
  • One edit is one undo step.
  • Only the font name is saved in the project. Missing fonts and missing characters are drawn with Galmuri11, with a warning under the font field.
  • NeoDunggeunmo is 16px at size 0 and sharpest at multiples of 16.
  • The built-in fonts are all under free licenses (SIL OFL 1.1, MIT, BSD), so use them freely in art and games. The texts are in the app folder's Licenses/.

Fill

The fill tool (G) paints the area of the same or a similar color as the clicked pixel. Left-click uses the primary color, right-click the secondary color.

Fill settings

  • ① Tolerance 0–100% (default 0): 0 fills only the same color, higher values include similar colors, 100% fills everything. Colors are compared with the clicked pixel.
  • ② Compare by (default Max channel difference (default)): how the color difference is measured. The magic wand uses it too.
MethodWhat counts as the same color
Max channel difference (default)The largest R, G, B or alpha difference (100% = 255).
RGB distanceThe straight-line distance of the R, G and B differences (100% ≈ 441).
OKLab (perceptual)A distance close to what the eye sees (black to white = 100%).
CIEDE2000 (perceptual, precise)A more precise measure of visible difference (ΔE00, 100% = 120).
Hue onlyOnly the hue angle (100% = 180°). Use it to pick all light and dark shades of one color.
  • ③ Contiguous only (on by default): on fills only the connected area, off also fills separate matching pixels. Toggle it with Shift+G.
  • ④ Connect diagonally too (8 directions): when on also connects pixels that touch diagonally.
  • ⑤ Anchor: find the area from the Current layer (default) or All visible layers. Paint always goes on the current layer.
  • ⑥ The eyedropper has the same source setting (default: all visible layers).

How the settings change the result

With Contiguous only off, the same color in the other boxes is painted too.

10% allows up to 26 per channel, so bands 16 away are filled and bands 32 away are left.

The same tolerance fills different cells with each method. OKLab and CIEDE2000 also fill grays of similar lightness; Hue only fills the whole red and orange rows.

Good to know

  • With Right button = eraser on, right-click erases the area completely.
  • With a selection, only its inside is filled. With symmetry on, the mirrored spots are filled too.
  • On a normal layer you can also fill pixels outside the canvas. Empty space is not filled.
  • In tile mode, clicking outside the canvas fills the matching spot inside.
  • If the pixel already has the color and the tolerance is 0, nothing changes.

Gradient

The gradient tool (F) paints a linear gradient in the direction you drag. Save gradients you use often as presets.

Dragging and settings

Drag from the ① start point to the ② end point (Shift = 45° steps). It paints inside the selection, or the whole cel without one.

  • ③ Gradient bar: click the upper half for an opacity stop and the lower half for a color stop. Drag a stop to move it; Delete removes it.
  • Edit the selected stop's ④ position % and ⑤ color (or opacity %).
  • ⑥ Interpolation: smooth / steps. ⑦ Color space for smooth blending: RGB / HSL / OKLab / OKLCH (default).
  • ⑧ Dithering: none / 2×2 Bayer / 4×4 Bayer. Mixes with a pattern instead of semi-transparency.
  • ⑨ The i icon shows usage hints. The right button does not paint, and settings are kept for the next launch.

Steps uses only the stop colors, in bands. Dithering mixes just the two neighboring stop colors in a pattern, so the palette doesn't grow.

Presets

  • Open the menu with the down-arrow button at the right end of the bar or by right-clicking the bar. ⑩ Save current gradient… saves it, and picking a ⑪ preset applies it.
  • A preset stores the stops, interpolation, color space and dithering. Favorites come first.
  • The built-in presets are Rainbow, Repeating Rainbow, Sunset, Ocean, Transparent Fade, Fire, Forest and Grayscale. Restore deleted or edited ones on the Presets tab of Preferences.
  • ⑫ Manage Gradient Presets… opens the Gradients tab of the Presets window. ⑬ Pick a preset and ⑭ favorite, rename or delete it, or ⑮ edit it and press ⑯ Save changes.
  • ⑰ Asset Manager: opens the Asset Manager filtered to gradients.

Received gradients can't be renamed or deleted. Edit one and click Save to My Assets to save a copy to My Assets, leaving the original as is.

Eyedropper

The Eyedropper (I) picks a color from the canvas into the primary or secondary color. Use it to reuse a color already in the picture.

Picking colors

  • Left-click picks into the primary color, right-click into the secondary color. Drag to keep picking; the color where you release stays.
  • Source: All visible layers (default) picks the color you see; Current layer picks only from the current layer.
  • While a selection is being moved, its pixels are picked first.
  • Clicking outside the canvas picks from a normal layer's pixels outside the canvas.

Temporary eyedropper with Alt

Hold Alt in a drawing tool to use the eyedropper for a moment. Left-click sets the primary color, right-click the secondary; release to return.

  • Works in: Pencil, Gradient Pencil, Eraser, Fill, Gradient, Shape, Dodge/burn, Dithering and the Replace color brush.
  • It does not work in the text, selection, move, pivot and 9-slice tools.
  • It uses the eyedropper tool's Source setting.
  • An Alt used this way doesn't open the menu bar when released.

Dodge/burn

The Dodge/burn tool (D) rubs painted pixels a little lighter or darker. Use it to shade without picking new colors.

  • Left button = lighten, right button = darken. In Saturation mode they make colors more vivid or duller.
  • Each pixel changes once per stroke. Release and rub again to change it more.
  • Transparent pixels stay; semi-transparent pixels keep their alpha and only change color.
  • The brush, symmetry, the selection and Shift+click lines work as with the pencil.

Lighten/darken settings

  • ① Mode: how colors change (default Hue shift). See the table below.
  • ② Strength 1–50% (default 10%): how much one pass changes a pixel. Keep it low and rub several times for an even build-up.

Modes

ModeLeft buttonRight button
Hue shiftLightens and turns the hue a little toward yellowDarkens and turns the hue a little toward blue/purple. Near-grays only change lightness
Lightness onlyRaises only perceived lightness, keeping hue and saturationLowers only perceived lightness, keeping hue and saturation
HSV valueAdds HSV value; at full brightness it moves toward whiteSubtracts HSV value
Mix white/blackMixes with whiteMixes with black
SaturationRaises saturation for a more vivid colorLowers saturation for a duller color. Grays don't change

Hue shift turns highlights toward yellow and shadows toward blue/purple, for livelier shading. Mix white/black makes highlights pastel and chalky.

Good to know

  • One stroke is one undo step, named Lighten or Darken (Saturate or Desaturate in Saturation mode).
  • The mode and strength are kept for the next launch.

Dithering

The Dithering tool paints the pixels under the brush in the primary and secondary colors following a pattern. Use it to mix two colors into an in-between tone with dots.

  • The left button paints the pattern pixels primary and the rest secondary. The right button swaps them.
  • The pattern is fixed to canvas coordinates, so overlapping strokes always line up.
  • The brush, symmetry, the selection and Shift+click lines work as with the pencil. There is no default shortcut.

Patterns

  • ① Pattern: the share of the primary color. Checker (50%) (default) is half, Sparse (25%) one in four and Dense (75%) three in four.

Painting the three patterns side by side gives a four-step transition. For one drag, use the dithering of the gradient tool.

Where Chickxel uses dithering

Dithering shows in-between colors through the ratio of dots, so the palette doesn't grow. These features offer it too:

FeatureDithering setting
DitheringPaints three 2×2 patterns with the brush
Gradient · Gradient PencilDithering: None / 2×2 Bayer / 4×4 Bayer — mixes only the two neighboring stop colors
Convert to palette colorsNo dithering / Ordered dithering (Bayer 4×4) — mixes in a pattern while converting to palette colors
Pixelate AI imageDither in the palette step: None / Bayer 2×2 / Bayer 4×4

Replace color brush

The Replace color brush (C) repaints, where you rub, only the color of the pixel you first pressed. Other colors such as outlines stay.

How it decides

  • The color to replace is the one under the pointer when you press. It doesn't change during the stroke.
  • Only exactly the same color is replaced. For similar colors, use the tolerance of the fill tool or the magic wand.
  • The left button replaces with the primary color, the right button with the secondary color.
  • Starting on a transparent pixel paints only transparent pixels, filling the empty space behind the drawing.

What it shares with other brush tools

The brush, symmetry, the selection, Shift+click lines and the Alt+click eyedropper work as with the pencil. The undo history shows Color replace brush.

Compared with Replace color in the Edit menu

Replace color… in the Edit menu (Ctrl+Alt+R) changes one color across the whole cel or selection at once. This brush changes only where you rub.

Selection tools

All four selection tools make the same kind of selection. While a selection exists, edit commands and drawing apply only inside it.

ToolShortcutHow it selects
Rectangle selectNoneDrag to select a rectangle.
Ellipse selectNoneDrag to select the circle (ellipse) that fits the dragged rectangle.
Lasso selectQDraw an outline; on release the closed shape is selected.
Magic wandWSelects the area of similar color around the click (settings).

The four tools share one toolbar slot. Each press of M cycles rectangle → ellipse → lasso → magic wand.

Rectangle select and ellipse select

  • ① While you drag, a dotted border follows the area to select. Press Shift during the drag for a square or circle.
  • A plain click without dragging deselects.
  • Dragging inside the selection without keys (four-way arrow cursor) lifts and moves its pixels.

Lasso select

  • ② Hold the mouse button and draw an outline; the area to select shows as a blue dotted line. On release the outline closes and its inside is selected. Clicking and dragging inside work as with rectangle select.

Add to, subtract from or intersect a selection

The keys you hold when you press the mouse button (or click, with the magic wand) decide how the new area combines with the current selection.

Keys heldResult
NoneReplace: only the new area remains.
ShiftAdd: the new area joins the current selection.
AltSubtract: the new area is removed from the current selection.
Shift+AltIntersect: only the overlap remains.
  • The mode is set when you press, so you can release the keys afterwards. Rectangle and ellipse select show the result while you drag.
  • A click with the keys held leaves the selection unchanged.
  • Pressing inside the selection with the keys held starts a new area instead of moving. For example, Alt+drag inside cuts a hole.
  • If you started an Add with Shift, release it and press it again to lock a square or circle.

The color in the middle of the cross cursor shows which mode a press would use. It changes as soon as you press or release a key. With rectangle, ellipse or lasso select, it becomes a four-way arrow inside the selection.

Moving, copying, deleting and other work on a selection is in Working with a selection.

Magic wand

The Magic wand (W) selects the area of similar color around the clicked pixel in one click. It shares its settings with Fill.

  • ③ The selected area shows a dotted border, and the status bar shows its pixel count and size.
  • ④ The tool options are the same values as Fill's. A change in one tool applies to the other.
  • Hold Shift or Alt when you click to combine with the current selection (Selection tools).
  • Clicking an already selected pixel selects again from there. With a growing mode, Shift+click widens the selection step by step.
  • Dragging inside doesn't move the pixels. Use the Move tool (V) or the nudge keys (Ctrl+Shift+Left and so on).

Magic wand settings

  • ⑤ Select: what a click selects (table below).
  • ⑥ Preview (on by default) and its limit: shows the pixels a click would change (see Preview below).
SettingWhat it does
ToleranceSelects colors up to this far from the clicked color.
Compare byChooses how the color difference for the tolerance is measured.
Contiguous onlyOn: only the connected area. Off: matching colors anywhere on the canvas.
Connect diagonally too (8 directions)Pixels touching diagonally count as connected.
AnchorReads colors from the current layer or from all visible layers.

Select

ModeWhat it selects
Within tolerance (default)Only pixels within the tolerance of the clicked color.
Plus 1-pixel borderThat area plus a 1-pixel border, reaching outlines and shading edges.
Plus touching colorsThat area plus every other-colored area touching it. It grows one step only, never into transparent pixels.

In the two growing modes, Connect diagonally too (8 directions) adds diagonal neighbors too. It stays available even with Contiguous only off.

Preview

  • ⑦ Pixels that would stay selected aren't tinted; only their dotted border shows.
  • ⑧ On hover, pixels a click would add are tinted blue and pixels that would leave the selection red. Pressing or releasing Shift or Alt updates it at once.
  • The number box limits how many pixels the preview may find (default 300, 1–100000). Above it no preview shows, but a click still selects them all. A large limit can slow down big images.
  • Turn Preview off to hide it. It also hides while dragging or transforming, or while a floating selection exists.

Working with a selection

Use this when you want to move, copy, delete or flip only the part you selected. Without a selection, the same commands apply to the cel or the whole canvas.

Moving and deselecting

ActionResult
Drag inside the selection (rectangle, ellipse, lasso), or drag with the Move toolLifts the selected part and moves it. Until you drop it, it is a floating selection.
Ctrl+Shift+Left Ctrl+Shift+Right Up DownMoves it by 1px.
Shift+Left Shift+Right Shift+Up Shift+DownMoves it by 8px.
EnterDrops the floating selection where it is.
Right-click the canvas with a selection tool, Ctrl+D, EscDrops it and removes the selection.
Ctrl+ASelects the whole canvas.
  • Plain arrow keys change frames. After you click the canvas with the Move tool, plain arrows move pixels too.
  • Select all (Ctrl+A) from another tool switches to rectangle select.
  • Pasting switches to rectangle select (unless rectangle, ellipse or lasso select is active).
  • Pixels on a locked layer can't be moved.

Commands that use the selection

CommandWith a selectionWithout a selection
Cut Ctrl+XMoves the pixels to the clipboard.Does nothing.
Copy Ctrl+CCopies the pixels.Copies the whole visible image of the current frame.
Paste Ctrl+VPastes at the selection's top left.Pastes where it was copied.
Delete DeleteClears to transparent.Deletes the frames, layers or cels selected in the timeline.
Flip horizontal (selection/cel) Shift+H · Flip vertical (selection/cel) Shift+VFlips the selected part.Flips the current cel.
Rotate 90° (selection/canvas) Ctrl+R · Rotate -90° (selection/canvas) Ctrl+Shift+RRotates the selected part by 90°.Rotates the whole canvas.
Transform (rotate/size)… Ctrl+TOpens the selection in transform mode.Opens the whole drawing of the current cel.
Add outline (primary color) Ctrl+Alt+OAdds a 1px outline around the drawing inside the selection.Adds it across the whole current cel.
Drawing tools (pencil, fill, shapes and so on)Paint only inside the selection.Paint anywhere.

Good to know

  • Pixels copied in the app paste at the same spot, even on another frame or layer.
  • A frame image copy pastes at (0,0), and images from other programs at the top left of the visible canvas.
  • On a layer with its own size, select all and copying without a selection cover only that layer's area.
  • Copying on the canvas clears cels or frames copied in the timeline, so pasting after clicking the timeline still pastes these pixels.
  • With a selection, delete clears only the selected part, even after you click the timeline.
  • With a selection, the Replace color… window offers Selection only.

Move tool and pixels outside the canvas

Use it when you want to move the drawing or a selected part somewhere else. The Move tool (V) moves the selection, or the whole current cel when nothing is selected.

Moving

ActionResult
Drag on the canvasMoves the selection or the whole cel. On a normal layer the pivot moves along.
Right-drag without a selectionMoves only the drawing; the pivot stays (also in the Ctrl temporary Move tool).
Arrow keys / Shift+arrow keysMoves by 1px / 8px. Each press is one undo step.
Hold Ctrl (⌘ on macOS) and drag while using another toolThe Move tool is active only while the key is held.
Select several cels in the timeline, then drag or use the arrow keysWithout a selection, moves every selected cel by the same amount.
  • On a text layer it moves the text (Text).
  • Locked layers don't move. When moving several cels, empty cels and empty layers that arrange children are skipped too.
  • The Ctrl temporary Move tool doesn't work in transform or canvas size mode, while typing, or on a tilemap or the empty layer view.

Cycle

Turn on Cycle when you want to line up a seamless pattern (tile). Pixels pushed off one side come back on the other.

  • Turn it on in Tool options or Tools menu Drawing options → Move: wrap around (off by default).
  • With it on, no pixels are kept outside the canvas.
  • A layer with its own size wraps within its own area.
  • Moving a selection never wraps.

Pixels outside the canvas

Pixels pushed off the canvas aren't deleted; they are kept up to 4096px around it. These are pixels outside the canvas, and they return when you move them back in. They appear when you:

  • drag or nudge with the Move tool
  • drop a selection or a pasted image outside the canvas
  • place an image larger than the canvas or sticking out of it (dropping it on the canvas, or Apply in the pixelate window)
  • shrink the canvas size (enlarge it again to get them back)
  • duplicate a layer or frame, or merge down (they are copied or merged too)

With the Move tool selected, pixels outside the canvas show faded (40%). Use Outside view on the top bar to show them opaque or hide them.

  • Exports, the preview and effects use only the inside of the canvas.
  • Pixels outside the canvas are saved in the project file. Older versions of Chickxel don't read them.

Drawing outside the canvas

When you want to fix drawing that sticks out past the canvas, paint, erase and pick colors on it directly. This works on normal layers.

  • Tools that work there: Pencil, Gradient Pencil, Shape Pen, Eraser, Shape, Dodge/burn, Dithering, Replace color brush, Fill, Eyedropper
  • You can draw up to 4096px around the canvas; it is one undo step together with the inside.
  • Fill clicked outside the canvas fills only outside the canvas.
  • With a selection or in tile mode, only the inside of the canvas is painted. Other layers' pixels outside the canvas aren't shown.

Transform mode (current cel)

Rotate, scale, move and distort the current cel's selection (or all its pixels) with handles. Open it with Ctrl+T or Transform (rotate/size)… in the Edit menu.

Until you apply, every drag redraws from the original, so pixels don't get mushy.

Controls

ActionResult
① Drag inside, arrow keysMove (Shift+arrow keys = 8px).
② Drag a square handleResize (Shift = keep ratio, Alt = from center). Dragging past the opposite side flips.
③ Drag just outside the borderRotate around the rotation center (Shift = 15° steps).
④ Drag / double-click the rotation centerMove the rotation center in half-pixel steps / reset it to the middle of the box.
Enter or ⑤ ApplyApply (one undo step)
Esc, Ctrl+Z or ⑥ CancelBack to before the transform
  • Pressing or releasing Ctrl, Alt or Shift mid-drag takes effect at once.
  • The rotation center starts at the current layer's pivot in this frame. Moving it doesn't change the pivot.
  • Switching tools, saving or other work applies it first. The preview is shown without layer effects.
  • It doesn't open in the view of an empty layer that arranges children, on a locked layer or on an empty cel. Opening it in canvas size mode applies the canvas size first.

Entering numbers

  • ⑦ In Transform · current cel in Tool options, enter the angle (−180 to 180°, clockwise), width/height % (−100% = flip) and center X/Y (the canvas position of the image's middle).
  • ⑧ Box width × height is the margin: it changes only the box size and leaves the drawing (top left fixed; chain = keep ratio).
  • ⑨ The Flip buttons flip the box horizontally or vertically. The change happens when you apply.

Resampling

⑩ Resampling is how rotated or scaled pixels take their color from the original. Neither method blends colors, so no new colors appear.

MethodResult
RotSpriteThe default. Diagonal lines and corners break less, and 1-pixel lines split less often.
NearestUses the nearest original pixel as is. Rotated lines can break into steps or get thicker.
  • If the original is larger than 512×512px, Nearest is used instead and Tool options says so.
  • Without free distort, 0°, 90°, 180° and 270° rotations and plain scaling look the same with both.

Free distort and right-click

ActionResult
⑪ Ctrl (⌘ on macOS)+drag a handleFree distort: a corner handle moves its corner and a side handle its whole side, making a trapezoid or other quadrilateral.
Right-click inside without draggingOpens the ⑫ menu: Rotate 180°, Rotate 90° clockwise, Rotate 90° counterclockwise, Flip horizontal and Flip vertical.
Right-drag insideMoves only the drawing. The rotation center and the pivot stay put.
Right-drag just outside the borderRotates around the middle of the box. The rotation center turns with the drawing.
Right-drag a handleMargin: changes only the box and leaves the drawing. Narrowing crops what falls outside.
Ctrl+right-drag a handleMoves the margin box's corners freely. Everything outside that shape is cropped.
  • Add Alt to a free distort and the opposite corner or side moves the opposite way. Twisted or dented shapes aren't accepted.
  • Widening the margin repeats a selection's edge pixels, or adds empty space without a selection. Parts to be cropped are previewed faded.
  • Dragging the margin without Ctrl, or changing the ⑧ box numbers, returns to a rectangular margin.

What changes

Transform mode changes only the current cel. Move several cels with the Move tool, and change every frame with Transform whole layer.

CaseWhat changes
With a selectionOnly the selected pixels change; the pivot stays. The selection keeps the new shape.
Without a selectionAll pixels change, and that frame's pivot follows the drawing. Moves made by right-dragging inside leave it in place.
Layer with its own sizeThe layer area grows if the drawing goes past it. Moving alone, without a selection, moves that frame's layer position (Position per frame). Rotating or distorting removes the 9-slice borders.

Canvas size mode

Use it when you want to enlarge or shrink the canvas, or change where the image sits in it. Open it with Ctrl+Alt+C or Canvas size… in the Edit menu.

As you drag the handles, ① the light-blue dotted line shows the new canvas and ② parts that will be cut off are faded. It applies to all frames and layers.

Controls

ActionResult
③ Drag a handleStretches or shrinks the image along with the canvas.
③ Right-drag a handleAdds margin or crops; the image keeps its size.
Shift while draggingKeeps the ratio.
Alt while draggingChanges both sides around the center.
④ Drag inside the image, arrow keysMoves the image within the canvas (Shift+arrow keys = 8px).
Enter or ⑤ OKApplies the change (one undo step).
Esc, Ctrl+Z or ⑥ CancelExits without changes.
⑮ Flip horizontal / vertical buttonsFlips the whole canvas at once. The mode continues.

Right-drag margin and Ctrl corners

  • ㉑ While you right-drag, the border turns yellow and the handles become outward arrows.
  • ㉒ Ctrl (⌘ on macOS)+right-drag moves just one corner (both ends for a side handle) to crop at a slant. Everything outside that shape is cut off.
  • Ctrl+drag without the right button slants the image to that shape (perspective).
  • Add Alt and the opposite corner moves the opposite way.
  • The new canvas is the rectangle around the shape; empty space is transparent.
  • Slanted pixels take their colors by the transform mode resampling setting.
  • Slanting removes the 9-slice borders.

Tool options

SettingWhat it does
⑦ Canvas width × heightThe new canvas size. Turn on the chain at the end to keep the ratio.
⑧ Anchor at canvas centerWhen on, both opposite edges move together.
⑨ Stretch with 9-slice · middle fillStretches the image by its 9-slice borders (below).
⑩ Image width × height · ⑪ chainThe image size. The ⑪ chain starts on and keeps the original ratio.
⑫ Aspect ratioOpens the Aspect ratio window.
⑬ Image position X/Y · ⑭ 9-direction anchorWhere the image sits. An anchor button snaps it to that spot (center by default).
Fit all artworkFits the smallest canvas that holds all artwork. The artwork isn't stretched.
  • Changing a number moves the handles too.
  • Fit also counts pixels outside the canvas and hidden layers.

Stretch with 9-slice

  • Use it when you want to stretch only the middle and keep the corners, as for buttons and panels. When there are 9-slice borders, ⑨ is on by default.
  • Middle fill: Stretch stretches the middle; Tile repeats its pattern.
  • While dragging, the borders show as green dotted lines. The image can't get smaller than both borders.
  • After applying, the borders stay the same distance from the edges, so they still match in Unity.

What applying does

  • Layer positions are adjusted too.
  • Parts cut off by shrinking are kept as pixels outside the canvas and come back when you enlarge it again.
  • Stretching or flipping the image changes pixels outside the canvas too.
  • Pivots move with the image. During the mode you see where they will land.

Resizing by cells on a tilemap layer

On a tilemap layer the mode shows a cell grid, and handles snap to cell boundaries. Only the canvas changes; tiles aren't stretched. Numbers are still in pixels.

Layer with its own size: Layer size

Use it when you want to resize just one layer with its own size. With that layer selected (Drawing), Ctrl+Alt+C opens Layer size mode.

The controls are the same and it applies to every frame and skin; the layer position and pivot follow the art. Flip and Ctrl corner drags aren't available.

Aspect ratio window

Fit the canvas to a ratio such as 1:1 or 16:9. Open it with the Aspect ratio button in the canvas size mode Tool options.

  • ⑯ Ratio: pick 1:1, 4:3, 3:4, 3:2, 2:3, 16:9 or 9:16, or type width:height (1–100 each).
  • ⑰ Keep: Keep width keeps the width and sets the height from the ratio; Keep height does the opposite.
  • ⑱ Method: Canvas only leaves the image and adds or crops space on the changing side at the anchor (cropped pixels stay outside the canvas). Stretch image fits the image to the new canvas and turns the image size chain off if the ratio changes.
  • ⑲ The preview shows the current canvas (dotted), the new canvas (accent border) and where the image goes. Parts leaving the new canvas are faded.
  • ⑳ Below it you see the new canvas size and the image size. If the size is outside 1px to the maximum, a warning appears and OK is disabled.

OK only sets the canvas size mode values. The canvas changes when you apply the mode (Enter); cancelling (Esc) reverts it. Your choices are remembered until you quit.

Transform whole layer

Rotate, scale, move and distort every frame and skin of the current layer together with one orange box. Open it with Transform whole layer… in the Edit menu (Alt+Shift+C).

The handles work as in transform mode, and the canvas size stays the same. Applying is one undo step.

Controls

ActionResult
Drag inside, arrow keysMoves the drawing of every frame together (Shift+arrow keys = 8px).
① Drag a square handleResize (Shift = keep ratio, Alt = from center, Ctrl = free distort).
Drag just outside the borderRotate (Shift = 15° steps). Each frame turns around its own pivot.
Drag the rotation centerMoves the rotation center of every frame by the same amount.
Right-click, right-dragSame as in transform mode (menu, moving only the drawing, rotating around the box middle, ① handle margins).
Click another frame in the timelineOn the same layer the mode continues and shows that frame. Selecting another layer cancels without applying.
Enter / EscApply / cancel
  • The box starts at the selection, at the layer's area for a layer with its own size, or else around the drawing of all frames (including pixels outside the canvas).
  • It doesn't open for an empty layer that arranges children, a locked layer or a layer with nothing drawn (the status bar says why).
  • ② Tool options show Transform · whole layer with the same values as transform mode. ③ Flip flips the box when you apply.
  • Changing a layer with its own size and 9-slice borders, without a selection, adds a Stretch with 9-slice row (as in canvas size mode).
  • The preview is shown without layer effects, and parts going outside the canvas are faded.

What applying does

CaseWhat changes
With a selectionOnly the pixels in that area change in every frame, and the pivots stay. The selection keeps the new shape.
Normal layer (no selection)The drawing of every frame (including pixels outside the canvas) and the layer's pivots all follow. If you narrowed the box, pixels outside it are removed.
Layer with its own size: resized or rotatedThe layer area follows the box, for every skin and linked cel. Stretching without rotating follows the 9-slice setting; rotating or distorting removes the 9-slice borders.
Layer with its own size: only movedThe layer's base position moves instead of the drawing. Per-frame differences are kept, and the pivot and child layers move along.
  • Moves made by right-dragging inside leave the pivots in place. Other layers' pivots and the canvas pivot don't change.
  • To move just one frame's position, move it in that frame with transform mode and no selection (Position per frame).
  • Moving a layer with its own size with the Move tool only moves the pixels in the cel; the layer position and pivot don't change.

Clean up lines

Clean up lines… in the Edit menu finds the lines in the current cel and redraws them as clean 1px lines and arcs with even steps. Use it to tidy wobbly or thick lines in one go.

  • Sharp corners are kept. With a selection, only the lines inside it are cleaned up.
  • ⑭ When the layer has drawings in two or more frames, All frames (N) appears. Turn it on to clean every frame with the same settings (one undo step; left-out strokes apply to the current frame only).
  • On an empty layer that arranges children, a tilemap or a locked layer, or with an empty cel, the menu item is disabled. Hover it to see why.

Preview

① The current picture and ② the cleaned one appear side by side, with the strokes found drawn over the current one. ③ Blue = straight pieces, pink = arcs, yellow dots = kept corners. ④ shows the strokes found and pixels that will change, updated when you change a setting.

ActionResult
Hover a strokeHighlights the stroke in both pictures.
Click a strokeLeaves the stroke out so it stays as is (dashed). Click again to put it back.
Wheel / drag / double-clickZoom / pan both pictures / fit to view

Find lines

Decides which pixels count as lines. Only thin patches of line color count, and lines of different colors are separate strokes even where they touch.

ItemWhat it does
⑤ Dark lines automatically · DarknessWhen on (default), colors at or below the Darkness lightness (default 35%) count as lines. To pick only light lines, turn it off and add their colors.
⑥ Line colorsColors that count as lines even if not dark. Turn on the eyedropper and click either picture to add one; remove it with the button on its chip.
⑦ Color toleranceColors this close (default 6%) to a line color count as the same line. Raise it if line edges mix in similar colors.
⑧ Max thicknessOnly patches with an average thickness up to this (default 2.5px) count as lines. Wide dark areas are left alone.
⑨ Min lengthPieces shorter than this (default 3px), such as eyes, dots and small patterns, are not touched.

Smoothing

ItemWhat it does
⑩ Straightening toleranceA line that wobbles within this distance (default 0.6px) becomes a straight line or arc. Higher straightens more.
⑪ Corner angleTurns sharper than this angle (default 40°) stay as corners. Lower keeps more corners.

New line color

ItemWhat it does
⑫ KeepThe default. Each new line uses its stroke's color (the most common color in the stroke).
⑫ Darken inner color · ⑬ DarkenEach new line pixel gets the inner neighbor color, darkened by Darken (default 50%). Use it to turn black outlines into darker shades of the fill colors.

Where old thick line pixels are removed, the spot becomes transparent outside the drawing and takes the surrounding color inside.

Applying and defaults

  • ⑮ Apply: replaces the picture with the cleaned one, and the status bar shows how many pixels changed. Undo once to revert.
  • ⑯ Defaults: puts every setting back to its starting value. Applied settings are remembered until you close the app.

To draw new lines one at a time as clean lines like these, use the Curve Pen.

Pivot tool

The pivot is the point an image turns or flips around. Use the Pivot tool (K) when you want to set the rotation center or the Unity sprite's reference point.

Every layer has its own pivot, and exports use the canvas pivot.

Moving the pivot

  • ① Drag on the canvas and the pivot mark (a circle with a cross) follows; on release it is set, snapped to half pixels (one undo step).
  • ② The 5×5 buttons jump to spots such as the edges, quarters and the middle. For odd sizes the middle rounds down (49 → 24).
  • ③ X is measured in pixels from the left and Y from the bottom (as in Unity, in 0.5px steps).
  • To see the pivot with other tools, turn on View menu Show pivot (Pivot on the top bar).
  • Other layers' pivots then show as small labeled dots too (Canvas).
  • When an effect such as shake moves the image, the pivot mark moves with it; the original spot shows as a small circle and dotted line.

Target

⑤ The pivot you are changing is named at the top of the group. Normally it is the layer selected in the timeline. To change the canvas pivot, click the Canvas W×H text at the timeline's top left.

KindPivot that changes
Normal layerThat layer's pivot
Layer with its own sizeThat layer's pivot, measured from its own area (used by the layer atlas)
Empty layer that arranges childrenThat layer's pivot. Set it only with the ② buttons and ③ numbers (dragging moves the children).
Canvas pivotThe reference point for sheet and Unity exports
