  • To match the canvas pivot to one layer's pivot, pick a Representative. It then follows that layer's pivot in every frame.
  • The representative layer gets a crown before its name in the timeline.
  • You can also set it from the layer's right-click menu.

From current frame on

④ Turn it on when the pivot must change partway, as in a walk cycle. The current frame through the last get their own pivot (and position, for a layer with its own size).

  • Changing the pivot then shifts this and later frames by the same amount. Earlier frames stay.
  • Turning it off removes the separate pivots and goes back to the default pivot.
  • ⑥ Cels with their own pivot show an orange diamond at their top left.
  • To change just one frame, use the frame header's right-click menu (Use a separate pivot for this frame only / Reset pivot to default).
  • Applying a transform without a selection, or permanently applying an effect with Move pivot along on to a layer with its own size, also creates separate pivots.

Where pivots are used

Check this when you want to know what a pivot change affects.

PivotUsed for
Canvas pivot (or the representative layer's pivot)The sprite reference point in Unity exports and sheets
Layer pivotThe starting rotation center of a transform and Transform whole layer, Around pivot in symmetry drawing, and that layer's layer effects (including scripts)
  • Canvas size mode and flipping or rotating the canvas move every pivot with the image.
  • Transform whole layer moves that layer's pivots.
  • A transform without a selection and a Move tool drag move that frame's pivot (not a right-drag).

Older .chickxel files without layer pivots open and look the same. Once saved, older versions can't open them.

9-slice

The 9-slice tool (S) sets the Unity Sprite Border. When stretched, corners and edges keep their shape and only the middle grows, which suits UI buttons and panels (Image Type = Sliced).

Setting the borders

  • ① Drag the four green lines; they snap to the nearest pixel boundary. A line can't cross the opposite one, and each release is one undo step.
  • ② L, T, R and B are pixels measured from the image edges. You can also type them.
  • ③ All 0 clears the borders.

Target

④ With a layer with its own size selected it applies to that layer; otherwise to every cell of the frame sheet. In the view of an empty layer that arranges children, each child shows its own green lines to change.

When stretched

Stretching without borders thickens the corners and border lines too, while 9-slice grows only the middle. Inside Chickxel you can stretch the same way with Stretch with 9-slice in canvas size mode.

Files and saving

This chapter covers opening files, keeping your work safe while you edit, and saving. Everything you edit lives in a single project file (.chickxel). For exporting outputs, see the Export and sharing chapter.

TaskPages
OpenOpening files, Opening PNGs, Opening images, animations and videos, Dropping images onto the canvas, Importing 3D models
While you editAutosave, Undo history that survives closing, Revert to saved
SaveSaving, File types and where they go, Export cell names, Cleaning up old outputs
CloseSave prompt when closing
Settings and problemsPreferences, When errors occur

File types and where they go

Read this to find out which files are made and where. You edit the project file (.chickxel); the other files are rebuilt from it when you save.

Files that are made

Unless you change the name or folder, everything is made next to the project and named after it. The example below is for saving hero.chickxel.

FileDefault nameMade when
Project filehero.chickxelEvery save. Layers, frames, effects and export settings are all stored here.
Frame sheethero.pngThe sheet row is checked for saving (the default)
Layer atlashero_parts.pngThere is a layer tree and the layer atlas is on
Unity settings fileshero.png.meta, hero_parts.png.metaThe game engine is Unity
Sprite LibraryOne default plus one per skin presetUnity, with Sprite Library on
Unity prefab and animationshero.prefab, hero_idle.anim, hero.controllerUnity, with Prefab or Animation on
Godot 4hero.tres, hero.tscnThe game engine is Godot 4
JSONhero.jsonThe game engine is JSON
GIF, APNG and frame PNGshero_idle.gifThat row is checked for saving
Asset package.chickxel-assetYou export assets in the Asset Manager. Double-click it to import.

Splitting per tag, skin or layer makes several files with that name added. Change names and folders in the Export dialog.

Hiding export paths

Export folders and names are saved so only this computer can read them (export path encryption), so sharing a .chickxel doesn't reveal your folders. On another computer, or after the settings folder is deleted, they go back to the defaults (the project folder and name).

Older and newer files

  • Files saved by this version can't be opened by older versions of Chickxel.
  • Older files are converted without asking and look the same. Groups become empty parent layers with the same name (Importing old files), and pivots carry over (Pivot tool).
  • A file from a newer Chickxel isn't opened; you're told so. Update the app.
  • Older projects from before the game engine choice open with Unity if they were writing Unity files, otherwise with None.

Good to know

  • Opening an exported PNG opens the project that made it (Opening PNGs).
  • Pixels pushed outside the canvas and script effects are saved in the project file too.
  • Effects this version doesn't know aren't drawn, but saving doesn't remove them. .gpixel files from the former name (Gpixel) open too.
  • Undo history, the app trash and preferences are in the settings folder, not in the project file.

Opening files

Read this when you want to open a project, picture or video to edit it. Each file opens in its own document tab; a file that is already open switches to its tab.

Ways to open

MethodHow
File menuFile > Open (.chickxel · image · GIF · video)… (Ctrl+O). Pick several files to open a tab for each.
Recent filesPick from File > Recent files or the start screen (up to 10; Clear list empties it). Folders are in Recent folders.
File browserClick a file in the file browser on the left. Choose its folder with File > Open folder… or Open Unity project….
Dropping on the windowDrop files outside the canvas (file list, start screen, tab strip, panels) to open them in new tabs. Images dropped on the canvas become layers.
Explorer · FinderDouble-click a .chickxel file (see below).

What each file type opens as

FileOpens as
.chickxel, .gpixelThat project.
PNGThe project that made the PNG, otherwise a new imported project (Opening (importing) PNGs).
JPG, BMP, GIF, WebP, ICO, animated PNGA new document with one frame per image frame (Opening images, animations and videos).
Videos (MP4, WebM, MOV, …), AVIF, HEICTurned into frames in a new document (needs ffmpeg). Videos first show the Import video window.
3D models (GLB, glTF, OBJ, FBX)Set the angle, light and frames in the Import 3D model window; the drawn frames become a new document (Opening 3D models).

Opening by double-clicking in Explorer or Finder

Double-clicking a .chickxel opens it in Chickxel, and a .chickxel-asset imports the assets inside. File associations work like this on each OS.

OSFile association
WindowsAssociated once you start the app (no administrator rights needed). If you move the exe, start it once again.
macOSAssociated once Chickxel.app is in the Applications folder.
LinuxNone yet. Open files with File > Open or by dropping them.
  • If the app is already running, the file opens as a tab in that window.
  • If another app opens .chickxel files, right-click the file and choose Chickxel under Open with (Windows) or Open With (macOS).

Previews in Windows Explorer

In Explorer's Medium icons, Large icons and Extra large icons views, a .chickxel shows its first frame. An older file that shows the app icon gets its picture after one more save.

Files changed outside the app

When another program (a sync app, another Chickxel window, …) changes an open file, Chickxel does this.

CaseWhat happens
The document you are viewingAsks whether to reload. With No the document stays as is, and saving afterwards overwrites the changed file.
Another tab without unsaved editsReread when you switch to that tab.
Another tab with unsaved editsKeeps your edits and notes it in the status bar. Saving overwrites the changed file.

Opening (importing) PNGs

Opening a PNG opens the project that made it (such as a .chickxel with the same name in the same folder). Otherwise the PNG is imported as a new project.

Saving an imported PNG

An imported project saves to a .chickxel with the same name next to the PNG (hero.png → hero.chickxel). The first Ctrl+S saves there without asking.

Saving rewrites the original PNG as the project's output and keeps the guid of its existing .meta. There is no autosave until the first save.

Sheets with cell information

  • If the .meta next to the PNG (Unity Multiple) has cells of different sizes, it's imported as child layers: one empty layer named after the file, with a child layer per cell (keeping name, size, pivot and border).
  • If there are two or more cells of the same size, you're asked to choose ① As child layers or ② As animation frames.
  • Importing as animation frames makes one frame per cell, and each cell's name becomes that frame's export cell name. Frames are aligned on each cell's pivot.

Splitting sheets without cell information

Use ③ Frame·Layer > Split into frames by grid: ④ pick the method and split by ⑤ cell size, ⑥ cell count or ⑦ automatically (by blobs).

Opening images, animations and videos

Use this to bring a picture such as a GIF or JPG, or a video, in as frames you can edit. It opens as a new document, and the first save asks where to put it.

Images and animations

Pick a file with ① File > Open (.chickxel · image · GIF · video)… (Ctrl+O) and ② a new document opens in a new tab. The canvas is the image size, with ③ one layer named after the file and ④ one frame per image frame.

  • Frame durations and transparency come from the file (0–10 ms becomes 100 ms). Photos are turned the way they were taken.
  • Files with more than 300 frames ask ⑤ "Open all / First 300 only / Cancel".

Videos and ffmpeg

To open videos (MP4, WebM, MOV, …) and AVIF/HEIC pictures, install the free program ffmpeg (ffmpeg.org, or winget install Gyan.FFmpeg on Windows). If it isn't found automatically, choose ⑦ its file in ⑥ Edit > Preferences… > Video (ffmpeg) with ⑧ Browse….

Opening a video first shows the Import video window. Set ⑨ the range, ⑩ frames per second (at most 12), ⑪ max frames (default 120), ⑫ size and ⑬ Smooth, check ⑭ the estimated size and memory, then press ⑮ Import.

More frames use more memory. Above an estimated 1 GB a warning appears; reduce the size or the number of frames.

Smooth on and off

Smooth applies only when shrinking with Fit long side. Turn it on for live-action video and off for recordings of pixel art, so colors don't blend.

Dropping images onto the canvas

Drag images (PNG, JPG, GIF, WebP, …) or videos onto the canvas and each file becomes a new layer. While dragging, ① a dashed rectangle shows where it will go.

Name and order of the new layers

  • ② The new layer is added above the current layer, named after the file. Several files stack in drop order; with a layer inside a layer tree selected, they go above its topmost parent.
  • The whole drop is a single undo step (Drop image).

Placement and the area outside the canvas

The image is placed with the pixel you dropped on at its center, in the current frame. Parts past the canvas edge aren't cut off, and an image larger than the canvas is centered (enlarge it in Canvas size mode to see it).

Animated GIF, WebP and APNG files and videos go into one layer starting at the current frame, adding frames at the end if needed.

Transforming right after the drop

A single-frame image goes straight into ③ transform mode (④ the status bar explains). Move or resize it and press Enter to apply, or Esc to keep it as inserted. Multi-frame images are inserted without transform mode.

Dropping outside the canvas

Dropping outside the canvas (file list, start screen, tab strip, panels), or dropping a .chickxel, opens the file in a new tab instead of adding a layer.

Saving

Press Ctrl+S (① File > Save (+ export sheets)) to keep your work in a file. The export rows checked for saving are made too, and ② the status bar shows the result.

What a save does

  1. The .chickxel is written (first to a temporary ….chickxel-tmp file, which then replaces the original).
  2. Every export row checked for saving is made (files that are made). If none is checked, the status bar adds (export off).
  3. Old outputs no longer made are moved to the app trash (Cleaning up old outputs).
  4. The status bar shows the saved file and the exported sheets and cell counts, and the file is added to recent files and undo history.

The name on first save

The first save of a new project suggests its temporary title as the name (sprite if there is none). If the name is taken, a free name such as sprite_1 is used.

Save as

After Ctrl+Shift+S (File > Save as…), later saves go to the new file. The original file and its outputs stay, and outputs with the default name are made under the new name.

Exporting without saving

Export in the Export dialog writes only the selected row without saving the .chickxel. A never-saved document asks for a save location first.

If saving fails

If the .chickxel was saved but only the outputs failed, ③ you're told so, and the project counts as saved.

MessageMeaning and what to do
Couldn't saveThe .chickxel couldn't be written. Remove the cause and save again, or save under another name.
The file is read-onlyTurn off read-only in the file's properties, or save under another name.
Another program has the file openClose that program (image viewer, game engine, …) or save under another name.
Export file names collideThe project is saved and only the export fails. Make the sheet, tag, layer or skin names, or the names and folders of list rows, differ.

When saving takes a while

If saving or opening takes a while, a progress box covers the whole window until it finishes; drawing, shortcuts and file drops are ignored meanwhile. Closing the window then closes it after the save.

Autosave

So you don't lose work when you forget to save, Chickxel saves by itself 3 seconds after you stop editing (on by default). Like Ctrl+S it exports too, and ① the status bar shows Autosaved.

When it saves and when it doesn't

  • It waits while you're drawing, dragging, transforming or while the AI is working.
  • Only files with a .chickxel on disk are saved. Save new projects and imported pictures yourself once.
  • It saves in the background, so you can keep drawing meanwhile. Only very large documents (over about 768 MB of pixels) show a progress box.
  • It pauses while a dialog asks whether to reload, save or revert.
  • If it fails, the status bar shows Autosave (file name) — with the reason. Saving yourself shows the failure dialog with details (Saving).

Turning it on/off and the wait time

  • ② Turn it on/off and change ③ the wait time (1–600 seconds) in Preferences > General.

Revert to saved

Use this to throw away all edits and go back to the last saved state. Click ① the button at the bottom of the toolbar or ② File > Revert to saved, then confirm with ③ Yes.

Good to know

  • Reverting can't be undone.
  • It is enabled only when there are unsaved edits and a file to go back to.
  • An imported PNG not saved yet rereads that PNG.
  • The current layer, frame and zoom stay. There is no default shortcut; assign one in Customizing shortcuts.

Undo history that survives closing

Undo and redo carry on after you close a file or quit and reopen it (on by default). History is kept only up to the saved state.

① Turning it on/off, ② retention period (default 30 days), ③ maximum size (default 500 MB) and ④ Clear all history are in Preferences > General.

Up to Undo steps steps are kept, and opening the file shows the number of steps carried over. Changes discarded with "Don't save" aren't kept.

When history is discarded

  • The file is missing or was changed outside the app
  • The file wasn't opened or saved within the retention period (0 days = unlimited)
  • All history exceeds the max size — starting with files not opened for the longest
  • The file was deleted in the file browser (restoring it doesn't bring the history back)
  • You press Clear all history (the current undo of open files stays)

History is looked up by file path. Renaming in Chickxel's file browser carries it along; renaming in Explorer or Finder breaks the link.

Export cell names

Every cell (Unity sprite) in an exported sheet gets a name. Set one yourself with ① Export cell name… in the frame header's right-click menu and ② type it; leave it empty to use the rule.

Naming rules

Names are decided in this order, counting from 0. The rule (Frame number or By tag) is chosen under Cell naming rule in Sheet settings.

CaseCell name
The frame has an export cell nameThat name
The rule is By tag and the frame is inside a tag{Tag}_{number within the tag} (e.g. run_0, run_1)
Otherwise{sheet file name}_{frame number} (e.g. hero_0)

/ and \ become _, and an empty name becomes sprite. Duplicate names within one sheet get _1, _2, … appended.

Layer atlas cells are named after layers, tags and skins (Export: frame sheet and layer atlas).

Cell names in Unity

Cell names become sprite names in the meta file. Renaming sprites used in prefabs or animations can break references, so changing the rule shows a warning. A renamed cell keeps the ID of the old cell at the same position.

Cleaning up old outputs and renaming files

A project records the outputs it exported. That record is used to clear old outputs and to move outputs along when you rename it. Cleared files can be restored from the app trash.

Clearing old outputs on save

Old outputs (and their .meta) no longer made after a method or name change move to the app trash on the next save or export, and ① the status bar says so. Outputs of rows still in the list, and files you kept when removing a row, aren't cleared.

Only files this project recorded as its exports are cleared; files you made yourself are never touched. Save as starts a new record.

Renaming or deleting in the file browser

Renaming or deleting a .chickxel in the file browser also moves its default-named outputs and .meta files, or sends them to the app trash (hero_walk.png → knight_walk.png). Unity guids are kept.

Outputs with a custom folder or name stay in place. To delete only the project and keep its outputs, use Delete .chickxel files only in the right-click menu (File browser).

Restoring

In the file list, ② Ctrl+Z restores the last trashed item. For older ones, open ③ View app trash…, pick ④ the entry and press ⑤ Restore (not possible if a file with the same name is already there).

Save prompt when closing

Closing a document with unsaved edits asks whether to save. When you close the window, quit, close all tabs or install an update, ① unsaved files are gathered in a single dialog.

  • ② Save selected files: saves the checked files (including export) and closes. If any save fails, nothing closes.
  • ③ Don't save: closes without saving. Discarded edits aren't kept in undo history either.
  • ④ Cancel: nothing closes.

How each case asks

How you closeHow it asks
Closing one tab (Ctrl+W, the tab's X, middle click)The same dialog with just that file.
Close other tabs (tab right-click)Asks for each unsaved tab in turn; Cancel stops there.
Closing the window, quitting, installing an updateGathers all unsaved files in one dialog.

In the list, never-saved new documents show Choose a save location, and imported PNGs show the file that will be created. Tabs open when you quit reopen at the next start (Document tabs and split view).

Preferences

Change app-wide settings in ① Edit > Preferences… (Ctrl+,). Per-project settings such as export live in the project file.

General tab

The ② General tab has these groups.

GroupWhat it does
LanguageDisplay language and language CSVs (Changing the display language).
③ Undo stepsUndo steps per document (default 1000, max 10000). Higher values use more memory.
④ Undo historyUndo history that survives closing.
⑤ SaveAutosave on/off and wait time.
⑥ App trash retentionThe app trash time limit (off by default) and size limit (default 1 GB) (File browser).
UpdatesCheck for a new version at startup (Updates).
⑦ DiscordShow activity on Discord ("Playing Chickxel") (on by default): your Discord profile shows "Playing Chickxel". Nothing about your work is sent (Privacy).
Reset settingsReset settings…: returns app settings and shortcuts to their defaults and the language to your computer's. Recent files, window layout, palettes, your presets and accounts stay.

Shortcuts tab

Change the keys of commands and tools in the ⑧ Shortcuts tab (Customizing shortcuts).

  • ⑨ Change, Add, None, Default: edit the selected command's keys.
  • ⑩ Search: find by command name or key.
  • ⑪ Export / Import (JSON): save or load shortcuts as a file.
  • ⑫ Assigning a key another command uses asks whether to remove it there.

Other tabs

TabWhat it does
AI Status⑬ AI status and settings (Install and log in). The Notifications group below sets whether ⑭ you're notified when the AI finishes or asks, ⑮ with which sound, and ⑯ even while you're looking at the chat window (Answering the AI's questions).
AI referencesThe AI reference folder (References: teach the AI your style).
PresetsRestore auto optimize presets restores deleted or edited built-in presets, and Restore default gradients reinstalls deleted default gradients. Your own stay as they are.
Video (ffmpeg)Location of ffmpeg for videos, AVIF and HEIC (Opening images, animations and videos).

The settings folder

Preferences and app data are in a Chickxel folder at a fixed place per OS. Open it with Help > Open settings file location.

OSLocation
Windows%AppData%\Chickxel\
macOS~/Library/Application Support/Chickxel/
Linux~/.config/Chickxel/

Recovery/Documents saved separately when an error forced the app to quit

References/The default AI reference folder

When errors occur

When an unexpected error occurs, the app usually keeps running and ① shows the error and the log file name. Use ② Open log folder and ③ Report on Discord to report it with the log (Feedback).

After closing the error dialog, open the log folder with ④ Help > Open error log folder.

Log files

Logs are kept in Chickxel/Logs in the settings folder as crash-YYYYMMDD-HHmmss.log (the latest 20), with the time, app version, OS and error details. Errors in quick succession are only logged, without dialogs.

Errors that force the app to quit

Each unsaved document is saved as a recovery file in Chickxel/Recovery/ in the settings folder (<document name>-<date time>-<number>.chickxel); the original files aren't changed. Open the folder with Help > Open settings file location and open the recovery file yourself.

Export and sharing

Export your art as sheet images for game engines or as GIF, APNG and frame PNGs, and upload it to the chickxel.com gallery. For exporting along with every save, see Saving.

PageWhat it covers
Export dialogLayout, the export list, format, name and folder, the export buttons
Sheet settingsLayout, cell count, spacing, scale and file splitting of the frame sheet and layer atlas; setting presets
Game engine filesUnity prefab and animations, Godot 4 SpriteFrames, Aseprite JSON
Unity Import SettingsUnity texture settings written to the .meta
Exporting GIF, APNG and frame PNGsAnimations for sharing and a PNG per frame
Upload to galleryUploading pictures to the chickxel.com gallery

Export dialog

Use this to turn your art into files for games or other programs, such as sheet images and GIFs. Open it with File > Export… (Ctrl+E).

Window layout

From left to right the dialog has ① the export list, the selected row's settings and the output preview. Changes go into the selected row right away, and each one can be undone.

  • ⑫ Middle = settings: the selected row's setting sections follow the Format group at the top.
  • ⑬ Right = output preview: the output preview lists each file to be made and its cells. Click a cell to go to its frame.
  • ⑭ Divider: drag it to split the width between the middle and right columns.

Export list

The list has one row for each thing this project exports. The first row is the project's sheet; you can add more rows of the same format with different names or folders (for example, a GIF per tag).

  • ① Export list: click a row to show it in the middle and right columns. Each row shows its file name and kind.
  • ② Add: copies the selected row to the end of the list with _2, _3… added to its name (④ off). Change its format in ⑥.
  • ③ List presets: saves the whole list under a name so you can load it in other projects.
  • ④ On-save checkbox: when on, the row is made again every time you save. A new project's sheet row has it on.
  • ⑤ Red trash button: removes the row. If it made files, you're asked whether to move them to the app trash; Cancel keeps them.

Saving (Ctrl+S) makes every row with ④ on again (see Saving). If two rows would make the same file, you're told the export failed.

Format, name and folder

  • ⑥ Format: PNG, JPG, TGA and BMP make a sheet image with the frames on one picture; GIF, APNG and PNG per frame make an animation. JPG also has a quality setting (1–100%, 90 at first).
  • ⑦ Name and Folder: the file name to make (before the extension; empty = the project name) and its folder. Change them with Change location….
  • ⑧ Game engine: chooses the engine files written with the images: None (default), Unity, Godot 4 or JSON (see Game engine files).
  • ⑨ Prefab, ⑩ Animation and ⑪ Files to be created: shown when Unity is chosen in ⑧.

Export buttons

  • ⑮ Export (blue button): writes only the selected row with the current settings, without saving the .chickxel. A sheet row closes the dialog; an animation row keeps it open.
  • ⑯ Save as…: saves just the same sheet images under a name you choose, apart from the project settings. No Unity settings file is written, and you're asked before overwriting.

Close (Esc) just closes the dialog. Changed settings are already in the project.

Good to know

  • Switching the project's sheet row to an animation format takes the sheet out of the list and puts an animation row with the same name and folder first.
  • The window position, size, split and collapsed sections stay the same in other projects.
  • A long export shows a progress box over the whole window until it finishes; drawing and shortcuts are ignored meanwhile.
  • Old outputs that are no longer made are cleared by old output cleanup.
  • When you load a list preset, file names that start with the project name take the new project's name, and folders are relative to the project folder. Loading can be undone.

Sheet settings

Read this to change how cells are laid out, spaced, sized and split into files. A sheet row (PNG, JPG, TGA, BMP) makes a frame sheet of the frames and a layer atlas of the layer images, and both use the same settings.

Setting sections

SectionMain settings
⑱ CommonSettings both outputs share: output, skins, layout, cell count, order, cell names, spacing, scale and more
⑲ FilesExport on save, folder
⑳ Frame sheetName, Single frame, path
㉑ Layer atlasName, keep original positions, Sprite Library, path
㉒ AdvancedPer sheet
㉓ Unity Import SettingsUnity texture settings. Shown only when the game engine is Unity.

Turning on Advanced > Per sheet (㉒) copies the current common values into separate settings for the layer atlas.

Common section

A new project starts with the frame sheet only, grid layout, the current skins, automatic cell count, 1 px spacing and 1× scale.

SettingWhat it does
LayoutGrid places cells of equal size in order; Auto layout packs cells of different sizes tightly.
Max width and packing order (auto layout)The sheet's maximum width (0 = auto) and packing order (In sort order or Largest first).
㉔ Cell count (grid layout)Auto (smallest sheet), N columns or N rows (1–1024).
㉕ Sort orderDrag the layer, skin, tag and frame rows or press Alt+↑/↓ to reorder them, and use New row here between rows to set line breaks. Cell names stay the same.
㉖ Separate filesSplits files per tag and per layer (layer atlas). Splitting per skin is chosen under Skin packing.
㉗ Cell namesFrame number or By tag (default). They become Unity sprite names, so changing them shows a warning (see Export cell names).
Spacing between cells0–64 px (1 px at first).
Trim bordersCuts the transparent border off each cell (off at first).
Power of twoRounds the sheet width and height up to sizes like 32, 64 or 128 (off at first).
Scale1–16×. Enlarges the output images by a whole number, keeping pixels crisp (1× at first).

Raising the scale keeps the size in Unity the same (Unity's size unit and 9-slice borders are multiplied too). Spacing and max width apply to the enlarged result.

㉘ Layers to include: Choose layers… picks the layers to include from the layer tree (turning one off leaves out its children). With skins, the Skin row and Choose skins… (N) also pick the skins and skin presets to export.

Files, frame sheet and layer atlas sections

  • Files section: export on save (the same as the list's on-save checkbox) and folder (empty = the project folder).
  • Frame sheet section: name (empty = the project name) and path. Single frame appears only when Sprite Mode is Single and exports one chosen frame at canvas size.
  • layer atlas section: name (empty = the frame sheet name + _parts), keep original positions (auto layout; on at first), Sprite Library (Unity only) and path. For which layers go in, see layer atlas.
  • The folder and sheet names are saved encrypted so only this computer can read them. Opened on another computer or by another user, they go back to the defaults (the project folder and name) (see File types and where they go).

Sheet presets

  • ㉙ Preset (right end of the Format title row): saves the selected sheet row's settings under a name so you can load them in other projects. It has built-in presets, reset to defaults, rename, overwrite, delete and JSON export/import.

A preset holds the file format, scale, JPG quality, game engine and export on save. Per-project values such as file names, folders, chosen skins and excluded layers aren't included.

Game engine files

Read this to put your sheets straight into a game engine such as Unity or Godot. Pick the engine under Game engine in the Format group, and the files that engine reads are written with the images.

Game engineWhat is written too
None (images only)Images only (the default for new projects).
Unity (.meta)A Unity settings file (.meta) per image, plus the prefab, animations and Sprite Library when turned on.
Godot 4 (SpriteFrames)A SpriteFrames .tres, plus a .tscn when Scene is on.
JSON (Aseprite format, Phaser, PixiJS and more)A .json with the same name for each frame sheet file.

Unity prefab and animations

Choosing Unity shows the two options below (off at first). The files are created in the frame sheet's folder, named after the sheet, and written every time the sheets are exported.

  • Prefab (layers placed): a Unity object, sheetname.prefab, with the layers in place. It follows the layer tree, and hidden layers go in turned off. Without a layer atlas, it holds one sprite.
  • Animation (clips and Animator): an animation clip per tag, sheetname_tag.anim, and an Animator that plays them, sheetname.controller. Speed, direction and looping follow the tags, and the first tag plays first.
  • Files to be created: with an option on, the names of the files to be created are listed (each also gets a .meta).
The prefab, clips and controller are rewritten on every export. To add components or child objects in Unity, do it in a Prefab Variant of this prefab.

Exporting for Godot 4

Choosing Godot 4 (SpriteFrames) writes an animation file, sheetname.tres (SpriteFrames). Put it on an AnimatedSprite2D node in Godot and each tag plays as an animation right away.

  • ⑰ Scene (.tscn, AnimatedSprite2D): when on, a scene with that node, sheetname.tscn, is written too. It keeps pixels sharp and autoplays the first animation.

Exporting JSON

Choosing JSON (Aseprite format, Phaser, PixiJS and more) writes a .json with cell positions, frame durations and tags. It uses the Aseprite format, so web game tools such as Phaser and PixiJS read it as is.

Good to know

  • Re-exporting keeps Unity's links, so sprites already placed in scenes don't break. Unchanged files aren't rewritten.
  • The Unity material is Sprite-Lit-Default in URP projects, otherwise Sprites-Default.
  • Effects applied to a layer together with its children, and top-level normal layers, aren't in the prefab.
  • Godot 4 and JSON files are made from the frame sheet only, not from the layer atlas.
  • Turning an option off makes the files it created earlier subject to old output cleanup.

Unity Import Settings

Read this to set how Unity reads your images (size unit, pivot, filtering). With Unity as the game engine, it appears as ㉚ Unity Import Settings at the bottom of the settings.

The values go into the Unity settings file (.meta). The initial values suit pixel art, so you can usually leave them; field names match Unity's Texture Importer.

FieldWhat it does (initial value)
Sprite ModeOne picture (Single) or several cells (Multiple) (Single). The layer atlas is always Multiple
Pixels Per UnitPixels per Unity unit, 1–10000 (16)
Mesh Type · Extrude EdgesSprite shape: a rectangle (Full Rect) or fitted to the art (Tight). With Tight, the edge grows by 0–32 px (1)
PivotThe sprite's anchor point: 9 presets or X/Y 0–1 (center). Single only
Generate Physics ShapeMakes a collision shape from the art (on)
AdvancedColor and transparency handling: sRGB (on), Alpha Source (Input Texture Alpha), Alpha Is Transparency (on), Read/Write (off), Mip Maps (off)
Wrap · Filter · AnisoEdges (Wrap, Clamp), pixel handling when scaled (Filter, Point = no blur), Aniso 0–16 (1)
Default platformMax size (auto or 32–16384), Resize Algorithm (Mitchell), Format (Automatic), compression (None), Use Crunch (off)

Exporting GIF, APNG and frame PNGs

Use this to make an animation to post online or use elsewhere. In the Export dialog, select a row whose format is GIF animation, APNG or PNG per frame.

FormatWhat it makes
GIF animationOne .gif file. Up to 256 colors per frame, and no semi-transparent colors.
Animated PNG (APNG)One animated .png file. No color limit, and partial transparency is kept.
PNG per frame (to a folder)One PNG per frame in the row's folder, in playback order (from <name>_001.png). No Speed or Loop.

Adding a row

If there is no such row, copy a row with Add and change its format. Save as GIF… in the preview's right-click menu opens the dialog with the first GIF row selected (adding one if there is none).

Settings

  • ① Range: the current tag (at first), all frames or ⑩ a chosen tag. Frames follow the tag's direction (reverse, ping-pong).
  • ② Zoom: 1–10×, keeping pixels crisp (at first, the zoom that makes the result about 256 px).
  • ③ Speed: Original speed (default), Speed multiplier (0.1–10×) or Fixed FPS (1–50). The project's frame durations don't change.
  • ④ Loop: loop forever or play 1–100 times (1 = once).
  • ⑤ Background: transparent or a background color. GIF turns semi-transparent pixels fully transparent or opaque; APNG and PNG per frame keep them with Transparent (alpha kept as is).

Settings are stored per row in the project and can be undone.

Preview and export

⑥ The preview on the right plays at the chosen speed, and ⑦ shows the output size, frame count and duration (and colors for GIF).

⑨ Export writes the file straight to ⑧ the row's name and folder, without a save dialog. A new row targets <project>.gif next to the project (<project>_<tag> for a tag); change it with Change location… in ⑧.

The dialog stays open afterwards, so you can change settings and export again. With the row's on-save checkbox on, the file is made again on every save.

Good to know

  • GIF rounds frame durations to hundredths of a second and can't go below 20 ms (so Fixed FPS goes up to 50).
  • With more than 256 colors (255 with transparency), GIF reduces the colors in each frame.
  • PNG per frame asks before overwriting files with the same names in the folder.
  • If compositing or writing takes a while, a progress box covers the whole window until it finishes.

Upload to gallery

Upload pictures to the chickxel.com gallery with File > Upload to Gallery… or the gallery button in the menu bar. One post holds up to 10 PNGs and GIFs.

Choosing pictures

  • ① Picture list: select a row to choose One frame (PNG) or Animation (GIF) for it in the middle. The first row is the cover.
  • ② GIF range: all frames (default), one tag, a frame number range (played backwards if the start is greater than the end) or the frames selected in the timeline. Up to 1000 frames.
  • ③ Details: title (1–100 characters, the file name at first), description (up to 2000 characters) and tags (comma-separated, up to 8, each up to 32 lowercase letters, digits and hyphens).
  • ④ The account to upload with: its profile picture, name and level badge; Chickxel Account… opens the account window (see Chickxel account).
  • ⑤ Preview: shows the selected picture (GIFs play at the chosen speed). Below it, a notice says that uploads are public right away.
  • ⑥ View Gallery: opens the chickxel.com gallery in your browser.

Scale (1–10×, 1× at first, result up to 2048 px), Speed (as in GIF export), the title, description and tags, and the two sharing settings below apply to the whole post. Pictures larger than 2048 px can't be uploaded.

⑦ In the list, ⑧ Add appends a row (a GIF of all frames when there are several, otherwise a PNG of the current frame). Reorder rows with the up and down buttons and remove one with its Remove button. Choosing the same picture twice shows ⑨ a warning, and only the earlier one is uploaded.

Sharing the editable file

Turn on Also share the editable .chickxel file (off at first) to upload this project's .chickxel too, so anyone can download and edit it in Chickxel. The shared copy leaves out your export folder and includes script effect source code. The file can be up to 8 MiB.

Allowing image download

Turning off Allow image download (PNG/GIF) (on at first) shows the post's PNG/GIF download buttons only to you and admins. The pictures stay public, and you can change this later with Edit on the post.

Uploading

Clicking Upload first opens the account window if you aren't signed in. While uploading, a progress bar and Cancel Upload appear; when done, the post opens in your browser.

Good to know

  • If one picture exceeds 4 MB or the total exceeds 16 MB, reduce the scale or the number of pictures.
  • If you hit the daily upload limit, try again later.
  • Uploads earn XP for your account; the completion message shows the XP gained and your level (see Account level).
  • Gallery cards show each PNG for two seconds and play each GIF once before moving on.

Importing 3D models

Draws a GLB, glTF, OBJ or FBX 3D model at the size, angle and light you choose and imports it as pixel-art frames. Use it to make game sprites such as 8-direction sprites, isometric props and walk cycles.

Opening a model shows the Import 3D model window; Import opens a new tab with one image per frame. The frames are ordinary images you can edit.

PageRead it when
Opening 3D modelsYou open a 3D model file or look for one in the file browser
The import windowYou want to know the import window and its presets
Size, angle, light and shadingYou set the size, view angle, light and shading
Pixel cleanup and outlinesYou clean up semi-transparency, colors and outlines for pixel art
Frames: single image, turntable, animationYou choose between a single image, several directions or an animation
Attaching animations from other filesYou attach animations downloaded from Mixamo or elsewhere to a character

Opening 3D models

Open a 3D model file like an image when you want to turn a 3D character or prop into pixel-art sprites. The Import 3D model window comes up first so you can decide how to draw it (see The import window).

Ways to open

MethodHow
File menuFile > Open (.chickxel · image · GIF · video)… (Ctrl+O). Choose 3D models (GLB, glTF, FBX, OBJ) as the file type to see only models.
3D menu3D > Open 3D model… (no shortcut) opens the window without a model (see Opening the window without a model below).
Dropping on the windowDrop the file on the window from Explorer or Finder. Even on the canvas it opens in a new tab, not as a layer.
File browserClick its thumbnail (see the figure below).
As a Pixelate sourceAdd a model to the Pixelate window and the images drawn in the same import window become the source (see Opening the window and adding a source).

Supported formats

FormatWhat is drawnNot supported
glTF 2.0 (.glb, .gltf)Shape, colors and textures, transparency and glass, unlit materials, animationsCompressed meshes (Draco, meshopt)
OBJ (.obj + .mtl)Shape, colors and textures, opacityAnimations
FBX (.fbx)Binary FBX 7.x (what Mixamo gives you): shape, textures, skeleton, animations, opacityASCII FBX, version 6.x or older

File browser and Details

① The file browser shows models as thumbnails drawn at the default angle, and ② a cube badge at the bottom right marks them as 3D models. ③ OBJ files look the same. Files with nothing to draw (such as animation-only files) show a cube icon.

Right-click a file and open Details to see ④ the type (GLB, glTF, OBJ or FBX 3D model) and ⑤ the triangle, material and animation counts.

Opening the window without a model

3D > Open 3D model… opens the window without a model. ⑥ Drop a model file anywhere on the window (a blue border shows) or pick one with ⑦ Open model…. ⑧ Import turns on once a model is read.

With a model open, the same ways switch to another model and keep the settings. Switching to a model without animations sets the frames to Single image. If that model is already open in a tab, Import switches to it.

Good to know

  • A Reading 3D model progress window shows while the file is read. Anything that can't be drawn is listed in orange at the bottom of the import window.
  • Keep an OBJ's .mtl file and textures next to it. Without them, a default material is used.
  • If a glTF doesn't open, export it again without compression; for FBX, export again as FBX Binary.
  • Shape keys (morph targets, blend shapes) are drawn in their base shape.
  • Models with no triangles or more than 5 million aren't opened.

The import window

Use this window to decide what sprites a 3D model becomes. Compare the original with the pixel result, pick a preset or adjust values, then click Import.

Basic steps

  1. Pick a preset that fits the sprite you want (see the Presets table below).
  2. Drag Original to set the view angle.
  3. Adjust the size, colors and outlines while watching the pixel result.
  4. Click Import to open the result in a new tab.

Parts of the window

  • ① Original: the smooth model before cleanup. Drag it to turn the model.
  • ② Pixel result: the output image. Scroll to magnify it 1–16× and drag with the right or middle button to move. The output size doesn't change.
  • ③ With several frames, play them or pick one with the slider.
  • ④ The file name and the numbers of triangles, materials and animations.
  • ⑤ Choosing a preset sets every value to the preset's (see Presets below).
  • ⑥ The rows of the boxes on the right are explained in Size, angle, light and shading and Pixel cleanup and outlines.
  • ⑦ The output frame count, size and duration per frame (ms).
  • ⑧ Click it to open the result in a new tab once drawing finishes. You can cancel in the progress window.
  • ⑨ The Output box sets the output size and frame mode (see Frames).
  • ⑩ Switches to another model file, keeping the settings.

Handles in Original

  • ⑪ Click a dot on the axis gizmo to view along that axis. Hollow dots are the opposite side.
  • ⑫ Drag a ring to turn the model. The X ring sets the vertical angle, the Y ring the horizontal angle.
  • ⑬ Drag the outer Z ring to tilt the image.
  • ⑭ Drag the sun to set where the light comes from. The closer to the center of the yellow circle, the more the light comes from the front. A hollow sun means light from behind.
  • ⑮ Moving the bar down moves the light from the front (0°) to the back (180°). It's the same value as Light front/back in the settings.

Presets

⑯ The eight built-in presets match common game views; your saved presets follow them.

⑰ Save… saves the current values under a name, and ⑱ Delete deletes the selected saved preset after asking.

PresetValuesUse
Top-down 8 directions (angled)48 px, 0° horizontal and 55° vertical, 8 directions, 16 colors, darkened outer outlineTop-down RPG characters and vehicles seen from an angle above
Straight overhead 8 directions32 px, straight down (90° vertical), 8 directions, 12 colors, single-color outlineVehicles and buildings in top-down shooters and strategy games
Isometric 4 directions (2:1)64 px, 45° horizontal and 30° vertical (2:1 isometric), 4 directions, 20 colorsFour directions of props and characters for isometric maps
Side view (side-scroller)64 px, straight from the side (90° horizontal, 0° vertical), animation at 12 frames per second, 24 colors, lines where parts overlapWalk, run and attack animations for side-scrollers
Item icon 32 px32 px, 30° horizontal and 25° vertical, 115% zoom, single image, 12 colors, single-color outlineItem icons for inventories and shops
Character animation 64 px64 px, 25° horizontal and 12° vertical, animation at 12 frames per second, 24 colors, lines where parts overlapCharacter animations seen from slightly to the front
Large and smooth (for Pixelate)256 px, smooth edges (4×4), no cleanup or outline, single imageA source for Pixelate (grid, palette and outline are done there)
8-direction turntable 64 px (default)64 px, 0° horizontal and 30° vertical, 8 directions, edge smoothing off, 12 colors, single-color outline (outer and overlaps)The window's starting values; turn a model through 8 directions first to set its size and angle

Good to know

  • The window starts with the values of 8-direction turntable 64 px (default). Your changes carry over to the next model until you quit the app (except the chosen animation).
  • All but the large smooth preset use no semi-transparency, 3 shading steps, 45% ambient and light at −40° horizontal, 50° vertical (side view −30° and 40°). All but the default preset use 4×4 edge smoothing.
  • Saving under an existing name replaces it. Built-in names can't be used, and the selected animation isn't saved.

Size, angle, light and shading

Set how large the model is drawn, from which side and under which light. The starting values are 64×64 px, 0° horizontal, 30° vertical, orthographic.

RowWhat it does
① SizeThe output size (1–2048 px). With the link at the end on (default), the ratio is kept.
② View angleHorizontal (−360 to 360°) circles the model, Vertical (−90 to 90°) looks down from above (90° = straight down), and Roll (−180 to 180°) tilts the image. You can also use the handles in Original.
③ ProjectionOrthographic keeps sizes equal at any distance, as most game sprites do; Perspective makes near parts larger. Field of view (5–120°) applies to perspective only.
④ ZoomAt 100% the model just fits the shorter side (10–1000%). Fit goes back to 100%.
⑤ Light directionWhere light comes from (horizontal, vertical), relative to the camera. In a turntable the same side stays lit in every frame.
⑥ Light front/backMoves the light from the front (0°) through the side (90°) to the back (180°). It's the same value as the front/back bar left of Original.
⑦ BrightnessAmbient (0–100%) is the brightness of faces the light doesn't reach; Light strength (0–200%) is what the light adds to lit faces.
⑧ Shading steps0 shades smoothly; 2–16 cut the shading into that many bands, like cel shading.
⑨ Edge smoothingOff (crisp) gives crisp stepped edges; 2×2, 4×4 and 8×8 give smooth ones. Turning off Textures uses only the material base colors.

Color rows below the previews

  • ⑩ Model colors: N lists the model's material colors (base colors without light and shade), most used first. Textured parts aren't listed.
  • ⑪ Click a swatch to highlight where that color is used, darkening the rest. Click it again to turn this off.
  • ⑫ Colors in this frame: N lists the colors of the result frame on screen; hover for #RRGGBB.

Changing model colors

Right-click a Model colors swatch and choose Change color… to change it; Back to original and Reset all colors undo changes. Changed colors are used in both the previews and the import.

Only the parts with that color change; other colors stay. A color with transparency draws that part translucent. The model file isn't changed, and opening another model clears the changes.

Shading steps

With steps, round surfaces split into a few bands, crisp like hand-drawn shading. Models with only flat faces change little.

Edge smoothing

Off can break up thin parts (eyes, beak); on adds semi-transparent edges and blended colors. When it's on, combine it with No semi-transparency and Colors.

Orthographic and perspective

Use orthographic for sprites placed on a tile grid, and perspective for single illustrations or dramatic icons.

Pixel cleanup and outlines

Use these settings to turn the drawn result into pixel art ready for a game: remove blurry edges, reduce the colors and add outlines.

BoxWhat it does
① Colors0 keeps the colors; 2–64 reduce them to that many. All frames share one palette.
② No semi-transparencyTurns half-transparent edge pixels fully opaque or fully transparent.
③ Outer · OutsideOuter draws a line around the model. With Outside on, the line goes outside the model; off, it goes on the edge pixels.
④ OverlapsDraws a line between parts that overlap front to back, such as a wing in front of the body.
⑤ Object edgesDraws a line where different objects touch. For how objects are split, see Object edges below.
⑥ CreasesDraws a line on edges that bend more than the angle next to it (1–179°, default 60°). Smaller angles give more lines.
⑦ Color changesDraws a line between parts of different colors, such as around the eyes and beak. The smaller the amount next to it (1–100, default 15), the more similar colors also get lines.
⑧ WidthThe outer line width (1–3 px). Other lines are always 1 px.
⑨ Color modeSingle color draws with the chosen color; Darkened (sel-out) darkens the model color under the line.
⑩ Line colorThe color for Single color mode (default: a near-black purple).
⑪ Darken amountHow much Darkened mode lowers the brightness (0–100%).

Cleanup runs in this order: no semi-transparency, color reduction, outlines. With no outline on, ⑧–⑪ are off; ⑩ is on only for Single color and ⑪ only for Darkened.

Outline types

Outer alone sharpens the silhouette, adding Overlaps separates overlapping parts, and adding Creases puts lines on edges too. If crease lines crowd a small sprite, raise the angle.

Single color and Darkened

Darkened (sel-out) lines follow the neighboring colors and blend into the art; a single color keeps the outline clear on any background.

No semi-transparency and colors

Edges become only opaque or transparent and the colors drop to 8, ready for a game. Too few colors can change small details such as eyes.

Color changes

Places in a different color, such as the eyes, cheeks and beak, get a line around them, so small details stand out. Light and shade within one color get no line.

Object edges

Each object in the file separates objects that touch (floor and machine, machine and bin). Each connected piece outlines every stacked box in a model built from boxes, which looks busy.

Glass and semi-transparent materials

Glass is tinted by its base color and shows what's behind it; over a transparent background its pixels stay semi-transparent (left). With No semi-transparency on, mostly see-through parts drop out and the rest becomes opaque (right).

Frames: single image, turntable, animation

Choose whether to make a single image, several directions such as 8-direction views, or an animation such as a walk. You choose in the bottom four rows of the Output box.

① ModeFrames made
Single imageOne image of the rest pose (100 ms)
Turntable (full turn)Turns the model one full circle and draws ② the frame count (1–64). 8 gives 8 directions 45° apart (100 ms per frame).
AnimationDraws ③ the chosen animation at ④ frames per second (1–60), up to the maximum frame count (1–1000, default 120). Only for models with animations.

② The frame count and Free rotation are on only for Turntable, ③ and ④ only for Animation. For Import… next to ③, see Attaching animations.

An 8-frame turntable gives 8 directions 45° apart, with the same light and colors in each.

The imported result

When it finishes, ⑤ a new tab named after the file opens with ⑥ one image per frame. The first save asks where to put it.

Good to know

  • With Free rotation on, the preview slider shows any angle from 0–359°. The number of frames imported doesn't change.
  • In turntables and animations, the model keeps the same size and position in every frame.
  • If you added the model as a Pixelate source, the frames become that window's source instead of a new tab.

Attaching animations from other files

When a character and its animations come as separate files, as with Mixamo, open the character model and load the animation files onto it. Files with the same skeleton can be combined.

Pick FBX or glTF files with ① Import… (several at once). Attached ones appear in ② the list under the file name, ③ the summary's animation count goes up, and ④ Frames switches to Animation. Check the motion with ⑤ play.

  • Bones are matched by name, ignoring case and everything up to the last : or | (such as mixamorig:).
  • Rotations are copied as they are, movement only for the top bone (Hips), scaled to the character's height; scale keys are dropped.
  • Motion of bones the character doesn't have is dropped. Files that couldn't be attached and warnings are reported together at the end.
  • Attached animations are gone when the window closes, and the model file isn't changed. To import again, load them again.

chick.glb had only Idle; attaching a walk with a Mixamo skeleton and drawing its 0.8 s at 10 frames per second gave 8 frames.

Using Mixamo step by step

  1. On mixamo.com, pick or upload a character and use Download with Format FBX Binary (.fbx) and Skin With Skin.
  2. Download each animation you want with Skin Without Skin. Turn on In Place for loops such as walking and running.
  3. Open the character FBX in Chickxel and pick all the animation FBX files at once with Import… in the import window.
  4. Choose one in the animation list, set it up with a preset (Side view, Character animation and so on) and import.

If you see "ASCII FBX files can't be read", download again from Mixamo with Format FBX Binary. Mixamo animations don't attach to characters from elsewhere, because the bone names don't match.

Pixelate AI images

AI-made "pixel-look" images have uneven cell sizes, blurred edges and hundreds of colors. The Pixelate AI image window turns them into clean pixel art where each source cell becomes one pixel. It takes single images, sprite sheets, animated GIFs, videos and 3D models.

Workflow

  1. Load a source: open the window and drop or paste an image.
  2. Run: make candidates with Clean up or Auto optimize.
  3. Pick: compare the candidates' scores and previews and select one.
  4. Touch up (when needed): exclude or pin colors, or change the settings and run again.
  5. Insert: press Apply or Add to new tab.

Opening the window and loading a source

Start here when you want to turn an AI-made image, video or 3D model into clean pixel art: open this window and load the source. Open it from the AI menu or the file browser.

Ways to open the window

WhereSourceUse it when
① AI > Pixelate AI image…None. Drop or paste an image into ② the empty window, or use ③ Browse….The image is in another app or on the clipboard
⑥ AI > Pixelate current layer…The current layer. Several frames are joined into one image.You already placed the AI image in a document
In the file browser, right-click ⑦ an image file > ⑧ Pixelate AI image…That fileThe image file is in your project folder

Loading and replacing the source

MethodDetails
DropDrop a file anywhere on the window (the first one if several).
PasteCtrl+V outside text boxes pastes a copied file or the clipboard image.
Browse…Starts in the folder selected in the file browser.
⑨ Replace sourceThe folder button in the bar under the source.

Replacing the source clears the candidates and optimization results and starts over. Dropping or pasting again also replaces it.

Animations, videos and 3D models

An animation, video or 3D model becomes one image with one cell per frame. 2. Frames is set to those cells.

  • A video first opens a window to choose the range and frame rate.
  • A 3D model first opens the Import 3D model window. For a large, smooth render, pick its Large and smooth (for Pixelate) preset.
  • With more than 300 frames, you're asked whether to load all or only the first 300.
  • Beyond 64 cells per side, only the leading frames are loaded, with a message.
  • Empty frames are left out of the result.
  • Replacing it with a single image returns the frame split to its remembered settings.

When a source comes in

Loading a source only prepares it and runs nothing. The candidate row gets a Not run yet tile for each checked preset.

  • Press Clean up or Auto optimize to make results.
  • With Auto optimize on load (off by default) on, a new source is auto-optimized right away (Auto Optimize Settings).
  • Apply frame split runs each checked preset once as set.

Good to know

  • Closing the window (Apply, Cancel or Esc) only hides it until you quit the app. Reopen it with ① to get the same source and candidates back; a running optimization keeps going.
  • Opening with a new source, as with ⑥ or ⑧, starts a new window. ⑥ doesn't open on an empty layer.
  • ④ Apply and ⑤ Auto optimize stay off until a source is loaded.
  • For an image from AI drawing, click Convert in the chat. Image generation from an external AI connection opens this window directly.
  • Videos and AVIF or HEIC images need ffmpeg.

Source and preview

Look here to check by eye how the result compares with the source. The source is on the left, the selected candidate's large preview in the middle and the candidate row at the bottom.

AreaWhat it does
① Source viewShows the source and frame boundaries. Wheel to zoom, drag to pan, double-click to fit.
② Large previewPlays the result next to the same source frame. Click once to open the enlarged view.
③ Color swatchesThe result's colors. Left-click highlights a color; right-click excludes it.
④ Playback and frame stripPrevious/next, play/pause, FPS and frame thumbnails.
⑤ Candidate tilesClick to select, double-click to apply (Candidates and scores).

Source view

  • Hovering a source cell highlights the matching result pixel.
  • Drag a frame box to move it, or its edges to resize it. On release the candidates are rebuilt with those boundaries.
  • Right-click the source to Copy image or Save as PNG…. Inside a frame you can copy or save just that frame.

Frames picked for sampling

Frames picked by Frames to sample for optimizing have a yellow border.

The bar under the source

ControlWhat it does
⑥ Show gridOn by default: draws the cell boundaries the result actually used over the source. Hidden when cells are tiny on screen.

The bar also has the Replace source button and the status text; hover the text to read it in full.

Playback and swatches

  • With the preview focused, Space plays or pauses and Left/Right change the frame.
  • Hover a swatch for its HEX value and pixel count. Drag the handle above the swatches to change their height.

Candidates and scores

Look here to compare the results of several presets and pick the best. Each candidate tile shows a result and its score.

Reading a candidate tile

PartMeaning
① Candidate tile thumbnailThe result's first frame.
③ Preset nameThe preset that made the tile; a run with edited values adds a number, such as name_1.
Colors · size · framesNumber of colors used, output size in pixels, number of frames.
② Outline fit modeThe result's Fit outline to 1 px mode.
Blue line at the bottomWhy it was made, and the score (e.g. As set (73.4)).

The line under the preview shows the selected candidate's score and size, and ④ the score breakdown shows each term (Score terms and weights).

⑥ Hover a tile for its full name and score breakdown.

Selecting and applying a candidate

  • Click a tile to select it and load its settings on the left.
  • If you have edits that haven't run, clicking another tile asks whether to save.
  • Double-click a tile to apply that candidate right away (Output).

Running and not-run tiles

  • A running tile has a yellow line circling its border. Click it to follow the best result so far; colors can't be excluded or pinned meanwhile.
  • ⑤ Presets not run yet show as Not run yet tiles at the end of the row. Right-click > Run only this preset runs just that preset.

Candidate tile menu

ItemWhat it does
Copy image · Copy enlarged (×4)Copies the first frame to paste on the canvas or into another app.
Save as PNG…Saves the first frame as PNG, named source name_preset name.
Rerun only this presetReruns just that tile. Disabled while running or if the preset was deleted.

Failed results and warnings

A result that is empty, single-colored or too small scores 0 and goes to the end. Selecting it shows why under the preview.

  • With an automatic grid, such a result is redone with another cell size.
  • The automatic cell size never exceeds 1/5 of the frame's shorter side.
  • If the selected result's long side exceeds 256 px, "Large result (…) — check the pixel size" appears under the preview.

Enlarged view and color decision

The Enlarged view puts the result and the source side by side at the same zoom to compare them cell by cell. Left-click an image in the large preview once to open it.

Zooming and panning

① The left pane is the current frame's result, ② the right pane the same source frame. Both always share zoom and position, and open at the largest integer zoom that fits.

ActionResult
WheelZooms around the pointer (up to 128×): in 0.25 steps from 1× to 2×, 0.5 steps from 2× to 4×, and larger steps above.
DragPans both panes together.
④ Fit imageFits both images to the panes without stretching.
Double-clickRefits at the largest integer zoom that fits.
Space · Left/Right · previous, play/pause and next in the top barPlays and steps frames with the same frame and FPS as the large preview.
Esc · click outside · Close buttonCloses the enlarged view.

③ The Show grid button draws the real cell boundaries on the source pane and a one-cell-per-pixel grid on the result pane. It changes together with the same button under the source. Hover the info icon in the top bar for a list of controls.

Showing where a color is used

Where you clickResult
Left-click a result pixelBriefly highlights the color's swatch and same-color areas, shows the HEX value and coordinates in the status line, and opens that pixel's color decision row.
Right-click a result pixelExcludes or restores the color shown there (excluding colors).
Left-click a source cellMarks the source cell and the result pixel it became with blue squares and opens the color decision row. The status line shows "Source cell #cell color → result #result color".
⑤ Color swatchesThe same as in the large preview: left-click shows where a color is used, right-click excludes, restores or unpins, and + at the end adds a color from the source.

While the eyedropper is active, clicking a source cell adds a pinned color. The frame strip is hidden for a single frame.

Color decision

⑥ The Color decision (x, y) row appears above the swatches when you left-click a pixel. It shows the color the pixel had after each step, in order; the last color in bold is the result.

Step nameMeaning
Cell color (method)The color picked from the source cell's inner pixels by the cell color method, before palette mapping.
PaletteThe palette color nearest to the cell color; shown as Palette (prefer shade family) when the shade family rule picked it, or Palette (dithering) with dithering.
Later stepsOnly steps that changed the color appear, such as outline cleanup, orphans, holes, frame consistency, pinned and excluded colors.
Closest palette colors (distance):The three palette colors nearest to the cell color, with distances and their lightness, chroma and hue differences. The chosen one is bold.
  • Hover a step name to see its rule. The row disappears when you change the selected candidate or the excluded or pinned colors.
  • With OKLab distance, a color with some chroma can come out closer than a grey that differs only in lightness. Try another color distance then.
  • A black cell that ends up light was repainted by Fit outline to 1 px to thin the outline. To keep it black, set it to Off and compare.

Optimization info

The middle of the top bar shows optimization info: the progress line and score graph for a running preset tile, or the summary and graph for an optimized candidate. It is empty for a candidate run as set.

Excluding and pinning colors

Touch up the selected result's colors without rebuilding it. Exclude unwanted colors, and pin source colors the result lost.

Excluding a color

  • Right-click a swatch to exclude that color. Only its pixels change, each to the remaining color nearest to the source. Right-clicking a pixel in the result image or the enlarged view works too.
  • ① An excluded swatch stays dimmed at the end; right-click it again to restore it. ② Restore all in the notice restores them all.
  • The last remaining color can't be excluded. Left-click an excluded swatch to highlight where it used to be.

Pinning a color

  • Press ③ +, then click a source cell: its representative color becomes a pinned color. Shift+click keeps picking; Esc or right-click stops.
  • A pinned color changes only the pixels closer to it. Its swatch has a blue border and triangle; right-click to unpin. Pinned colors are never excluded.

Pixels that 7. Cleanup changed (outline, orphans, holes, L corners, flicker) are compared by their cleaned-up color, so a pinned color leaves them alone.

What carries over

SituationExcluded and pinned colors
New candidates and Auto optimize resultsApplied too; a color within OKLab 0.03 counts as the same.
Apply · Add to new tabIncluded in the inserted result.
Saving a presetNot saved.
Replacing the sourceCleared.
Viewing a running tileCan't be changed until it finishes.

Presets and Clean up

Use presets to save settings under a name and reuse them. The green Clean up button makes a result from the current values.

The preset bar

ControlWhat it does
① Preset listChoosing one only loads its values; results stay. New preset at the end makes a new preset.
② SaveEnabled only after edits; saves the current values.
③ Preset settingsThe gear icon. Opens the Auto Optimize Settings window to rename, copy and delete presets.
④ Clean upThe green play icon. Runs the preset with the current values (below).

A preset holds every step's settings and the optimize criteria, but not pinned or excluded colors.

Clean up

④ Clean up runs the chosen preset with the current values and replaces its tile. The button slowly pulses while you have edits that haven't run.

CaseBehavior
Some steps have Optimize onOnly those steps are auto-optimized.
No step has Optimize onMakes one result as set.
A saved preset as isThe values found are written into the preset.
Unsaved editsAdds new name_1, name_2… tiles and leaves the preset unchanged.

Built-in presets

A fresh install has these presets. All can be edited or deleted; Reset in the Auto Optimize Settings window restores them.

Same hue familyKeeps light and dark shades of each color well (32 colors).AllYes10000 · 300

  • Built-in presets start with a 300-second time limit (Quick check: 30 seconds).
  • Settings without a preset start at 2000 trials and 120 seconds. New presets and copies take the budget shown on the left.
  • No built-in preset removes the background or dithers.

Settings column and Quick settings

Open the settings column on the left when you want to adjust the result yourself. Changes take effect when you press Clean up.

  • Click ① the vertical bar to open the settings column; click again to close it.
  • The preset bar is at the top; each step below can be collapsed.
  • Steps with Optimize checked let optimization vary their values (Optimize criteria).

Steps at a glance

StepWhat it doesChange it when
0. Optimize criteriaBudget (trials, seconds) and scoring rules for Clean up and Auto optimize.Optimization takes too long or stops too early, or the score prefers results that look worse.
Quick settingsGathers often-changed values: output size, Fit cells by row, target colors, Remove background and one palette.You want a given output size, want to turn background removal on or off, or want frames to share colors or not.
1. BackgroundMakes the background transparent (Keep background at first).The source has a solid background that stays in the result, or a white sticker border.
2. FramesSplits a sprite sheet into frames.Frames merge or split in the wrong place.
3. Pixel gridFinds the size of one source "pixel" (the cell size) and the cell boundaries.The result is far too large or small, or cell lines miss the color blocks.
4. Output sizeSets the output size from the cell size, a fixed size or a factor.You need a specific size such as 32×32.
5. Cell colorPicks one color per cell.Blurred edge colors leak into the result.
6. PaletteMerges similar colors into a palette and maps each cell to a palette color.There are too many or too few colors, shading turns into another hue, or you must use a given palette.
7. CleanupFixes orphan pixels and holes and fits the outline to 1 px.The outline is thick or broken, or dots such as pupils disappear.
8. Frame consistencyMatches pixels that differ slightly from the neighboring frames to reduce flicker.The animation flickers.
OutputHow the result is inserted: new layer or new document.You want another destination or frame duration.
AI (experimental)Asks the AI chat to touch up the result or rank candidates after applying.You want AI help.

Quick settings

Quick settings gathers the values you change most. They stay in sync with the same settings in the steps below.

  • ② In the output size row, Auto (default) follows the cell size; off fits the width × height you enter.
  • With the link button on, the width-to-height ratio is kept.
  • ③ Fit cells by row (up to 30%): fits cell boundaries to the source row by row (Local cell correction).
  • In the target colors row, a result with more colors than this scores lower. If you need exactly that many, set the color count in 6. Palette.
  • Frames to sample (default 5): with many frames, only this many are used to search faster.
  • The Remove background check makes the background transparent (same as 1. Background).
  • ④ One palette for all frames: on makes all frames use the same colors.

Background and frames

Look here when the source background stays in the result, or a sheet with several frames needs splitting. ⑤ 1. Background removes the background and ⑥ 2. Frames splits frames.

1. Background

The background is kept at first. To remove it, turn on Remove background in Quick settings or choose ⑦ a mode.

ModeResultUse it when
Keep backgroundRemoves nothing (the initial value).The source is already transparent, or the background is part of the art.
Auto (border color)Removes the most common border color as the background.A character on a solid backdrop such as white.
Pick colorRemoves the color chosen with the color button next to it.Auto picks the wrong color.
SettingDetails
ModeHow the background is found (table above).
ToleranceHow close to the background color a color must be to be removed (default 24). Auto next to it picks it from the image.
Only background connected to the borderOn by default: removes only background connected to the border, so the same color inside stays.
Use existing transparencyOn by default: keeps transparency already in the source.
Alpha thresholdPixels fainter than this (default 128) count as transparent. Lower it to keep soft shadows.
Specks (px)Removes detached bits smaller than this (default 24 px); 0 turns it off.
  • ⑨ Remove sticker border (on by default): also removes a white band around the outline.
  • ⑩ Keep background (one color): keeps the background, flattened to one color.
  • The blurred fringe between background and sprite goes too.

2. Frames

Choose how to split with ⑧ the mode. All frames get the same canvas size (Same canvas size for all frames).

ModeHow it splitsUse it when
Single imageThe whole image is one frame.One character or scene.
Auto (split by empty space)Splits at empty rows and columns. Small pieces (Merge small pieces (%), default 12%) join the nearest frame (within Distance (px) 48).The sheet has gaps between frames.
GridSplits into Columns × Rows cells (1–64 each). Hands and feet crossing a line stay in their own frame.The sheet is evenly spaced or frames touch.

Align: where frames sit

AlignPlacementUse it when
Bottom centerFeet on the bottom of the canvas, centered horizontally (default).Animations with feet on the ground, such as walking.
CenterPlaces each frame in the middle of the canvas.Effects such as explosions or spells, or icons.
Keep offsetsKeeps each frame's position from the sheet.Animations where position is the motion, such as a jump.

Good to know

  • Frames are split once when a source is loaded. After changing values, press Apply frame split to split again.
  • A frame holds only the pixels inside its blue box. If hands or feet are cut off, enlarge the box in the source view.

Pixel grid and output size

Look here when the result is far too large or small. One pixel of an AI image is really a block of several px (a cell); step 3 finds the cell size and step 4 sets the output size.

Grid detection

With ③ Mode of ① 3. Pixel grid at Auto (edge analysis) (default), the cell size is found for you. With Manual you enter it.

ModeWhat it does
Auto (edge analysis)Follows cells even when their size varies (default). Right for most AI images.
ManualUses evenly spaced cells. Choose it when Auto finds double or half the real size.
Pixel size from valueWhat it measures
Auto (best edge match)Uses whichever of the three below fits the source best (default).
Outline thicknessUses the outline thickness as the cell size.
Color edgesMeasures the spacing of color changes.
Block edgesMeasures the spacing of strong color edges, ignoring faint texture.
  • Pixel size from is used only when Fit outline to 1 px isn't By shrinking.
  • A clean enlarged image with few colors gets its exact scale.
  • Allowed size jitter (%) (default 15%): how much cell sizes may vary.
  • The info line under the settings shows the cell size found and why.
  • Re-fitting cell lines per band is covered in Local cell correction.

Manual grid and grid-first optimization

SettingDetails
Manual · Pixel size (px) · Offset X·YEnter the cell size and start X/Y yourself. Use detected as manual copies the detected values.
Add to auto pixel size (px)A value added to the cell size found automatically.
Optimize grid fit firstWhen on, optimization finds the cell layout first, then optimizes the other steps on it.
Preview cellsAdds a tile at the front of the row for checking the cell lines. Values found aren't written to the preset.

A manual grid wins over By shrinking in Fit outline to 1 px. Add to auto pixel size and start X/Y differ per image, so presets don't save them.

4. Output size

The boxes shown depend on ④ the mode of ② 4. Output size. ⑥ Padding (px) (default 1 px) is the empty border around the result.

ModeOutput size
From pixel sizeEach cell becomes one result pixel (default). Closest to the source.
Fit a fixed sizeFits ⑤ Width×Height (e.g. 32×32). The link (Keep aspect) keeps the ratio.
Scale factorMultiplies the source size by a factor (0.125 for an 8× enlargement).
  • Width × height is a maximum. A small source gives a smaller result.
  • Padding counts toward the size: 64×64 with padding 1 leaves 62 pixels on the long side.

Local cell correction

⑦ Local cell correction (on by default) re-fits the cell lines per band to that band's strong color edges. It follows art that is half a cell off in places.

Settings

SettingRange · defaultMeaning
Max shift (% of cell)5–99%, default 35%The farthest a line may move; neighboring cell widths stay within 70–130% of the original.
Band (cells)1–32 cells, default 2Width of each band fitted separately. Smaller follows more closely (1 = every row), larger is smoother.
Minimum cell size (px)1–16 px, default 4 pxThe average cell size from which correction applies. Lower values also adjust small cells but may follow blur noise.

Good to know

  • Within a band the lines stay straight, so cells are rectangles; the lines bend where bands change.
  • The grid stays straight if the average cell is below the minimum or no line moved. The selected-result line shows "Local correction applied to n/m frames" or similar.
  • With step 3's Optimize on, optimization also tries it on and off with different max shift and band.
  • Two sharp edges within one cell can't both be matched by one line.

Fit cells by row

  • Fit cells by row (up to 30%) in Quick settings applies this correction row by row. Turning it on fixes Max shift at 30%, Band at 1 and Minimum cell size at 4 px, and sets the size jitter to 0.
  • While on, these values, step 3's Optimize check and Optimize grid fit first are disabled. Optimization also leaves the grid alone, along with the settings that affect it (outline lightness, Fit outline to 1 px, sticker border). Turning it off also turns local correction off.

Cell color and palette

Look here when the result has too many or too few colors, or must match a given palette. Step 5 picks one color per cell and step 6 reduces those colors to a few.

5. Cell color

The Mode of ① 5. Cell color is how one color is picked in each cell.

MethodColor picked
Most frequent colorThe most common color in the cell (default). Right for most AI images.
MedianThe middle value of each channel. Ignores stray specks.
Center pixelThe center pixel. For clean enlarged pixel art.
MeanThe average. Tends to leave muddy colors on edges.
Center-weighted meanAn average that counts the center more.
Dominant-color weighted meanAverages only the largest color group. For small sprites with many edges.
SettingDetails
Ignore cell border (%)How much of each cell's border to ignore (default 20%). Raise it for wide blur.
Transparency threshold (%)A cell less painted than this becomes transparent (default 50%).
Color snap stepHow close colors must be to count as one (default 20).

Most frequent color and Dominant-color weighted mean pick the color covering most of each cell; the averages make muddy mixes.

6. Palette method

The ③ Mode of ② 6. Palette is how colors are reduced. It starts at OKLCH grouping.
