At the same 11 colors, only k-means keeps the white highlight.

MethodHow it reducesColor count
OKLCH groupingMerges colors close in lightness, intensity and hue (Group within). Good for many shading steps.Set by the range
Distance groupingMerges colors within Group radius (default 0.04) of each other.Set by the radius
k-means (OKLab)Splits evenly into the chosen number of colors. Colors of small areas can vanish.2–64 or Auto
Median cutHalves the colors repeatedly; fast.2–64 or Auto
Existing paletteUses only the colors of the palette chosen in Palette.That palette
Keep original colorsKeeps the cell colors without reducing.As many as cells have
  • ⑥ Only the boxes the chosen method uses are shown (e.g. the Group within row for OKLCH grouping).
  • Automatic color count picks enough colors that small but noticeable ones (such as a red scarf) survive.
  • Don't optimize next to the method makes optimization keep the method and vary only its values.
  • With One palette for all frames (on by default), all frames use the same colors.

Palette colors and edge cells

SettingDetails
Palette colorsThe color each group uses (table below).
Edge cell weightCounts blurred edge cells less (default 0.25).
Snap near-black to one outline colorOn by default: merges near-black colors into one outline color.
Palette colors valueResult
Original colorsThe color actually used most in the group (default). Whites and golds stay crisp.
Average colorsThe group's average color. For smooth gradients.

④ Color distance and ⑤ Prefer shade family are covered in Color distance and dithering.

Color distance and dithering

Look here when shading turns an odd color or gradients break into steps. Color distance decides which color is closest; dithering mixes two colors in a checker pattern.

Color distance

Used when cells pick palette colors and for pinned and excluded colors. The score uses a separate scoring color distance.

OKLab easily turns greys and dark gold into other colors; the other two keep each color family.

DistanceCharacter
OKLCh hue-weightedDefault. Weighs hue (red, blue and so on) more, so shading doesn't turn into another color.
CIEDE2000A standard formula tuned to the eye. Separates grey and blue shades well but is a little slower.
OKLabTreats every difference the same. Fastest; for images with few, distinct colors.
Hue weight (number box next to it)How many times hue differences count (default 2). 1 equals OKLab.

Keeping shades and hues

  • Prefer shade family (on by default): prefers a lighter or darker shade of the same color.
  • Keep distinct hues (on by default): adds a noticeable color, such as small ear shading, to the palette if it would turn into another color. Tune it with Min chroma, Min area and Hue diff (°).
  • Optimizing the Palette step tries both on and off.

Dithering

Dither (default None) mixes two palette colors in a checker pattern to make colors the palette lacks.

None gives flat fills, 2×2 a coarse pattern, 4×4 smoother blends.

DitherResult
NonePaints each cell with the nearest color (default).
Bayer 2×2Mixes with a small pattern; for small sprites.
Bayer 4×4Mixes more smoothly with a larger pattern; for wide gradients.

Don't optimize next to dithering (on by default) keeps optimization from changing it. A pattern can raise the score yet look noisy up close.

Cleanup and outlines

Look here when outlines are thick or bumpy, specks remain, or an animation flickers. Step 7 fixes outlines and specks; step 8 fixes flicker.

7. Cleanup

Items turned on in ① 7. Cleanup apply in the table's order.

SettingInitial valueWhat it does
Remove orphan pixels · Max contrastOn · 0.18Removes lone pixels that differ from their surroundings. High-contrast dots such as pupils stay.
Fill 1-px holesOnFills 1-px holes closed on all sides.
Fit outline to 1 pxBy shrinkingHow the outline becomes 1 px (table below).
Use the source outlineOn③ Thins only what is outline on the source, so black clothes and hair aren't shaved.
Vector simplify (px) · Keep small blobs (cells)0.6 · 6For Redraw as vector: how much to straighten the line, and which small blobs (such as eyes) to leave alone.
Clean lines and curves · Corner angle (°)On · 40° (10–170°)For Redraw as vector: tidies the line into straight lines and curves. A lower angle keeps more corners.
OutlineAs isHow dark edge pixels are tidied (table below).
Reduce L-corner doubles on outlinesOffRemoves doubled pixels at L-shaped corners.

Off shaves the black shorts where they touch the gap; on keeps them.

Choosing ④ Redraw as vector reveals ⑤ Clean lines and curves and ⑥ Corner angle (°).

As is leaves the gaps, Unify only evens the color, and Ensure closed also closes the gaps.

OutlineResultUse it when
As isLeaves the outline alone (the initial value).Colored outlines on purpose, or no outline at all.
Unify to the darkest colorMakes the dark edge one darkest color.The outline color is blotchy.
Ensure a closed 1-px outlineEvens the color, then adds 1 px where the outline is missing to close it.The outline has gaps.

Fit outline to 1 px

② Fit outline to 1 px is how a thick outline becomes 1 px.

ModeWhat it doesUse it when
By shrinkingDefault. Sets the cell size to the outline thickness, scaling the sprite down.The outline is drawn as thick as one source cell.
Thin outline onlyKeeps the cell size and peels doubled outline from the inside (up to 3 layers).The outline is drawn two or more cells thick.
Redraw as vectorKeeps the cell size and redraws the outline as a 1-px line.The outline is bumpy or thick in places.
OffDoesn't fit the outline.You want the source thickness.

On the left the steps are uneven; on the right lines and curves run evenly.

8. Frame consistency

⑦ Match neighbor frames (reduce flicker) (off by default) evens out pixels that differ slightly from the frames before and after, reducing flicker. Pixels differing more than Max contrast (default 0.12) stay.

Auto optimize

Use it to find a better result without adjusting settings one by one. It tries different values for each checked preset and keeps the highest-scoring result.

Starting and reading the result

PartWhat it does
① Auto optimizeStarts the search.
② Settings buttonOpens the Auto Optimize Settings window to choose the presets to run.
③ Best-score graphPlots the best score after each try.
④ Continue optimizingContinues a candidate that stopped because time or tries ran out.
⑤ RevertRestores the settings from before the run.
⑥ One-line summaryShows the time taken and the score change.
⑦ Blue line on the tileWhy it stopped, and the score: Optimized (no more gain), Time limit, Trial limit or Cancelled.

When it finishes, each preset keeps one best tile. For saved presets, the values found are written into them.

While it runs

  • The button turns into the red ⑧ Cancel, and ⑨ the progress line shows the tries and best score.
  • ⑩ The running tile shows the best so far; ⑪ waiting tiles show Waiting.
  • ⑧ Cancel stops only the current preset. To stop all, right-click > Cancel all.
  • Replacing the source stops the run. Closing the window doesn't.
  • With many frames, only some are used to search; the result is then applied to all frames.

With ⑫ Optimize on for 7. Cleanup and ⑬ Redraw as vector with Clean lines and curves, optimization also tries ⑭ Corner angle (°).

How it searches and when it stops

PhaseWhat happens
Coarse sweepScans values broadly.
ClimbingMakes small changes near the best spots.
Restarts (passes)When it stops improving, starts again from other good results.
  • The same image and settings always give the same result when stopped by tries.
  • It stops when the tries or seconds in 0. Optimize criteria run out. With both at 0 it runs until it stops improving.
  • The Optimize checks in the step headers decide which steps are varied.

Auto Optimize Settings window

Open it with the gear button in the preset bar or the settings button next to Auto optimize. Choose the presets to run, and rename, copy or delete presets.

ItemWhat it does
① Preset checklistChecked presets run in Auto optimize and get a place in the candidate row. The same checks decide which tiles appear when a source is loaded and which presets run when the frame split is applied.
② Rename · Copy · DeleteAct on the preset selected in the list (disabled while running). Copy makes name copy 1 right after it, from the current values if it's the preset open on the left. Delete asks first; the last preset can't be deleted.
③ Trial historyShows the selected preset's last run: the top 50 trials by score and the values they changed.
④ Auto optimize on loadOff by default. When on, every source that comes in auto-optimizes all checked presets.
⑤ ResetAfter confirmation, restores the preset list, values and checks to a fresh install and deletes your own presets and the trial history. Can't be undone.

Rename asks for ⑥ the preset name, and asks again if another preset has it.

Preset values are edited in the settings column; choices here are saved when you close the window. The Presets tab in Preferences can also restore built-in presets.

Score terms and weights

Look here to compare candidates by number. The score (0–100) adds up terms such as how close the result is to the source and how clean the outline is, by their weights.

Score terms

TermWhat it measuresDefault weight
FidelityHow closely it matches the source. Higher follows the source more.4
Orphan pixelsHow few lone pixels there are.1
Outline gapsHow few gaps the outline has. Set to 0 for art without outlines.0.5
Color countWhether the color count fits the target. Ignored when the target is Auto.0.5
Frame consistencyHow little colors flicker between frames.1
Grid fitHow well the cell lines match the source.2
1-px outlineWhether the outline is 1 px. Lower it if you want thick outlines.4
Major color shiftWhether large areas such as hair and clothes kept their color.2
Block repetitionWhether the result isn't needlessly large.2
Line cleannessWhether outline steps are even (jumps like 3·1·3 lose points).1

Clean up and Auto optimize look for the highest score. The selected candidate's per-term scores appear in the breakdown under the preview.

Score weights

Each term, such as ③ Line cleanness, has ④ a weight (0–10); 0 ignores it. The Faithful to source and Clean pixels presets use different weights.

Optimize criteria

Change these when Auto optimize takes too long or the score picks results that look worse. ① 0. Optimize criteria sets how long to search and what counts as better.

Settings

SettingDetails
TrialsMaximum tries (0–10000, 0 = no limit).
SecondsMaximum time per preset (0–600 seconds, 0 = no limit). Built-in presets start at 300 seconds, settings without a preset at 120.
Frames to sampleWith more frames than this, only some are used to search faster (default 5, 0 = all).
Target colors · Auto · StrictMore colors than this lowers the score. Auto (default) ignores it; Strict also penalizes too few.
Fidelity · Cell color · Scoring color distance (major shift, cell match)How Fidelity is measured (How Fidelity is measured).
Score weights (0 = ignore)Each score term's weight (Score terms and weights).

Per-step Optimize

Only steps with ② Optimize on are varied; the others use their values as set.

StepValues tried
1. BackgroundRemove sticker border
3. Pixel gridCell size and cell lines.
5. Cell colorCell color method and its values.
6. PalettePalette method, color count, color distance, dithering and more.
7. CleanupCleanup on/off and the outline fit mode.

Good to know

  • The run stops at whichever of tries and seconds comes first.
  • With both at 0 it runs until it stops improving; the cancel button stops it.
  • A palette method or dithering with Don't optimize on isn't changed.
  • The cell size isn't made smaller without reason.
  • With a fixed output size the cell size isn't varied. Optimize grid fit first finds the cell layout first.

How Fidelity is measured

Change these when the score penalizes dithering or stray dots in outlines too much or too little. They set how Fidelity, the biggest score term, is measured.

How Fidelity compares

The Fidelity box sets how the result is compared with the source.

ValueHow it comparesSuits
Cell by cellCompares each result pixel with its own cell's color only (default).Ordinary pixel-art characters.
Blended with neighborsCompares as seen from a distance, blended with neighbors. Dither patterns don't lose points.Dithered art, or art with wide smooth areas.
Center weightedOn by default: center pixels of each cell count more.Can be off for sources with little blur.

Representative cell color

The Cell color box sets which color a source cell counts as. Shown only with Cell by cell.

A cell crossed by the outline becomes skin with Dominant group but muddy brown with Cell average.

ValueThe source cell's colorSuits
Dominant groupGroups similar colors and uses the largest group's color (default).Most art with distinct outlines and fills.
Cell averageUses the average of all colors in the cell.Art that blends softly inside each cell.
Group rangeHow different colors can be within one group (default 0.06). Shown only for Dominant group.Raise it for noisy sources; lower it for closely spaced shading.

Scoring color distance

The scoring color distance only affects the score, never the result colors. It is used by Fidelity and Major color shift.

OKLCh hue-weighted splits a color difference into lightness (L), intensity (C) and hue (H). The L, C and H weights appear only then.

For art with many greys, metals or shadows, CIEDE2000 or OKLCh hue-weighted is safer.

DistanceHow it measuresSuits
OKLabTreats every difference the same.Few, distinct colors.
CIEDE2000A standard formula tuned to the eye; a little slower.Lots of grey, white or blue.
OKLCh hue-weightedPenalizes a change of hue more (default weights 1 · 1 · 2).Characters where different hues touch, such as skin and a red shirt.
L weightHow many times lightness differences count (default 1).Raise it to keep shading steps.
C weightHow many times intensity differences count (default 1).Raise it to stop colors going dull.
H weightHow many times hue differences count (default 2).Raise it to stop skin and red cloth merging.

Output

Use this to insert the selected result into a document. Apply inserts it where the Output step says; Add to new tab always makes a new document.

SettingWhat it does
① New layer in the current document (from the current frame)Inserts a new layer from the current frame (one undo step). Unavailable with no open document.
② New documentOpens a new document the size of the result in a new tab.
③ Frame duration (ms)Length of new frames (10–10000 ms).
④ Also save the colors as a new paletteAlso saves the colors used as a new palette.

Buttons at the bottom

  • ⑤ Reset presets: keeps the source and resets the candidate row.
  • ⑥ Grow to image size (off by default): grows the canvas when it is smaller than the result.
  • ⑦ Add to new tab: makes a new document of the result size.
  • ⑧ Apply: inserts as the output setting says. Double-clicking a tile also works.

Good to know

  • A single-frame result enters transform mode. Move it and press Enter, or Esc to keep it as inserted.
  • Parts outside the canvas aren't cut off. Enter doesn't apply.
  • If options in the AI (experimental) step are on, the chat opens after inserting.

Refining with AI (experimental)

Use this to ask AI to refine the result further. After inserting, the AI chat is filled with a request and images; you press send yourself.

Settings

SettingWhat it does
① Touch up with AI after applyingWhen on, applying opens the chat with the source and ② the prompt filled in.
② PromptThe request put in the chat. The default asks to fix outlines, the face and stray pixels while keeping the palette and size. The button next to it restores the default.
③ ModelChoose Claude or Codex from ⑦ the list.
④ Add the source to AI referencesAdds the source to the AI references when applying.
⑤ Ask AI to rank candidatesAttaches the source and the top ⑥ Candidates to send candidates, and fills in a request to rank them and suggest better settings (not sent).

Layer tree · skins

Split a character into child layers of different sizes, such as head, body and legs, under one layer. You export both the combined animation and per-layer sprites. Each layer can hold several sets of outfits or gear (skins), and a skin preset switches several layers at once. You can swap the same combinations in Unity.

The workflow and where each step is explained.

StepWhat you doDetails
1. Build the treeCreate a parent layer for a character or a set of gear, then add children or move drawn layers under it.Creating child layers · Nesting and un-nesting layers
2. DrawDraw each layer at its own size in each frame. Effects go on that layer only or on it combined with its children.The layer tree · Drawing (layer with its own size selected) · Layer effects in the tree
3. LayoutMove layers by hand or turn on auto layout, and place them per frame if needed.Arranging (empty layer selected) · Changing positions per frame · Auto layout
4. SkinsAdd another set of art to a layer and choose the skin each layer wears.Skins · Empty skins and duplicating · Switching skins per layer
5. Skin presetsSave a skin combination across layers as a preset, switch it at once, and stack presets.Skin settings window · Skin presets
6. ExportOn save, produce the frame sheet, layer atlas and Sprite Library for Unity.Export: frame sheet and layer atlas · Exporting skins · Unity Sprite Library

The layer tree

Use this when you want to draw a character in separate layers, such as head, body and legs. Put a layer under another and it becomes a child; layers joined this way form the layer tree.

Structure

  • ② Child layers go under a ① parent layer. A parent that only holds children, with no picture, is an empty layer. Tilemap layers can't join a tree.
  • ③ Each child has its own size (e.g. Head 47×40), and ④ you draw it at that size in each frame.
  • ⑤ The position is where the layer's top-left corner sits in the frame. Usually all frames share it, but you can vary it per frame.

How a parent affects its children

ItemBehavior
Drawing orderChildren draw above their parent. Under the same parent, the layer higher in the timeline draws on top.
Show · lockHiding or locking a parent also hides or locks every layer under it.
OpacityThe parent and its children fade together as one picture.
MovingMoving a parent moves its children too.
Layer effectsCan apply to that layer only or to it combined with its children (Layer effects).
SkinsChosen per layer. To change several layers together, use a skin preset.

The canvas depends on the selected layer

Selected layerCanvas
Empty layerShows the whole frame; drag children into place (Arranging).
Layer with its own sizeYou draw that layer at its own size, with the others shown (Drawing).
Normal layer outside a treeYou draw at canvas size. Putting it in a tree gives it its own size.

Creating child layers

Create empty layers and child layers from the menu or the timeline's right-click menu. To nest a layer you already drew, move it under another layer.

Adding from the menu

Frame·Layer > ① New empty layer (Ctrl+Shift+N) creates a picture-less parent layer right above the current layer. ② New child layer… (Shift+P) adds a top child to the current layer.

Adding from the timeline

Right-click a layer name in the timeline and, under New, choose ③ New empty layer (directly above) or ④ New child layer… (top child). Tilemap layers can't have children.

New child layer dialog

Set ⑤ the name and ⑥ the size. The size starts at the canvas size; change it later with Layer size.

A new child layer goes in as the parent's top child, empty, in the middle of the canvas. A duplicate name gets a number. Adding one under a normal layer gives that layer its own size, with its drawing kept in place.

Nesting and un-nesting layers

Use this to put a drawn layer under another layer or take it back out. Layers under it move along, and one undo reverts it.

Dragging names and Shift

Drag a name in the timeline and ① the layer floats along while a gap opens where it will land. Hold Shift and drop on another layer to make it ② that layer's top child.

How you dragResult
Dragging up or down without ShiftOnly reorders within the same parent.
Dragging to the leftEvery 20px to the left moves it one level out of its parent.
Dropping on another layer's row with Shift heldMakes it that layer's top child; this works at any depth.
A layer under itself, a tilemap layer, a locked layerCan't drop there (not-allowed cursor).

To take layers out, right-click and choose Move out of parent. They land right above their former parent.

When a normal layer joins the tree

  • A normal layer gets its own size, just big enough for its drawings. The drawing stays where it was.
  • An empty layer, or one with an effect that draws by itself such as Text, gets the whole canvas size.
  • Its 9-slice border (the stretchable edge) starts empty, and its pivot keeps pointing at the same spot.

When a layer leaves its parent

  • A layer taken out doesn't turn back into a normal layer. Its size, position, skins and children stay.
  • If it has a drawing, it stays in the layer atlas.

Viewing in the timeline

Read this to inspect, collapse or expand the tree in the timeline. Child layers appear as indented rows under their parent.

Parent rows and child rows

  • ① The triangle collapses and expands child rows. The state is saved in the file.
  • ② A collapsed parent's cells show small pictures including its children. Expanded, it shows only its own picture.
  • ③ Child rows step inward per level and connect to the parent with orange lines. The layer size shows after the name.
  • ④ A layer with more than one skin shows a skin chip with its current skin. Click it to switch (Switching skins).
  • ⑤ Hiding or locking a parent also hides or locks the layers under it; their eye icons look dimmed.

Other indicators

  • Dragging a name reorders it within its parent; a dragged parent takes its children along. To move to another parent, see Nesting and un-nesting.
  • With a child layer selected, the top of the timeline shows its size and current-frame position, e.g. 47×40 @7,4.
  • A pivot mark at a cel's top left means that frame has its own position or pivot.
  • Merge down works only between normal layers outside a tree, each with one skin.
  • From level 6, a depth number before the name replaces the lines.
  • Alt+click an eye or lock to keep only that layer; its parents and children stay too.
  • Collapsing and expanding isn't recorded in undo history.

Arranging (empty layer selected)

Select an empty layer to see the whole frame on the canvas and drag its child layers into place. Moves here apply to every frame.

Selecting and moving

  • ① Hover a child to see a dotted border with its name and size. Where layers overlap, the selected layer comes first, otherwise the topmost one painted there.
  • Click to ② select a child. Click again to cycle through the layers beneath it. Click an empty area or press Esc to deselect.
  • Drag and drop to move it. Every frame moves by the same amount; per-frame differences stay.
  • Arrow keys move it 1px, Shift+arrow keys 8px.
  • Double-click to switch to drawing that layer.

Locked layers can't be moved. To move a layer in one frame only, see Changing positions per frame.

When the parent uses auto layout

If the parent uses auto layout, you're asked This layer's parent uses auto layout. Turn off auto layout and move it? before moving. After confirming, drag again; with the arrow keys or Position… the move happens right away.

Pivot markers

  • ③ Child pivots are blue dots; ④ the large orange marker is the parent layer's pivot, which the Pivot tool edits.
  • Click the text of the canvas chip at the timeline's top left to target the canvas pivot (the sheet and Unity export pivot).
  • Turn on the Pivot view in the top bar to show every visible pivot with labels; with it off, they show only while the Pivot tool is selected (Canvas).

Changing positions per frame

Use this to move a layer in some frames only, like a swinging sword, without redrawing it. Each frame adds its own distance to the base position shared by all frames.

Moving in one frame only

  1. Select the layer, go to the frame, and remove any selection.
  2. Enter Transform mode with Edit > Transform (rotate/size)… (Ctrl+T).
  3. Drag inside the box. Leave size and rotation alone; right-drag to keep the pivot.
  4. Apply (Enter). Repeat on each other frame.

How each method moves frames

MethodFrames moved
Dragging or arrow keys in the empty layer view, the layer's right-click Position…Moves the base position, so every frame moves equally.
Moving the layer without resizing in Transform mode (Ctrl+T) with no selection, then applyingMoves that frame only. The pivot moves along; right-drag to leave it in place.
Moving or resizing the layer in Transform whole layer (Alt+Shift+C) and applyingApplies to every frame. Moving alone moves the base position.
Baking an effect such as Shake with Move pivot along onEach frame's position moves by as much as the effect moved it.
Turning off From current frame on in the Pivot toolClears per-frame places from the current frame to the end, back to the base position.

Checking and undoing

  • Such cels show a pivot mark at the top left in the timeline.
  • The layer's right-click Position… shows the current frame's position. Changing it there moves every frame equally.

Edits that keep per-frame positions

The distances are saved in the file and survive duplicating frames or layers, resizing the layer, auto layout, resizing, flipping or rotating the canvas, merging files and taking a layer out of its parent.

The layer atlas doesn't depend on position, so it doesn't change. Moved places show on the canvas, the preview and the frame sheet.

Drawing (layer with its own size selected)

Select ① a layer with its own size and ② its cell appears with a border; draw inside it with any drawing tool.

Other layers show in drawing order: the parent below, children on top.

To dim the other layers, turn on Focus in the top bar (`).

Changing size and position

  • Resize: Frame·Layer > ③ Layer size… or right-click the layer → ④ Layer size…. Pick where the image goes with ⑥ the anchor. It applies to every frame and skin, and drawn pixels stay in place.
  • On the canvas: with such a layer selected, Ctrl+Alt+C opens Layer size mode instead of canvas size mode. There is no flipping, and Fit all artwork fits the layer's artwork.
  • Set the position numerically: right-click the layer → ⑤ Position…. It shows the current frame's position; changing it moves every frame and the children.
  • To move or resize every frame on the canvas, use Transform whole layer (Alt+Shift+C). To move one frame only, see Changing positions per frame.

Pivot and 9-slice

  • ⑦ The Pivot tool (K) and the 9-slice tool (S) apply to the selected layer.
  • The pivot and 9-slice border are shared by all skins and exported as the layer atlas sprites' Pivot and Border.

Layer effects in the tree

Read this to decide whether an effect such as an outline goes on one layer or the whole character. On a layer with children, each effect in the effects dialog has ① Apply to.

Differences by target

Applies toCanvas, preview, frame sheetLayer atlasBaking
This layer only (also any layer without children)That layer's own picture.Included. The sprite grows to fit effects that spread outward, like outlines.Possible; the layer grows to fit.
Combined with childrenThe picture combined with its children.Not included (frame sheet and screen only), nor in the Unity prefab.Not possible ("Combined with children" effects can't be baked — switch them to "This layer only" or apply them to the child layers.).

Editing a layer with effects hidden

With ③ Effects on, ② the drawing and pivot appear as moved by the effect.

Turn ⑤ Effects off to edit ④ the original drawing and pivot. The preview keeps showing effects.

Baking effects into the art

  • Baking draws the effect into the layer's pixels. Use Apply permanently in the effects dialog or Apply effect permanently in the effect row's right-click menu. Only This layer only effects can be baked, and not on locked layers.
  • Baking an effect like Shake with Move pivot along on turns the motion into that frame's layer position, not into the art.
  • If baking makes the layer bigger, a parent with auto layout rearranges its children.

Auto layout

Auto layout lines up a layer's direct children horizontally, vertically or in a grid, and redoes it when they change. Right-click a layer with children and choose ① Auto layout….

Settings

Adjust the values while watching ② the preview, then click ③ Apply to turn it on.

SettingDescription
④ DirectionHorizontal · vertical · grid (set column count)
⑤ AlignTop/center/bottom or left/center/right
⑥ DistributionStart · center · end · even
⑦ Spacing · paddingIn px
⑧ OrderTop child first · bottom child first
⑨ AreaWhole canvas by default, or set X, Y, W, H
⑩ Hidden children keep their placeOn by default: toggling visibility doesn't move the others. When off, only visible children are laid out.

While it is on

  • ⑪ A diamond appears on the layer's row; click it for the settings. The right-click menu shows Auto layout settings… checked.
  • Children are laid out again when you add or delete one or change sizes, order or the canvas size.
  • Moving a child by hand asks to turn auto layout off. Per-frame distances add on top.
  • Turn it off with Turn off auto layout in the right-click menu or Turn off auto layout in the dialog. Children stay where they are.

Skins

A skin is one set of art for a layer. Each layer has a list of skins, and ① only one is shown.

Skin rules

  • Skins of the same layer replace each other.
  • ② Different layers stack in layer order.
  • Only the artwork differs. Size, position, pivot, 9-slice and children are shared.
  • Skin names are unique within a layer. Tilemap layers have no skins.

Example: body, hair, armor

Put hair and armor layers over a body layer and pick a skin on each to build a character. Leave the gear's first skin, "none", empty.

Layer (bottom → top)SkinsPurpose
Bodydefault · muscular · zombieSwaps the whole body.
Hairnone · short · longStacks on the body.
Armornone · leather · plateStacks on top.

Creating skins

Right-click ③ a layer and choose from ④ Add skin. Name it, and the layer wears the new skin right away.

ItemSkin created
Empty skin…An empty skin
Duplicate current drawing…A copy of the current skin's art
With children… (when the layer has children)Adds an empty skin of that name to the layer and all its descendants, and saves them as a skin preset of that name

You can also create skins in the Skin settings window. Rename, delete and reorder them from the right-click Skin: name submenu or that window.

Empty skins and duplicating

A skin shows only its own art; undrawn cels stay empty instead of borrowing from the first skin. To start from the same art, use Duplicate.

New skin (empty)

Adding ① the empty skin to Props with With children… and wearing it blanks the Gem and Sparkle cels; ② the canvas and ③ the preview show only Body.

Duplicate

Creating ④ the skin Copy with Duplicate current drawing… copies the art, so ⑤ the gem and sparkle remain. After that, the two skins are edited separately.

Where empty cels stay empty

Empty cels are never filled with first-skin art anywhere: canvas, preview, export or AI.

Switching skins per layer

Each layer wears its own skin. Switching ① only Head to zombie changes ② just the head; ③ Body stays knight.

Where to switch

Click the skin chip after a name for ④ the skin list; picking one applies it right away. ⑤ Skin settings… opens the Skin settings window.

You can also switch in the Skins row below the preview: ⑥ the preset box applies a skin preset, and ⑦ each layer's skin box picks its skin. ⑧ Settings… opens the Skin settings window.

Use ⑨ Skin: name in a layer's right-click menu to pick a skin, or to rename, delete or reorder it. Previous/next skin is Alt+[ / Alt+].

Switching skins isn't recorded in undo history, but it is saved in the file, so the document is marked modified.

Skin settings window

Shows skin presets and each layer's skins as cards for editing. Open it from ① the skin chip menu, ② Frame·Layer > Skin settings… or ③ Settings… in the skin row.

Layout and cards

④ Skin presets shows the preset cards; ⑤ Layer skins has a row, indented like the tree, for each layer with more than one skin. Opening it from a chip or menu scrolls to that layer's row.

⑥ A skin card is the thumbnail and name of a skin; the first is marked (default) and the current one is highlighted. Click to apply; double-click to apply and close. ⑦ New skin adds an empty skin, ⑧ Duplicate a copy of the current one.

Card menu and order

  • In a card's right-click menu (⑨) choose Apply, Pick for a preset, Duplicate, Rename…, Delete or Move forward/back. Drag cards to reorder; the front one becomes the first (default) skin. Undo (Ctrl+Z) and redo (Ctrl+Y) work here too.
  • A long row of cards scrolls sideways; you can also drag an empty spot between cards.
  • Close it with OK (Esc). Card clicks have already applied their skins, so this only closes the window.

Skin presets

A skin preset is a named set of skins, one per layer. It changes only the layers it contains, so presets can be stacked (trousers + top).

Registering

In the Skin settings window, Ctrl/⌘+click cards to pick one per layer (① ②); click again to unpick. Press ③ Save as preset… and enter a name. ④ shows how many are picked; Clear picks unpicks all.

Pressing ③ with nothing picked saves the current skin of every layer with more than one skin. Add skin > With children… also creates a preset of the same name.

Applying and editing

Click ⑤ a preset card to apply it; double-click to apply and close. ⑥ Right-click to Overwrite with current skins, rename, delete or reorder.

Presets follow layers and skins through renames. Deleted entries are skipped when applying, with a warning on the card. Saving and editing presets can be undone; applying isn't recorded.

Export: frame sheet and layer atlas

Read this when saving a layer-tree character for a game. It can produce two images; turn each on or off in ③ Output.

The two outputs

  • ① Frame sheet: an animation sheet with all layers combined in each frame.
  • ② Layer atlas: one picture (sprite) per layer and frame, collected in one image. If ④ the name is empty, it is <frame sheet name>_parts.

Layer atlas sprites

  • Only layers in a tree go in, one per drawn frame, in timeline order. Normal layers outside a tree and tilemaps are left out.
  • Each sprite's Pivot is the layer pivot and its Border is the 9-slice border. Only the layer's own effects are included.
  • Layout, sorting and cell names follow ⑤ the common settings. Old files no longer produced go to the app trash (Files).
  • With Unity as the engine, .meta files are written too, and you can produce Prefab (layers placed) (objects nested like the tree), animation clips and a Controller (Export).

Exporting skins

Use this to export several skins (outfits) of a character as images. Choose them under Skin in the common export settings.

SettingResult
① Current skins (default)Exports one set with each layer's current skin.
② Selected skinsExports the skins picked in ③ Choose skins into ④ one image or ⑤ separate files (<sheet>@<skin>.png).

Choose skins dialog

  • Check ⑥ the layers and skins to include, indented like the layer tree.
  • Unchecking a layer leaves out its children too. This also applies when exporting current skins.
  • For the frame sheet, each checked skin adds a set with only that layer changed. First skins aren't listed.
  • Each skin preset checked in ⑦ adds a set wearing it to the frame sheet.

Cell names

In one image, cells are named <name>@<skin>, e.g. Head@knight. In the layer atlas the first skin keeps plain names, and other skins add only cels they drew.

If you don't select the first skin, its sprites are left out.

Unity Sprite Library

Turn on ① Sprite Library (Unity only) in the layer atlas section to create assets for Unity 2D Animation. You switch skins by swapping one asset.

What a library contains

  • ② A default library <layer atlas name>.spriteLib (first skins) plus one per skin preset, <layer atlas name>@<preset>.spriteLib. Only presets containing an exported layer or skin get a file.
  • Category = layer (same names get 2, 3 …), Label = frame (0, 1 … or idle_0 …).
  • All libraries share Categories and Labels; only the sprites differ. A preset library uses the preset's skins for its layers and first skins for the rest, so one animation works for every skin.
  • Cels not drawn in that skin get an empty sprite.

Using it in Unity

  1. Put a Sprite Library component on the top object with the default library.
  2. Give each layer object a Sprite Resolver (Category = layer) and keyframe its Label (frame).
  3. To switch skins, swap the component's asset for a preset library. To switch one layer alone, add another Sprite Library to its object.

Importing old files and PNGs

Files from older versions and PNG sheets with mixed cell sizes open as a layer tree.

Older files with groups

Groups from older versions (file format 6 or earlier) become picture-less parent layers, and their parts become child layers. Group effects become Combined with children effects, and group skins and outfit presets become skins and skin presets; you're told when the file opens.

Saving writes the new file format (7), which older versions of the app can't open.

Atlas files from older versions

An old atlas .chickxel becomes a picture-less parent 부위 with one child layer per sprite. Unity guids and spriteIDs carry over.

PNG sheets with cells of different sizes

It is imported as ① an empty layer named after the sheet with ② one child layer per cell, each at its original place. ③ Frame sheet export starts off.

Tilemap layers

A tilemap layer builds a game map by placing tileset tiles on a grid of cells. It always sits at the top level, and every frame uses the same map. Compositing, preview, export and layer effects work as for normal layers.

Your first tilemap layer starts with the built-in tileset, so you can place tiles right away. Find what you need below.

PageRead it when
Creating a tilemap layerYou add a new tilemap layer
When a tilemap layer is selectedYou want to know what changes when a tilemap layer is selected
Tile toolsYou place, erase or fill tiles, or copy cells
Tileset panelYou pick the tile or auto tile set to place
Current pickYou check what goes on which floor, or flip the brush
FloorsYou stack tiles, or hide and lock floors
Choosing and managing tilesetsYou choose another tileset, or create and delete tilesets
Using several tilesetsYou open several tilesets on one layer
Tileset settingsYou edit tiles, floors and auto tile sets in one place, or import tiles
Big tilesYou make tiles that cover several cells or rise above them, like trees
Editing tile imagesYou repaint a tile with the pixel tools
Auto tilesYou create or edit the rules of an auto tile set
Animated tilesYou make tiles that move, like waves or torches
Built-in tilesetYou want to know what's in the built-in tileset
Converting to layersYou turn a tilemap into normal image layers
Saving and tileset filesYou save a tileset to a file to share it between projects

Creating a tilemap layer

Create one from the menu or the timeline's right-click menu. An empty tilemap layer appears above the current layer, with no dialog.

From the menu

Choose Frame·Layer > ① New tilemap layer (Alt+Shift+N).

From the timeline

Or right-click a layer row or an empty spot in the timeline and choose ② New tilemap layer under New.

Tilemap layer names show a grass-block icon.

Which tileset it starts with

The new layer uses the first tileset found in this order:

  1. The first tileset of the selected layer, if it is a tilemap layer
  2. The first tileset used by a tilemap layer in the project
  3. Otherwise, a new copy of the built-in tileset

To use another tileset, choose Replace with another tileset… in the group menu (Choosing tilesets).

When a tilemap layer is selected

Selecting a tilemap layer switches the screen to placing tiles. Selecting another layer switches it back.

  • ① The toolbar shows the tile tools (Tile tools).
  • ② The Tileset panel replaces the color panel; pick tiles to place there.
  • ③ The top bar shows the current tool and how to use it.
  • ④ The view buttons on the right of the top bar switch to tilemap views (table below).
  • ⑤ The tool options show the Tiles group (Current pick).
  • ⑥ The Floors group below it chooses the floor to place on (Floors).
  • ⑦ The canvas's bottom left and the status bar show the cell, floor and tile name under the pointer.

Floor view is one split button. Its main area cycles All floors → Focus floor → Floor only; its arrow or a right-click opens the list.

Tilemap view buttons

ButtonWhat it does
Cell gridShows the tile grid, also outside the canvas (View > Tilemap cell grid).
Tile motionWhen off, animated tiles stop on screen; exports are unaffected (View > Play animated tiles).
All floors · Focus floor · Floor onlyShows only the target floor clearly, or hides the others (Floors).

Commands that don't apply

Pixel-only commands such as adding an outline, replacing colors or transforming are grayed out; their shortcuts only show a notice.

Tile tools

The tile tools use the pixel tools' shortcuts. They work cell by cell, and one drag is one undo step.

  • ① A translucent preview shows what would be placed, including neighbor changes for the auto tile brush. A locked floor shows an orange dashed outline and a lock.
  • ② Shows the cell, floor and tile name under the pointer, with a warning if the target floor is locked or hidden.

Tools

ToolKeyWhat it does
Tile brushBPlaces the tiles picked in the Tileset panel, in the same arrangement.
Auto tile brushShift+BPaints with the picked auto tile set; edges follow the neighbors (Auto tiles).
Tile eraserEDrag to erase the visible tile under the pointer; big tiles go whole. With a fixed floor, only that floor.
Tile rectangleRFills the dragged rectangle with the brush or set.
Tile fillGFills connected cells that look the same (see Fill).
Tile pickerIPicks the visible tile under the pointer, or its auto tile set, into the brush.
Cell selectMSelects a rectangle of cells (see Selecting and copying cells).

Mouse and keys

  • The right button erases.
  • Hold Alt to pick a tile from the map.
  • X, Y and Z flip the brush horizontally or vertically and turn it 90°. Turning needs square cells.

Picking and erasing visible images

The picker and cell info use the image visible under the pointer, not just its cell: picking a tree's canopy picks the tree. In position-sorted tilesets the eraser also works on rising images. Rectangle and fill still use cells.

Fill

Tile fill fills connected cells where all visible floors look the same, placing on the target floor. So you can fill just the inside of a wall drawn on another floor. Auto-tiled areas count as one region.

Selecting and copying cells

Select cells and copy them (Ctrl+C) to make them the brush. Cut (Ctrl+X), Delete (Delete) and Select all (Ctrl+A) work on cells too. Paste (Ctrl+V) switches to the tile brush.

Outside the canvas

You can place tiles outside the canvas. When you resize the canvas, outside tiles preview where they will come in, and the grid moves along.

Tileset panel

Pick what to place here. There is one group per tileset open on the layer.

  • ① Header: the arrow folds the group. The number is the tile count; double-click the name to rename. A highlight line shows when the brush comes from this tileset.
  • ② Menu button: opens the header menu, as does right-clicking the header.
  • ③ Auto tile sets: the color strip shows the join group (same color = same group). Click to paint with it, double-click to edit, right-click for a menu.
  • ④ The last plus cell creates a new auto tile set (Auto tiles).
  • ⑤ Tile list: grouped by floor, hiding tiles used by auto tile sets (view settings). Click, drag or Shift-click cells to put them in the brush as arranged. Double-click switches to the tile brush.
  • ⑥ Add tileset: opens one more tileset on this layer (Using several tilesets).

What the list shows

MarkMeaning
Thick blue outlineThe picked cells (the current brush); dashed while the auto tile brush or eraser is in use.
Light blue cellsTiles used by the picked auto tile set
Play mark at the bottom rightAn animated tile; it plays in the list too.
Yellow dot at the top rightA big tile, larger than 1×1
TooltipName, Id, default floor and footprint; frame count and time for animated tiles

Header menu

⑦ The header menu holds actions for that tileset.

ItemWhat it does
Settings…Opens the tileset settings.
Replace with another tileset…Replaces this tileset; map cells show the tiles with the same Id.
RenameRenames the tileset.
Close on this layerCloses it on this layer; grayed out if it is the only one (Several tilesets).

Tile right-click menu

Right-click a tile for the ⑧ menu.

ItemWhat it does
Settings…Opens the tileset settings with that tile selected.
Edit image…Edits the image in a new tab (Editing tile images).
Animated tile…Opens the animated tile window (Animated tiles).
Duplicate tileDuplicates the picked tile.
Merge into big tileMakes one big tile from the picked cells (Big tiles).
Delete tilesDeletes the picked tiles; if the map uses them, it asks and removes them there too.

Current pick

The Tiles group in the tool options shows what you will place and on which floor.

  • ① Pick card: the image, name and target floor. A locked or hidden floor turns it orange with a lock or closed eye.
  • ② Parts: the tiles of the auto tile set. Click one to place just that tile by hand, to replace a shape the set picked. Click the set again to return to the auto tile brush.
  • ③ Flip horizontally or vertically, turn 90° (square cells only), or reset. Keys work too (Tile tools).
  • ④ Random: with several tiles in the brush, each cell gets one at random.

③ and ④ show only with the tile brush, rectangle and fill.

Floors

Tile floors are sets of cells in a tilemap layer. The same floor overwrites; another floor stacks. Floors higher in the list draw on top.

  • ① Auto (default): tiles go to their default floor, auto tiles to their set's floor.
  • ② Click a floor row to turn Auto off and work only on that floor, including the eraser and fill. The left bar marks the target floor, orange if locked.
  • ③ Eye: a hidden floor is also left out of the preview and exports.
  • ④ Lock: tile tools leave a locked floor alone.
  • ⑤ Cells placed on that floor
  • ⑥ Moves the floor up or down.
  • ⑦ Adds a floor.

Double-click a row to rename; right-click to rename or delete. Deleting a floor removes its tiles, so it asks first. Floors added from another tileset only allow visibility and lock.

Floor view

The floor view buttons affect only the canvas. Focus floor shows the target floor clearly and the rest translucent. Floor only hides the rest.

Choosing and managing tilesets

Opens from Add tileset or Replace with another tileset… in the Tileset panel. Click a row to use that tileset.

  • ① A tileset already open on the layer is marked "Open" and can't be clicked.
  • ② A tileset with an incompatible cell size can't be clicked; an orange note says why.
  • ③ The built-in tileset adds a new copy to the project each time (Built-in tileset).
  • ④ Opens the tileset manager.

Tileset manager

Lists every tileset in the project; create and delete them here. Each action is one undo step.

  • ⑤ Editing the name renames the tileset right away.
  • ⑥ Cell size, tile count and usage (Open · another layer · not used)
  • ⑦ Saves a tileset file (.chickxel-tileset) (Saving and tileset files).
  • ⑧ Only a tileset no layer uses can be deleted.
  • ⑨ Set a name and cell size and press Create for an empty tileset (floors: Ground, Decor, Top).
  • ⑩ Imports a tileset file from another project.

Using several tilesets

One tilemap layer can have several tilesets open. Tiles from any of them go onto the same layer.

  • ① First tileset: the layer's floor list starts from its floors.
  • ② Each further tileset has its own group and header menu.
  • ③ Add tileset opens more. The cell size must match the first tileset.

The ④ purple crate and ⑤ pot come from the second tileset but sit on the same Decor floor as the first tileset's ladder.

Floors and groups

Floors of further tilesets are matched as below. Auto tile sets with the same join group connect across tilesets.

Floor of another tilesetWhere it goes
The first tileset has a floor with the same nameShares that floor.
Terrain floors (water, sand, dirt, grass, paving)Gathered into the first tileset's Ground floor, still drawn in their own floor order.
Other floors (walls, objects, overhead and so on)Added in that tileset's order after the shared floors. Their order, name and deletion are fixed, and empty ones are hidden from the list.

If any open tileset sorts by position, the whole layer does.

Closing a tileset on the layer

Use Close on this layer in the group menu. Cells placed from it are removed too, so it asks first. The tileset stays in the project; delete it for good in the tileset manager.

Tileset settings

Open this to edit tiles' names, floors and sizes or auto tile sets. Use Settings… in a group menu or a tile's right-click menu; changes apply right away.

  • ① Pages: Tiles · Floors · Auto tiles & groups · Tileset · View
  • ② Tiles by floor. Pick several to change their default floor or delete them together.
  • ③ Edit the picked tile's name, size (Big tiles) and default floor.
  • ④ Edit image… (Editing tile images), Animated tile… (Animated tiles), Duplicate tile and Delete tiles.

The Tileset page sets the drawing order: Floor order (side view) or Position (top-down). Position adds two settings to the Tiles page.

  • Draw class: Ground, Upright (walls, trees) or Overhead (roofs). Several tiles can be changed together.
  • Baseline: where the tile meets the ground, in px from the footprint top. Buttons snap it to the footprint or image bottom.
  • Ground draws first, then Upright, then Overhead. Upright tiles sort by baseline across floors, so a crate behind a wall is hidden and one in front covers it.

The Floors page works like the floor list in the tool options (Several tilesets).

Auto tiles & groups page

  • ⑤ Edit each set's name, floor and group here. N rules… opens the set editor (Auto tiles). New auto tile set makes a new one.
  • ⑥ The group list. Renaming a group updates all its sets.

Tileset page

Change the name and cell size. The cell size can change only while there are no tiles.

  • ⑦ Where the tileset file is, or a note that it lives only in the project.
  • ⑧ Save tileset file… and Reload from tileset file (Saving and tileset files).
  • ⑨ Cuts a PNG sheet or the current frame into cells and adds them as tiles. Set the margin, gap and floor, and skip empty or duplicate cells if you like.

View page

⑩ How the Tileset panel list looks: split by floor, hide set tiles, show set parts (on by default) and cell size (Auto, 16–64 px). Applies to every project.

Big tiles

Each tile has a footprint, the cells it covers on the map. A big tile, larger than 1×1, replaces the tiles it overlaps on its floor.

  • ① Footprint (width × height). The image may be larger: this tree covers 1 cell with a 32×44 px image.
  • ② Image offset: px from the footprint's top left to the image's top left
  • ③ Align image bottom: aligns the image bottom with the footprint bottom so it rises above.

Merging cells into a big tile

Drag over cells in the tile list, right-click and choose ④ Merge into big tile to make a new big tile (the originals stay). It is grayed out when floor splitting or hiding rearranges the shown cells.

Editing tile images

Edit image… in a tile's right-click menu or the settings opens the image in a new tab to repaint with the pixel tools.

  • ① The tab is named "Tile image: tile name".
  • ② The canvas is the tile's footprint.
  • ③ Parts beyond the footprint show outside the canvas; anything you draw there is included.
  • ④ The tile changes only when you press Save to tile under the tab (Ctrl+S).

What Save to tile writes

  • Visible layers are merged, including outside the canvas, and cropped to the image. Layer effects are left out.
  • An animated tile opens with one frame per image; frame duration is the time per image. Up to 64.
  • Closing the tab with changes asks whether to save them to the tile.

Auto tiles

An auto tile set looks at the 8 cells around each cell and picks a tile with the matching edge. Double-click an auto tile cell or click the plus cell to open this window.

  • ① Name, floor and join group, with the sets it joins listed below.
  • ② Floors the something-there condition checks, and other floors to clear where this set is painted (locked floors excepted).
  • ③ Tile list: pick tiles and use Put picked tiles in rule N or a double-click to add them to the selected rule (blue outline).
  • ④ Auto tile rules, tried from the top. The rule used by the cell under the mouse on the preview board is outlined in orange.
  • ⑤ Build rules from a template: Row (4) · Column (4) · 4-way (16) · 3×3 (13) · 8-way (47). For a sheet in template order, use Fill picked tiles in order. New sets start from 8-way (47).
  • ⑥ Try the set on the preview board (12×9 cells). Left = paint, right = erase, Shift+left = a gray "something there" cell. Hover a cell to see its rule. The board isn't saved.

The set changes only when you press OK (one undo step).

One rule

  • ⑦ Each outer cell cycles through the conditions below; right-click cycles backwards. The center is this cell.
  • ⑧ Tiles: click to replace with the picked tiles; Ctrl+click adds (same tile: weight +1). Several tiles are chosen at random by weight, but the same cell always gets the same tile.
  • ⑨ Also match flipped or 90°-turned shapes, placing the tile the same way (rotation needs square cells).
  • ⑩ Move earlier or later (higher first), duplicate, delete

Right-click a tile to increase or decrease its weight, Set weight… (1 or more) or remove it.

Conditions

MarkConditionMatches when
EmptyDon't careAlways.
Green plusMust be thereThis set or one of its join group is there.
Red minusMust not be thereNeither this set nor its join group is there.
Orange triangleSomething thereThe same group, or any tile on the something-there floors, is there.
Blue circleNamed group thereAn auto tile whose join group matches the rule's Group field is on any floor.

How a rule is chosen

  • The first matching rule from the top wins. Put specific rules above broad ones.
  • If nothing matches, the rule whose conditions miss the least is used.
  • Rules without tiles are skipped.
  • Placing or erasing by hand re-picks nearby sets that use something there.

Join groups

Sets with the same join group connect without an edge, even on different floors (dirt and mud in the built-in tileset). With no group, a set joins only itself.

Rules using Named group there show a Group field. For example, water below can pick a waterfront cliff.

Using something there

Use something there for shapes that touch other tiles. Built-in water checks the Ground and Front floors: under stone it fills without a surface, but in front of a background wall the surface stays.

Painting

The tile rectangle and fill also paint with the picked set. To replace a chosen shape, pick a tile from parts and place it by hand.

Animated tiles

An animated tile cycles through images (waves, torches). Open it with Animated tile… in a tile's right-click menu; changes apply on OK.

  • ① Frames play left to right. The first is what the list and exports show; one frame means a still tile.
  • ② Move the selected frame earlier or later, duplicate it or remove it.
  • ③ Time per frame (ms) and the time of one loop.
  • ④ Pick tiles to add (drag for several). An animated tile adds all its frames.
  • ⑤ Add as frames (or double-click) inserts after the selected frame. Tiles of another size are skipped with a reason. Up to 64 frames.
  • ⑥ Enlarged playback preview
  • ⑦ Play/pause and the current frame number
  • ⑧ Shows the tile repeated 3×3.

On screen and in exports

  • On the canvas, preview and tile list they keep moving even without playback. Turn off Tile motion in the top bar to freeze them.
  • With several frames, the frame's time plus the screen clock is shown.
  • Exports, saving and AI use the image at each frame's start time, so results are always the same.
  • To export the motion of a one-frame document, use Converting to layers.

Built-in tileset

Use this to build a map right away, before drawing tiles. Your first tilemap layer starts with the side-view Sandbox surface & caves; the top-down Top-down village & water is also available.

  • ① Dirt and mud are in the same group, so they join without a border.
  • ② Dirt and stone are in different groups, so a border appears between them.
  • ③ Grass, snow and leaf covers painted on top of the ground hang down over it.
  • ④ Water and lava get a wavy surface where the top is open and an edge where the side is open. The waves move.
  • ⑤ A torch takes one cell, but its glow reaches the cell above. Torches and coins are animated.

Water heights

  • ⑥ Water rising one cell at a time forms a straight slope.
  • ⑦ Water one cell wide above its neighbors comes to a point.
  • ⑧ A difference of two cells or more gives a vertical edge.
  • ⑨ Next to water filling the space under a block, the surface slopes up to meet it.

Top-down village & water

A 16 px village tileset seen from above. Higher ground covers lower ground, and banks form along water. A door placed in a wall joins the walls on both sides.

This tileset draws things lower on screen in front (right). So a crate behind a wall is hidden and one in front covers it.

Walking the map in Movement test

In Movement test (F5), choose a tilemap layer as Map to walk on it. In top-down mode walls, objects and water block you, and walls hide the character behind them.

Floors and contents

Floor (top first)Contents
FrontWater and lava (no surface where they touch Ground or Front tiles)
DecorPlatforms, ladders, vines, fences and objects such as trees, flowers, rocks, torches, coins, chests and doors
CoverGrass, snow, autumn leaves
GroundDirt and mud (group "Dirt"), stone and stone with ore (group "Stone"), sand, stone brick, wood planks
Background wallDirt, stone and brick walls (group "Background wall"), wooden walls

Ground and wall sets keep their tiles in a 4×4 block at the top left of the list; grab it as a rectangle to place it at once. Turn off hiding set tiles in the view settings to see them.

Sandbox has 532 tiles of 16 px. The project gets a copy, so edit freely, and save it as a tileset file to use elsewhere.

Converting to layers

Turns a tilemap layer into a layer tree of image layers. Right-click the layer and choose ① Convert tilemap to layers (also in the Frame·Layer menu).

  • ② An empty layer of the same name takes over its visibility, lock, opacity and pivot. Layer effects become Combined with children effects.
  • ③ Each floor becomes a child layer under it (empty floors are skipped).

A position-sorted tileset produces Ground (per floor) → Upright (merged into one) → Overhead (per floor) child layers.

Animated tiles

  • Each frame gets the image at its time, so the motion is kept. Identical frames share linked cels.
  • A one-frame document first gets enough frames for every animated tile to loop (up to 240).

Saving and tileset files

Tilemaps and tilesets are saved in the project file (.chickxel). Save a tileset as a tileset file (.chickxel-tileset) to share it between projects.

  • The project keeps a copy of every tileset it holds, including closed ones.
  • Save tileset files from the Tileset page of the settings or the tileset manager.
  • Saving the project also writes changes to the tileset file.
  • Opening reads the tileset file, or uses the project copy with a notice if it's missing. Reload from tileset file rereads it any time.
  • In another project, use Import tileset file… in the tileset manager.

AI chat (Claude · Codex)

AI chat is a window where you ask Claude Code or Codex CLI, installed on this computer, to edit the open image in plain words. The AI places pixels, works with frames, layers, skins and layer effects, and generates source pictures with Codex. No API key is needed; usage counts against your own Claude or ChatGPT subscription.

CategoryPages
Getting startedWhat it can do · One-time setup: install and log in · The AI Status and Settings window · Opening the chat and its tabs · How it works
ChattingChatting · Marking up an image to fix · While the AI works: questions, notices, progress · Editing or deleting the last message · Continuing past chats · Help and example requests
Drawing and editingEdits the AI can make · Generate image → pixelate · Drawing settings · AI drawing tips and tools · Asking for layer effects · References: teach the AI your style
SafeguardsUndoing · Limiting what the AI can edit
Other AI appsUsing other AI apps · External AI connection: differences and fixes
TroubleshootingTroubleshooting

How your work is protected

  • Undo: each AI reply is one undo step (Undoing).
  • Locked layers: the AI never touches layers locked in the timeline.
  • Current file only: when on, the AI edits only the open image and can't open or create other files (Limiting what the AI can edit).
  • Saving and tools: the AI saves only when asked and uses only Chickxel's tools. It searches the web only when you ask (How it works).

What it can do

Chat with an AI inside the editor to create and edit images. Ask in plain words, like "add a black outline" (①), and the AI edits the open image directly (②).

What you can ask for

TaskExample requestDetails
Draw and editDraw a 16×16 slime character. Transparent background, 4 shades of green, dark outline.Edits the AI can make
Generate a picture → pixel art (Codex)Draw a knight with a sword.Generate image → pixelate
Animation and framesMake a 4-frame walk animation of this character and tag it walk (120 ms per frame).Edits the AI can make
Layer tree and skinsMake a character with head, arm, and leg layers under a body layer.Edits the AI can make
Layer effectsAdd a 1 px black outline to the character and a white hit flash on frames 3–4.Asking for layer effects
New effects (scripts)Make a script effect that flashes red and scatters into pieces when hit, and save it to my effects.Asking for layer effects
Draw from your referencesDraw a 32px bat like the slime in the references.References: teach the AI your style

What you need

No API key is needed. Chickxel uses Claude Code (Claude subscription) or Codex CLI (ChatGPT subscription) installed on your computer, and usage counts against that subscription. Pick the AI with the ③ tabs.

One-time setup: install and log in

Chickxel doesn't include Claude Code or Codex CLI, so install one and sign in once. The chat window's buttons do both.

Installing a CLI

  1. If the CLI is missing, click ① Install and log in via terminal.
  2. Review the commands to run (⑤) and approve them (⑥).
  3. A terminal runs the official install command, then the sign-in command. Finish the browser sign-in yourself.
  • If Node.js, which Codex needs, is missing, it's installed too (winget or the official ZIP on Windows, Homebrew or nvm on macOS).
  • If installation fails, read the error in the terminal, then use ② Copy, ③ Set path manually or ④ Check again.

Signing in

If it's installed but not signed in, click ⑩ Log in with Claude / Log in with Codex or ⑪ Log in via terminal. The signed-in account shows at the top of the window (⑫).

Updating the CLI

When the CLI you use has a new version, a notice appears above the chat input box. Press Update and confirm: a terminal updates the CLI only, and the next message uses the new version.

You stay signed in. You can't update while a reply is coming in, and there is no notice when you're offline.

Good to know

  • Chickxel finds the CLI executable and runs it directly, so you don't need to change your PATH.
  • If the saved path fails, Chickxel switches to a working CLI in another known location.
  • The Codex CLI bundled with the macOS ChatGPT app is found automatically.
  • On Windows, a command line that is too long (about 32,000 characters) isn't started, and a "command line is too long" error is shown.

The AI Status and Settings window

The AI Status and Settings window checks the CLI installation, sign-in and editor connection. It sends no model request, so it uses no AI usage.

Chickxel checks the install and login status (⑦ ready, ⑧ not installed); ⑨ Continue to AI opens the chat.

It appears first each time you open the chat window. Open it anytime with AI > Open AI Status… or Open AI status and settings in Preferences > AI Status.

Status rows

RowWhat it checks
MCP serverChecks that the MCP server inside Chickxel answers. When it works, the number of tools is shown.
CLI/versionRuns the chosen CLI and shows its version and full path. If a different executable is on the PATH, that path is shown too.
Sign-inShows whether you are signed in and the account (or sign-in method).
MCP connectionClaude Code is started with the same settings as the chat and shows ⑦ Live CLI connection passed · tool list received once its tool list arrives. On failure, the row shows where it stopped and the CLI's error (Troubleshooting). For Codex CLI it only checks that the settings can be read; the tool connection is confirmed on the first message.

Buttons

ButtonWhat it does
Install or updateShows the commands to run and, once you approve, installs or updates and signs in in a terminal. It checks again automatically afterwards.
Sign inOpens the CLI's sign-in command in a terminal, then checks again automatically.
Sign outSigns out after asking. Chickxel doesn't store passwords or tokens.
Change executableLets you pick the CLI executable to use.
Find automaticallyClears the chosen path and searches the known locations again.
Check all againChecks every row again.

Options

OptionWhat it does
Don't show againWhen on, the choice is remembered however you close the window, and the chat opens without it. It appears again when a CLI location or custom path changes.
Open in centerOn (default): the chat window opens at the center of the editor each time. Off: it opens where you last left it.

Opening the chat and its tabs

There are three ways to open the AI chat window. If it is already open, the same command brings it to the front.

  • ① The AI chat button on the right of the bar below the menu
  • Menu ② AI > ③ AI chat
  • Ctrl+L (shown next to ③)

The chat is a separate window that remembers its size and reopens next time if it was open when you quit. Its position follows Open in center in AI Status and Settings.

Claude and Codex tabs

Each ④ tab has its own conversation, input box, attachments and model, and conversations carry on when you switch.

  • Work continues if you switch tabs during a reply; a dot shows after the busy tab's name.
  • Only one tab works at a time. Messages sent from the other tab meanwhile wait in the ⑤ queue and go out in order (Chatting).

How it works

Read this when you want to know how the AI edits your image and where your data goes. The AI model isn't inside Chickxel: the AI program you installed (the CLI) talks to the model and uses Chickxel's editor tools.

  1. When you press Send, Chickxel starts the CLI and passes it your text and images.
  2. The CLI sends the request to the Anthropic or OpenAI model with your signed-in subscription.
  3. When the model calls a tool, the editor runs it and returns the result. Each call appears as a tool line in the chat.
  4. When the reply ends, everything changed in that turn is grouped into one undo step.

How the AI reaches the editor

The AI uses the editor tools through a connection open only on this computer (the MCP server). Each time the app starts it makes a new secret value (token) and rejects requests without it.

There's nothing to set up. The connection details are passed to the CLI on each run (a temporary MCP config), so your Claude and Codex settings stay unchanged.

With Allow external AI apps on in AI > External AI Connection…, other AI apps can use the same tools with their own token (Using other AI apps).

What the AI can use

CLILimits
Claude CodeBuilt-in tools are off except web search and web fetch. Other tool servers in your settings aren't used.
Codex CLIThe shell, browser and image generation are off, and it runs without permission to change files. Project documents such as AGENTS.md aren't read.
  • Web search is used only when you ask the AI to look something up (References).
  • Image generation starts a separate Codex CLI with image generation on, just for that picture.
  • The AI pane in Effect Maker can only read the document and references and replace that editor's source.

What is sent and what is stored

  • Your messages and images, and whatever the AI looks at with tools, go through the CLI to that company's model. A web search also sends the search terms and the pages read.
  • Chats are saved on this computer under <settings folder>/Chickxel/Chats/ (Continuing past chats).

Chatting

Use this window to ask the AI to draw or edit. Type a request in the input box and send it; the AI replies and edits the open image.

To do thisHow
Send / new lineEnter (or ① Send) / Shift+Enter
Attach a reference image② Image…, paste a picture (Ctrl/⌘+V), or drop image files on the input box
Show the current frame③ Attach canvas
Change modelThe ④ Model list at the top; applies from the next message.
See past chats / start a new one⑤ New chat (shows the past chats list)
See what the AI can do, examples, and tipsThe ⑥ question mark button next to New chat
Edit or delete the last messageThe ⑦ pencil and trash icons under the last message (Editing or deleting the last message)
Stop the reply⑧ Stop (in place of Send while a reply is coming in)

Choosing a model

⑨ Default is the model set in that CLI, shown with its real name. Hover a model for its description.

The chosen model is remembered per AI, and the Effect Maker AI pane uses it too.

Attaching images: image chips

Each attached image goes into the text at the cursor as an ⑩ image chip (a thumbnail with #1, #2…).

  • Hover a chip for a ⑪ preview.
  • Delete a chip like a single character with Backspace or Delete; its attachment goes too.
  • Click a chip to see it ⑫ large, with ⑬ Copy image, Save image… and ⑭ Remove attachment.
  • The sent message shows chips (⑮) in the same places. The AI also knows each image by its chip number.
  • Reopened chats show the chips too; clicking one enlarges the saved 64 px thumbnail.

Copying messages and saving images

  • ⑯ Every message you sent has a Copy message text button under it. It copies the text only, without chips.
  • Click any other image in the chat (results, generated originals) to open the image markup window.
  • Right-click any image for Save image… and Copy image.

Sending while a reply is coming in

Messages you send while a reply is coming in wait in the queue above the input box and go out in order. Starting a new chat clears the queue.

  • ⑰ Handle: drag up or down to change the order.
  • ⑱ Pencil: brings that message back into the input box to edit; what you were typing goes into the queue instead.
  • ⑲ Trash: discards that message.
  • ⑳ Send now: stops the running turn (even another tab's) and sends that message right away.

Marking up an image to fix

Click an image in the chat, or Edit image on a result card, to open this window. Mark the spots to fix with lines and numbered comments and send it to the AI.

  • ① Draw (selected at first): drag to draw a red line where you want a change.
  • ② Comment: click to place a numbered circle and type a short note (Enter to confirm, Esc to cancel). Click a number to edit its note.
  • ③ Erase: click or drag to remove the marks you pass over.
  • ④ Undo: undoes the last mark added or erased (Ctrl+Z outside the input box).
  • ⑤ Copy image and Save image…: copy the original without marks, or save it as a PNG.
  • ⑥ The picture fits the window; small pixel art is enlarged by a whole factor so it stays sharp.
  • ⑦ Write what to change and press Enter or Send (Shift+Enter for a new line, Esc to close).

What gets sent

  • The original picture is attached, plus a copy with your marks if you made any. The comment list (number · position in % · note) follows your text.
  • With text but no marks, only the original and your text are sent; with neither, nothing is sent.
  • The message goes to the tab shown in the chat window, and this window closes. If a turn is running it joins the queue; if that AI isn't set up yet, you're warned and nothing is sent.

While the AI works: questions, notices, progress

When the AI needs a decision, a ① question card appears above the input box, switching to that tab if needed. The AI waits for your answer.

  • ② Click one of the options (2–5) to answer; your choice also appears in the chat.
  • ③ Other shows an Other answer box. Type your answer and press Enter or Submit answer.
  • ④ Cancel (or Esc in the answer box) closes the question; the AI won't guess and carry on.

Notifications

When the AI asks a question, finishes or hits an error, a notification appears at the bottom right of the screen for 6 seconds with a sound. Click it to bring the AI chat window to the front.

By default there is no notice while you're looking at the chat window, and a queue of messages notifies only after the last turn. Change this and the sound under Notifications on the AI Status tab of Preferences.

Progress display

While a reply is coming in, the ⑤ progress line at the bottom shows what's happening (thinking, writing a tool call, running in the editor and so on) and the elapsed time.

Large jobs that draw many frames can take over a minute to prepare. If the received character count keeps growing, it's working.

Editing or deleting the last message

The ① pencil (edit) and ② trash (delete) icons sit under your last message. They're disabled while a reply is coming in; a turn you stopped can be deleted.

  • Delete: removes that message and the reply after it. The previous message then gets the icons, so you can step back one message at a time.
  • Edit: removes it the same way and puts its text and image chips back in the input box to change and resend.
  • If that reply changed the drawing, you're asked Also undo the drawing changes made in this exchange? Choose ③ Undo, ④ Keep drawing or ⑤ Cancel (nothing is removed).
  • You can also delete or edit the last message of a reopened chat. The drawing question isn't asked there, and its images aren't resent.

The AI doesn't remember what you removed. The next message starts a new AI session with a recap of the remaining chat.

Continuing past chats

Chats are saved automatically each time a reply finishes. Claude and Codex keep separate lists.

  • Press ① New chat to show the ② past chats list. Each row shows the title (first message), date and model.
  • Click a row to see that chat again; messages you send continue it. The Undo this step buttons of earlier turns don't come back.
  • The ③ star makes a chat a favorite: pinned to the top and never deleted automatically. The ④ trash deletes a chat after asking (⑥).
  • Type a message in the ⑤ box below the list and send it to start a new chat.
  • Up to 20 chats are kept per AI; beyond that, the oldest non-favorite chat is deleted.
  • When you reopen the chat window or restart the app, each tab shows the chat (or list) you were last on.

Help and example requests

Press the ① question mark button next to New chat to open a help panel beside the chat, which stays usable. Press the button again or ② X to close it.

  • ③ What the AI can do: the AI's tools, explained by group (Edits the AI can make).
  • ④ Example requests: click one to put it in the message box (the help panel stays open), then edit and send it.
  • ⑤ Tips: be specific about size, palette and style, attach a reference picture, and split big jobs into steps.

Edits the AI can make

Read this to see what you can ask the AI to do and what the tool lines in the chat mean. The AI looks at the open image and edits it with Chickxel's editor tools.

The picture shows one "draw a staff" turn. The AI ① looked at the image, ② drew lines and an ellipse, and put them on ③ a new layer (Staff).

Tool list

Use this table to look up a name shown in a tool line. The groups match What the AI can do in the chat help.

CategoryWhat it doesTools
Look at the drawingReads the open document's size, frames, and layers, views it as an image, or reads exact pixel colors.get_document get_image get_pixels
Generate image → pixelateDraws characters, items and animations (all frames on one sheet) with Codex image generation and converts them to pixel art. Choose the mode and options in Drawing settings in the header.generate_pixel_art
