Turning a group off in Tools the AI can use keeps the AI from using its tools. The view and drawing tools are always on.

Tool lines

The ①② tool lines show each tool the AI called and how long it took (e.g. get_image (22s)). A failed tool also shows the error.

Linked cels

Ask "link frames 4–8" or "make that link green" and the AI links, unlinks and recolors cels (link_cels, unlink_cels, set_link_color).

Saving and temporary titles

It saves only when asked. When it draws on an unsaved new image, it gives it a short temporary title (even with Current file only on), and asks whether to save before switching to another document.

Good to know: the pixel text the AI reads

When the AI reads exact pixels, the image is sent as text. Each character stands for a color (. is transparent), with x coordinates on top and the y coordinate at the start of each row. For example:

legend:
A = #2B2B3A
B = #C0C0D0
. = transparent
rows:
   001111111111
   890123456789
30|....AAAA....
31|...ABBBBA...
32|..ABBBBBBA..

The AI finds positions from these coordinates, so it misplaces fewer pixels in wide images.

Generate image → pixelate

Use this when you want a full picture made from your words and then turned into pixel art. Ask the Codex tab; Codex makes a source picture, and you convert it to pixel art yourself.

Generating a picture

  1. Ask the Codex tab for what to draw. It uses your ChatGPT login (no API key) and takes about 30–90 seconds per image.
  2. When it's done, a result card with the original appears in the chat. Nothing is converted or inserted automatically.
  3. Press Convert, convert it in the Pixelate AI images window and insert it as a new layer or document.

Generation options

These appear in drawing settings when Auto or image generation is chosen. They're saved in your settings.

Generation optionWhat it does
⑧ Refine the generation requestOn (default): your message is reworded for image generation. Off: your text is sent as written to make one image, and the options below aren't used.
⑨ Style referenceA pixel-art example sent with the request (Auto · None · Specific reference). Auto sends one only when a reference matches.
⑩ Generation (animation)All on one sheet (recommended) (default) · One image per frame. One image per frame often changes the look and takes longer (available only with image generation).
⑪ Default animation framesIdle 4 · Walk 6 · Run 6 · Attack 6 · Jump 6 · Hurt 3 · Death 5 (2–12). A count you give in the chat wins.
  • Size: a size you mention is only a rough hint. Result size, background removal and where to insert are set in the Pixelate AI images window.
  • Background: unless you mention one, the image gets a plain transparent background.

Animations and reference images

  • Animations: ask something like "make a walking animation" and every frame is drawn side by side in one image (a sprite sheet). Watch the ⑰ progress line and cancel with ⑱ Stop. Auto makes frames one at a time.
  • Turning a reference image into pixel art: attach a picture and ask to turn it into pixel art, and a new image is generated keeping its character, pose, outfit and colors. Without a size, Codex picks one from 8–256 px when Refine the generation request is on.

Result card

  • ⑲ The result card shows the generated original. For an animation, the frames sit side by side in one image.
  • ⑳ Convert opens the Pixelate AI images window with that original (one frame per cell for an animation). Edit image opens the image markup window.

The latest 40 generation records are kept. An image whose record is gone shows Add as new layer instead of Convert.

In the Claude tab

Claude can't create images, so it always draws pixels directly, and a ㉑ red alert icon shows next to image generation in drawing settings. Explicitly ask it to "let Codex do it" to generate that picture with Codex.

Generation prompt card

  • ㉒ When generation starts, a card with your original request appears. Click it to expand ㉓ the full instruction actually sent; it stays in reopened chats.

Drawing settings

Use the drawing settings panel to choose whether the AI generates a new picture or places pixels one by one. Open and close it with the ① Drawing settings button (sliders icon) at the top of the chat.

  • ② Codex CLI status (logged in / not installed / not logged in) shows at the top.
  • ③ Hover over an option or its info icon for a longer explanation.

Drawing mode

  • ④ Auto (default): places pixels when Current file only is on, and otherwise tries image generation first. If generation fails, it places pixels instead (not after Stop).
  • ⑤ Generate image → pixelate: always generates an image (Generate image → pixelate).
  • ⑥ Draw pixels directly: the AI places pixels one by one with the pixel tools.

Only the options for the chosen mode are shown: generation options for image generation, ⑦ Use palette colors only for drawing pixels, and both for Auto.

Use palette colors only

⑦ When on, the AI paints only with colors from the active palette (off by default). Other colors become the nearest palette color.

  • Transparent (erasing) still works; semi-transparent colors become an opaque palette color.
  • It applies in both tabs and doesn't affect generated images or layer effects.
  • When off, the AI doesn't see the palette and picks whatever colors suit the picture.

Whole-picture review when drawing pixels

After drawing with pixels, the AI looks at the whole picture again and scores it out of 10. It fixes what's wrong, up to three rounds.

  • Before drawing, the AI lists the subject, size, pose and colors from your words and images and plans the layout.
  • The score is the AI's own judgment; if it doesn't improve, the AI explains the remaining problems.
  • The review and fixes are part of the same undo step, and take extra time and usage.

AI drawing tips and tools

The lower part of the drawing settings panel turns on or off the tips given to the AI and the tools it may use. Changes apply from the next message.

AI drawing tips

TipWhat it coversAt first
Drawing process (criteria, references, review)Turn the request and attachments into concrete criteria, fill in missing decisions, check references, then review the whole image with a score and fix it.Off
Characters and peopleHow to draw good character sprites: size and proportions (2-3 heads tall when small), silhouette, outline and shading order.Off
Objects and itemsIcon sizes, readable silhouettes and shading per material (metal, wood, glass).Off
Backgrounds and tilesSeamless tiles, lower contrast than characters, color by distance and texture as clusters.Off
Drawing motionsFrame counts per motion, key poses first, the same volume in every frame, durations and tags.On
In-betweeningAdd frames between two existing frames to smooth the motion (move along arcs, easing, redistribute durations).On
Clean up the current image as pixel artMake the current image cleaner pixel art without redrawing it: fewer colors, no blur or stray pixels, 1 px outline, even line steps.On
Match drawings in the open folder (consistency)Before drawing something new, look at related or recent drawings in the folder open in the file browser (including subfolders) and match their scale, outline, palette and shading. Needs the "Read drawings in the open folder" tool group.On
  • ㉔ Tips are per-task instructions that help rough requests come out as good pixel art. Only the ones that are on are sent, and the AI follows those that fit.
  • With pixel drawing they guide how the AI draws; with image generation, how it writes the generation request.
  • With image generation in the Codex tab and Refine the generation request off, the tips aren't used and this section is dimmed.
  • Match drawings in the open folder (consistency) needs Read drawings in the open folder in the tools below.
  • ㉕ The pencil button edits a tip's text. An edited tip shows ㉖ edited.

Tools the AI can use

㉗ Turns the editor tools the AI may use on or off by group; the AI can't use tools in a group that is off. Reading the document, viewing the image and drawing pixels are always on, so they aren't listed.

Image generation is set by the drawing mode, not this list. Ask the user exists only in the chat, and the external AI connection has its own choices.

The Edit tip window

  • ㉘ The instructions the AI reads. English works best, but any language is fine.
  • ㉙ Reset to default: puts the default text back. It's kept only when you save.
  • ㉚ Save: uses the edited text from the next message; an empty box means the default. Edited texts are shared by the chat and the external AI connection.

Asking for layer effects

The AI can add and change layer effects. Effects don't change pixels, so you can edit or remove them later in the Effects dialog or the ① timeline.

Asking for an effect

  • ② Ask things like "add a 1 px black outline to the character" or "add a white hit flash on frames 3–4".
  • Animated effects play over the frame range you give, or over the whole animation if you give none.
  • Effects are picked by ID (outline, hitflash and so on) or display name; your own and received effect presets can be added by name too.
  • It can also change existing effects' settings, frame range, on/off state, order, clipping and color gradients.
  • Applying effects permanently and enlarging the canvas to fit effects happen only when you ask.

Effect changes (③) are undone with the reply's one undo step (④). Effects on locked layers are never changed.

Making new effects with scripts

When no installed effect fits, the AI writes a script effect itself. For example: "Make an effect that flashes red and breaks apart when hit, as a script, and save it as my effect."

  • The AI writes a script effect on a layer and previews the result. Its scripts run without asking, in the same sandbox; if a script has an error, nothing changes.
  • A finished effect is saved as your own script effect and opened in the Effect Maker window.
  • While Effect Maker is open, the AI reads and edits its source and changes preview values and gradients (for example, "make the preview color a red-to-yellow gradient").
  • The AI adds useful settings on its own (direction, speed, color, strength and so on) and lists them in its reply.
  • Publishing is up to you; the AI can't publish effects online.

References: teach the AI your style

Use references when you want the AI to follow your style. Put in art you like, style rules and notes; the AI looks through them before drawing and checks its result against the rules.

The references window

Open it with AI > References…. On the left are ① thumbnails, a ② search box and a ③ kind filter; on the right are a ④ preview, ⑤ title, description, tags and kind, and ⑥ palette swatches.

ButtonWhat it does
⑦ Add files…Adds image or note files. You can also drag files onto the window.
⑧ Add current canvasAdds the open image.
⑨ DeleteMoves the selected reference to the app trash (built-in ones can't be deleted).
⑩ Open folderOpens the references folder in Explorer/Finder.
⑪ Edit rulesOpens your own style rules, rules.md (created from a template if missing). The AI reads them with the default rules.
⑫ Caption with AIPuts a request to caption uncaptioned references into the chat input box. It's sent only when you press send.

When some references have no description, the window shows ⑬ "N references have no description". Descriptions and tags help the AI find them.

Using references in the chat

  • Asking: say something like "Draw a 32px bat like the slime in the references." and the AI looks up the references before drawing. It searches this user guide too.
  • Adding from the chat: say "save this to my references" and the AI adds the current canvas with a title, description and tags, even with Current file only on.
  • Looking it up on the web: the AI searches the web only when you ask, for example "look it up on the web and draw it". It can't see pictures on the pages and draws from what it read.

Folder and files

  • Folder (⑭): by default <settings folder>/Chickxel/References. Files you put there outside the app appear when the window opens.
  • Supported files: images (.png, .gif, .chickxel) and text notes (.md, .txt). Titles, descriptions and other details are stored in index.json in the same folder.
  • Built-in references (marked Built-in, can't be deleted): the style rule set Chickxel default rules and five example images (slime, sword, character, slime bounce, grass tile).
  • Preferences: in Edit > Preferences… > AI references you can ⑮ change the ⑭ folder, go back to ⑯ Default or ⑰ Open folder.
  • ⑱ Show built-in examples (off by default): when on, the built-in example images are used in the list, search and style references.

References stay on your computer. Only the AI CLI you use (Claude Code or Codex) sees what the AI fetches with its tools.

Undoing

Each AI reply is one undo step, so Ctrl+Z undoes it all at once. Each reply also has a ① result image and ② Undo this step / ④ Reapply buttons.

Adding a chat image as a new layer

③ Add as new layer under an image adds it as a new layer on the current frame in transform mode. Move or resize it and press Enter.

  • It's on reply result images, image generation result cards whose record is gone, and images in reopened chats.
  • Adding is one undo step; with no document open, it opens as a new document.
  • While the AI is working, you're only told to try again when it's done.

Limiting what the AI can edit

Turn on ① Current file only below the input box before sending, and the AI edits only the image open at that moment. Locked layers are always protected, whatever this setting.

Current file only

  • Inside that image the AI can still draw, add layers, frames and skins, and add layer effects.
  • Opening or creating other files (new project, open) is refused with a ③ red line. Saving still works.
  • Tools that only write outside the document, such as adding references or saving a script effect, still work.
  • If you switch document tabs while the AI works, everything except reading is refused until that turn ends.
  • The chat shows the scope (②), like Edit scope: current file "slime.chickxel" only. Turn it off to lift the limit from the next message.

Locked layers

The AI never touches locked layers or child layers under a locked parent. Lock layers the AI must not change with the ⑤ lock icon in the timeline.

  • Changing their pixels, name, visibility, opacity, position, size, layer effects or skin is refused with a "locked" reason (④).
  • Deleting a frame where a locked layer has pixels, and resizing the canvas while any layer is locked, are refused too. Reading still works.

Using other AI apps

Use this when you want another AI app, such as Claude Desktop, Cursor or Codex CLI, to edit the image in Chickxel. Turn it on in AI > External AI Connection…; it's off at first.

Turning it on

  • ① Allow external AI apps: turn it on and press OK to start accepting connections. Turning it off cuts off connected apps.
  • ② Tools the AI can use: turns tool groups on or off for that app. Open files and image generation are off at first.
  • ③ AI drawing tips: turns on or off the tips sent to that app. On/off is separate from the chat; edited texts are shared.
  • ④ Connection settings: the settings to paste into the other app. Copy them with the button on the right (Copy connection settings).

② and ③ can be changed only while ① is on. After changing them, restart the app or reconnect its MCP server to see the change.

Registering Chickxel in another AI app

Paste what ④ copies into the app's MCP settings (where it registers outside tools). It looks like this:

{
  "mcpServers": {
    "chickxel": {
      "command": "C:\\Users\\me\\AppData\\Local\\Programs\\Chickxel\\Chickxel.exe",
      "args": ["--mcp"]
    }
  }
}

The Chickxel path is usually %LOCALAPPDATA%\Programs\Chickxel\Chickxel.exe on Windows and /Applications/Chickxel.app/Contents/MacOS/Chickxel.App on macOS. Where to paste it in each app:

AppWhere to register it
Claude DesktopAdd it to mcpServers in claude_desktop_config.json (Settings > Developer > Edit Config), then quit the app completely and start it again.
Claude CodePut it in the project's .mcp.json, or for every folder run claude mcp add --scope user chickxel -- "path to the executable" --mcp.
CursorPut it in ~/.cursor/mcp.json (one project only: .cursor/mcp.json) and check that chickxel is enabled in the MCP list.
Codex CLIAdd the TOML below to ~/.codex/config.toml. Wrap a Windows path in single quotes.
ChatGPT desktop appUses the same config.toml as Codex CLI (or add it under Settings > MCP servers in the app). Works only in Work or Codex chats.
 
[mcp_servers.chickxel]
command = 'C:\Users\me\AppData\Local\Programs\Chickxel\Chickxel.exe'
args = ["--mcp"]
tool_timeout_sec = 3600

tool_timeout_sec = 3600 keeps long tools such as image generation from being cut off at 60 seconds.

Once registered, the app lists the chickxel tools. Try asking it to "add a 1px white outline to the open image".

How to connect below ④ expands per-app setup steps. Copy guide copies them; paste them into your AI app and it helps you register Chickxel.

Connection indicator

⑤ While the connection is on, a plug icon and a dim External AI show at the right end of the status bar. When another app calls a tool, they light up for 4 seconds with the tool's name.

External AI connection: differences and fixes

Read this to see how an external AI app differs from AI chat, and what to check when it won't connect.

How it works

  1. The app starts Chickxel as a connection-only process with no window (Chickxel --mcp).
  2. Chickxel runs only the tools you allowed, and the canvas changes right away.
  3. Long tools such as image generation send progress notes while you wait.

You don't need to restart the app after restarting Chickxel.

Differences from AI chat

Check this table when something behaves differently from AI chat.

ItemAI chatExternal AI connection
ConnectionChickxel starts the CLI.The app starts Chickxel --mcp (register once).
UsageYour Claude or ChatGPT subscriptionThat app's plan, plus your Codex quota for image generation.
UndoOne reply is one step (Undoing)One tool call is one step ("AI: tool name")
AI questionsA question card (While the AI works)Asked in the app's conversation (no ask_user).
Opening filesOpens files with open_file.Only with Open files turned on (off at first)
Image generationFollows the drawing mode in drawing settings.Only when turned on. When it's done, the Pixelate AI images window opens right away.
Current file onlyTurned on with the chat button (Limiting what the AI can edit).Not used.
Locked layersNever touched.The same.
Drawing rulesGiven as the system prompt (with the tips that are on).Sent the same rules and enabled tips on connecting (some apps may ignore them).
Where you see itTool lines and result pictures in the chat windowThe app's conversation; Chickxel shows only canvas changes and the connection indicator.

Safety

  • The connection is open only on this computer and needs a secret value (token) that is new each time Chickxel starts.
  • <settings folder>/Chickxel/mcp-external.json, which holds the token, exists only while the connection is on. Other programs under your account can use it to connect, so turn the connection off when you don't need it.
  • The AI saves only when asked and never touches locked layers.
  • Images and document details the app looks at go to that app's model.

If it doesn't connect

SymptomFix
The app gets "Chickxel isn't running or its external AI connection is off"Start Chickxel and check that Allow external AI apps is on. The app then continues without a restart.
The app has no chickxel toolsCheck the config file's location and the JSON's commas and brackets, then restart the app. If you moved Chickxel, copy the connection settings again.
open_file or generate_pixel_art is missing or refused as "not available over the external AI connection"Turn that group on in Tools the AI can use and reconnect. ask_user is never available externally.
Image generation fails with "Couldn't read subject_image as a PNG image"The app can't pass the image as a base64 PNG without a data: prefix. Describe the subject in words instead.

Troubleshooting

Read this when the AI doesn't answer or can't edit your image. First press Check all again in the AI Status and Settings window to see what's blocked.

When the AI can't reach the editor

If the AI can't get the editor tools, the chat shows a ① red line. Find its message in the table below.

MessageMeaningWhat to check
No request arrivedThe CLI couldn't reach Chickxel at all.Check whether proxy settings (HTTP_PROXY and so on), a VPN or security software block connections inside this PC.
The auth token did not matchIt connected, but was refused because the secret value (token) didn't match.Update the CLI with Install or update and check again.
A request reached the server, but tools/list did notIt connected but stopped while getting ready.Try updating the CLI. GET: HTTP 405 under the message is normal.
  • Under the message, the requests Chickxel received and their response codes are listed (up to 8).
  • The red line ends with the path of the CLI that tried to connect; check that it matches the one in the AI Status window.
  • It's normal for your Claude settings to have no chickxel server. The app passes the connection details on each run as a temporary MCP config.

When the CLI path changes

If the CLI location changes during a chat (install, update and so on), the message isn't sent and "The CLI path changed. Reconnecting the session; send your message again after the check." is shown.

What looks different with Codex

Codex replies appear all at once when fully written and show only the ② time, not the cost (Claude shows the cost too).

If you distribute the app

If you distribute an app with this feature, check Anthropic's and OpenAI's usage policies (limits on using subscription logins in third-party apps).

Shortcuts

Every menu command and every key outside the menus, grouped by menu. The keys in the tables are the defaults; None marks commands without a default key. Change any key in Customizing shortcuts.

The in-app guide shows the keys you currently use in these tables. On macOS read Ctrl as ⌘ and Alt as ⌥.

PageKeys covered
Customizing shortcutsChanging, exporting and importing keys

Transform and imageEdit menu from the flips to Preferences, applying and canceling transform and size modes

Customizing shortcuts

Change any shortcut in the Shortcuts tab of Edit > Preferences…. You can export your keys to a JSON file and use them on another computer.

Shortcuts tab

The ① Shortcuts tab of Edit > Preferences… (Ctrl+,) lists menu commands, tools, tool options and keys that aren't in any menu (drop selection, arrow-key nudging, peek previous frame, selection slot and more).

  • For each row choose ② Change (use the key you press), ③ Add (one more key), ④ None or ⑤ Default. After Change or Add, the row ⑥ waits for a key (Esc cancels). Mouse buttons 4 and 5 can be assigned too, with modifiers (for example Ctrl+Mouse4); other mouse buttons and the wheel can't.
  • ⑦ Search by command, category or key, and reset everything at once with ⑧ Reset all.
  • ⑨ Export (JSON)… saves the current shortcuts to a file, and ⑩ Import (JSON)… applies them again. The same file works on both Windows and macOS.

Keys already in use

If you assign a key used by another command, ⑪ you're asked whether to remove it from that command.

Checking changed keys

Changed keys appear right away in menus (see ⑫), toolbar tooltips and the in-app guide. ⑬ Help > Shortcut list shows only the default keys and mouse actions.

Editing and selection

Use these keys to undo, copy, paste and select quickly from the keyboard. They cover the top of the Edit menu plus selection keys that aren't in any menu.

Menu commands

CommandDefault keyWhat it does
UndoCtrl+ZUndoes the last action. In the file list it restores the last deleted file.
RedoCtrl+YRedoes an undone action. Ctrl+Shift+Z works too.
CutCtrl+XCuts the selection to the clipboard.
CopyCtrl+CCopies the selection, or the whole frame if nothing is selected.
Copy images only (no links)Ctrl+Shift+CIn the timeline, copies the selected cels or frames as plain images without links (see Editing the timeline). Elsewhere it works like Copy.
PasteCtrl+VPastes the clipboard image as a selection you can move.
DeleteDeleteClears the pixels in the selection. On macOS ⌫ works too.
Select allCtrl+ASelects the whole canvas.
DeselectCtrl+DRemoves the selection.

Keys outside the menus

CommandDefault keyWhat it does
Drop selectionEnterDrops pasted or moved pixels in place. While transforming, applies the transform.
Deselect (canvas)EscCancels a transform or size mode, otherwise removes the selection.
Move 1pxCtrl+Shift+Left Ctrl+Shift+Right Up DownMoves the selection (with the Move tool, the cel's pixels) by 1 pixel.
Move 8pxShift+Left Shift+Right Shift+Up Shift+DownMoves the same thing by 8 pixels.

Good to know

  • Undo works per stroke, 1000 steps by default. You can raise it to 10000 in Preferences.
  • For sideways moves add Ctrl+Shift. Plain ←→ go to the previous and next frame.
  • In a moving mode (Move tool, transform, canvas size, arranging an empty layer's children), plain ←→ move too after you click the canvas.

Keys in the menu

The Edit menu shows the keys from Undo to Deselect at ①.

Keys outside the menus and the mouse

CommandDefault keyWhat it does
Tap Space to play/pause previewSpaceA short tap of Space plays or stops the preview; holding it while dragging pans the view.
Add multiple frames from the next-frame cellDrag the Add frame button right and releaseAdds that many empty frames at once. The copy button duplicates the last frame that many times.

Lock all other layersAlt+click the lockLocks every layer except this one. Do it again to unlock all.

Good to know

  • Skin switching isn't recorded in undo history. Layers and skins are covered in the Layer tree · skins chapter.
  • Stepping frames with arrow keys works from any panel unless you're typing text. After you click the timeline grid they move the cel selection, and in a moving mode they nudge by 1px.
  • If the current layer is a child, a new layer goes above its topmost parent, and an empty layer stays under the same parent.
  • With Alt+click, the layer's parent and child layers are kept too. One undo reverts it.

Keys in the menu

The Frame·Layer menu shows the keys from Play / stop to Next skin at ①.

Language

Chickxel includes Korean and English. You can add other languages with a translation CSV, and edit the built-in text with a CSV too. A new display language takes effect after a restart.

PageRead it when
Changing the display languageYou switch the display to Korean, English or a language you added
Creating a new languageYou translate Chickxel into a language that isn't in the list
Editing and removing built-in textYou edit built-in text or remove a language you added

Changing the display language

Choose the display language at the top of the General tab in Edit > Preferences…. A new language takes effect after Chickxel restarts.

Choosing a language

  • Open ① Edit > Preferences… (Ctrl+,). ② Language is at the top.
  • Choose from the ③ Display language list and click ④ OK. Languages added from a CSV appear as Name (code), and built-in languages edited by a CSV as Name (edited by CSV).

Restarting to apply

  • Click ⑤ Restart now to close the app and reopen it in the new language (you're asked about unsaved documents first). Choose ⑥ Later to apply it the next time you launch.

The language on first launch

On first launch Chickxel picks the language matching your computer's UI language: an exact match with region first (pt-BR), then the parent language (pt), otherwise English. It picks again the same way if the file of the CSV language you chose is removed.

What the language changes

ItemLanguage shown
Menus, windows and messagesThe chosen language; untranslated text uses the same built-in language, or English
Guide (F1)Korean when Korean is chosen, otherwise English
Changes in the update dialogNotes in the chosen language, or English (see Updates)
Help > Website and the Website button in the About windowThe Korean website for Korean, otherwise the English website
Number and date formatsUnchanged (they follow your computer's settings)

Creating a new language

To make a new language, export the translation CSV, add a language column, translate it and add the file back. Edit the CSV in a spreadsheet such as Excel or Google Sheets. You can add a partial translation; empty cells show English.

Exporting and translating

  1. Save a CSV file with ① Export translation CSV… in Preferences. You can open it in Excel, Google Sheets and so on.
  2. The first row is key,ko,en and the second row is #name,한국어,English. Add a new column at the far right with the language code in the first row (e.g. ja) and the language name in the second row (e.g. 日本語).
  3. Translate the text in that column. Braces such as {0} and {name} are placeholders for values, so keep them as they are (you may change their order).

CSV layout

RowContent
First rowkey and the language codes (key,ko,en,…). A language code uses letters, digits, - and _, up to 32 characters.
Second row#name and the name shown in the list for each language (#name,한국어,English,…)
Other rowsThe text key (<table>.<key>, e.g. UiStrings.Language_Label) and the text for each language. Don't change the keys.

The exported file is comma-separated UTF-8. Importing reads comma, semicolon and tab separators, so it's fine if Excel saves with semicolons. One file can contain several language columns.

Adding and applying it

  • Choose the file with ② Add language from CSV… and it appears in the ③ language list. If you added just one new language, it is selected for you. Click OK and restart to apply it.
  • The number of strings added for each language appears below the list ("{name} ({code}): added {count} strings.").
  • Columns that aren't valid language codes are skipped and keys Chickxel doesn't have are ignored; the counts are reported.

Adding the same language code again overwrites it. Added languages are saved as <settings folder>/Chickxel/Languages/<language code>.csv.

Editing and removing built-in text

You can override Korean or English text you don't like with a CSV, and undo added languages or edited text by deleting their file in the language folder.

Editing built-in text

Edit the ko or en column in an exported CSV and add it with Add language from CSV…: only the cells that differ from the built-in text are saved, and only those override it. If no cell differs, that language's CSV is removed.

Removing an added language

  • ① Open language folder opens the folder containing the CSV files of added languages. Deleting a file removes that language from the list, or, for a file that edited a built-in language, restores the original text.

Glossary

Chickxel terms used in this guide and what they mean. Click a term to open the page that explains it. In the guide text, the first mention of a term on each page links to the same place.

TermMeaning
9-sliceA Unity sprite border that splits an image into nine parts so its corners stay crisp when stretched.
account level (level badge, level chip)Your Chickxel account's level, which rises with the XP you earn by sharing and shows as an Lv badge next to your name.
active paletteThe palette you last clicked or edited; Edit-menu and AI palette tools use it.
Add rangeThe effect range given to effects newly added in the effects dialog.
AI chatThe window where you ask Claude or Codex to edit the drawing.
AI drawing tips (drawing tips)Per-task tips given to the AI in advance so rough requests still come out as good pixel art; each can be turned on or off and edited.
AI references (reference library)A library of images, notes and rules on your computer that the AI looks through before drawing.
AI Status window (AI Status and Settings)The window that checks CLI installation, version, sign-in and MCP connection, and installs or signs in.
AI tools (MCP tools)The editor functions Chickxel exposes over MCP for the AI to read and edit the image.
allowing scripts to run (allowed to run)Deciding that a script effect you haven't seen before may run on this computer.
animated effect (animated effects)An effect that changes over its effect range (flash, shake, explode and so on).
animated tile (animated tiles)A tile that cycles through several images at a set time per image.
app trashChickxel's own trash that keeps deleted files and old export outputs for a while (separate from the Windows or macOS trash; File > View app trash…).
apply effect permanently (apply permanently, applied permanently)Bakes an effect into the cel pixels and removes it from the effect list.
Apply to frames (frame range)A brush tool setting that applies a stroke to the other frames in a chosen range at the same spot as on the current frame.
Apply to layersAn eraser setting that applies the stroke at the same spot to other layers in the same frame (above, below, all or selected layers) as well as the current one.
Asset ManagerThe window for installing, importing and publishing assets such as effects and gradients.
auto layoutA layer setting that lines its direct children up by size and re-lines them up whenever the children change.
Auto optimizeSearches each checked preset for the highest-scoring values of the steps that have Optimize on.
auto tile rule (auto tile rules)One entry of an auto tile set: conditions on the 8 neighbors plus the tiles to place. Rules are tried from the top.
auto tile set (auto tile sets, auto tiles, auto tile)A set of rules that places tiles with the right edge shape by looking at the 8 neighbors of each cell.
autosaveSaves automatically after a short pause.
base positionThe layer position shared by all frames; per-frame distances are added to it.
big tile (big tiles)A tile whose footprint is larger than 1×1; it replaces every tile it overlaps on the same floor.
brush shape (brush size)The shape (square, circle, diamond) and size (1–32) that brush tools such as the pencil and eraser paint at once.
built-in tilesetThe Sandbox surface & caves and Top-down village & water tilesets shipped with the app; projects receive copies.
candidate tile (candidate row)A tile in the Pixelate window showing one preset's result and score; click to select it, double-click to apply it.
canvasThe central area where you view the current frame and draw.
canvas pivotThe canvas's own pivot used for sheet and Unity exports; with a representative layer it follows that layer's pivot.
canvas size modeA mode for setting the new canvas size and image position directly on the canvas.
cel (cels)The drawing of one layer in one frame — a single timeline cell.
cel shadingShading that cuts brightness into a few flat bands.
cell name (cell names)The sprite name of each sheet cell (by frame number or by tag).
Chickxel accountThe chickxel.com account used for gallery and asset uploads (sign in with Google, GitHub or Discord).
child layer (child layers)A layer nested under another layer. It has its own size and position and is drawn above its parent's picture.
Choose help on screen (help picker)Press F1, then click a button or panel on screen to open its guide page.
Claude CodeAnthropic's command-line AI tool. Signed in with a Claude subscription, it powers the Claude tab of AI chat.
Clean up lines (line cleanup)An Edit menu command that finds the outlines and inner lines of the current layer and redraws them as clean 1 px lines and arcs with even steps.
clipping (clip to effect above)A setting that applies an effect only to the pixels made by the effect above it.
Codex CLIOpenAI's command-line AI tool. Signed in with a ChatGPT subscription, it powers the Codex tab and image generation.
color decisionThe row in the enlarged view that shows how one pixel's color was decided at each step.
color distanceHow closeness between two colors is measured (OKLCh hue-weighted, CIEDE2000, OKLab), used when cells pick palette colors.
color pickerThe panel that sets the color being edited with a square, honeycomb, wheel or sliders.
color spaceThe color model used to blend a smooth gradient (RGB, HSL, OKLab, OKLCH).
Contiguous onlyA setting that limits fill and the magic wand to the area connected to the clicked pixel.
convert to palette colorsReplaces the image's colors with the nearest palette colors.
copy images only (images-only copy, images-only copies)Copies timeline cels or frames as images only without their link groups.
Current file onlyA chat toggle that limits the AI to the image open when you send.
curve pen (Curve Pen)A drawing tool that builds and adjusts a curve (Bézier) path from anchor points and handles, then paints it as a clean 1 px line with even steps.
display languageThe language of menus, windows and messages; changing it takes effect after a restart.
ditheringMixing two colors in a dot pattern so they read as an in-between color; used by the dithering tool, gradients and palette conversion.
document tab (document tabs)A tab above the canvas for each open document.
Dodge/burn (Lighten/darken)A tool that rubs painted pixels lighter (left button) or darker (right button).
Draw pixels directlyA drawing mode where the AI places pixels one by one with the pixel tools.
Drawing settings (drawing settings)The chat panel that sets the drawing mode and generation options.
easingHow an animated effect speeds up and slows down over its range.
Effect MakerThe window for writing, previewing and publishing script effects.
effect paddingThe space an effect reserves outside the image to draw in (outline thickness, shadow offset and so on).
effect preset (effect presets)Several effects and their values saved together under a name.
effect range (effect ranges)The frames an effect applies to: all frames or a start–end frame.
effect row (effect rows, effect box)The row each effect gets under its layer in the timeline, with a box showing its range.
effects dialogThe dialog for adding effects to one layer, changing their order, settings and ranges, and previewing them.
empty layer (empty layers)A parent layer that only holds its children and has no picture of its own (created with New empty layer).
empty layer viewThe canvas state with an empty layer selected, where you see the frame composite and drag the child layers into place.
end chickThe chick shown on the last frame of a transform effect's preview.
enlarged previewA resizable window that plays the preview enlarged.
eraserA tool that erases pixels to transparent (or by a strength and channels).
error log (error logs)Files describing unexpected errors; the latest 20 are kept in Logs in the settings folder.
excluded color (excluded colors)Removing one color from the selected result, replacing its pixels with the nearest remaining color.
export cell name (export cell names, cell naming rule)The name of a cell (Unity sprite) in an exported sheet, set per frame or generated by the naming rule.
Export dialogThe dialog that exports your picture as sheet images, GIFs, game engine files and more.
export on saveThe checkbox on each export list row; when on, that row's outputs are made whenever you save. The sheet row of a new project has it on.
export path encryptionWriting a project file's export folder and names locked with this install's own key (local-path-key.bin), so others can't see your folder paths.
export preset (export presets)Named export settings you can reuse in other projects.
Extend framesAn effects dialog setting that adds frames to an effect's range to create animation frames.
external AI connectionA setting that lets other AI apps use the running Chickxel's editor tools through Chickxel --mcp.
eyedropperA tool that picks a canvas color into the primary or secondary color.
fast pathThe execution path that converts and compiles a script's source to run it fast.
ffmpegAn external program used to open videos, AVIF and HEIC files; it isn't bundled with Chickxel and must be installed separately.
FidelityThe largest score term, measuring how closely the result matches the background-removed source cells.
file associationThe OS registration that makes double-clicking a .chickxel in Explorer or Finder open Chickxel.
file browserThe left panel that shows a folder's images as thumbnails and opens them.
first skin (base skin)The skin at the front of a layer's skin list; it uses the plain cell names in the layer atlas.
Fit cells by rowA Quick setting that starts from a uniform grid, fits cell boundaries per row and column to the source's color edges within 30%, and keeps the grid fixed during optimization.
Fit outline to 1 pxHow a thick outer outline becomes 1 px: By shrinking, Thin outline only, Redraw as vector or Off.
fit to windowSwitches to the largest zoom that fits the whole image in the canvas area.
floating selectionSelected pixels that were lifted or pasted and not yet dropped.
footprint (footprints)The number of cells (width × height) a tile covers on the map; its image may be larger.
frame (frames)One picture of the animation: a timeline column with its own duration (ms).
frame durationHow long one frame is shown (ms).
frame header (frame headers)The frame number cell in the timeline's top row; click it to select the frame.
frame sheetA PNG with every frame laid out in a grid.
frame splitThe step that splits a source with several frames into one frame each.
Generate image → pixelateA drawing mode that makes a picture with Codex image generation and converts it to pixel art.
GIF animation (animated PNG)An Export dialog format that makes a single animation file (GIF or APNG) for sharing.
gradient pencilA pencil that paints along the stroke in gradient colors.
gradient preset (gradient presets)A gradient saved under a name with its stops, interpolation, color space and dithering.
gradient stop (color stop, opacity stop)A point on the gradient bar that sets which color (or opacity) sits at which position.
gradient toolA tool that paints a linear gradient by dragging.
grid detectionFinding how many source pixels make one "pixel" and where the cell boundaries are.
guide windowThe in-app window for searching and browsing this guide (Help > Guide…).
hue shiftA shading method that turns the hue toward yellow when lightening and toward blue/purple when darkening (the default lighten/darken mode).
image chip (image chips)The marker for an attached image inside the message text: a thumbnail and a #number.
image markup windowA window where you mark the spots to fix on an image in the chat with lines and numbered comments and send it to the AI.
Import 3D modelThe window that draws a 3D model into pixel-art frames after you set its size, angle, light, outlines and frames.
Import SettingsThe Unity Texture Importer settings written to the .meta file.
Import videoThe window that extracts a video into frames after you set the range, frame rate and size.
info box (info boxes, info badge, info badges)The small rounded square with an i. Hover or click it to show a longer explanation; click again to close it.
join group (join groups)A name that makes auto tile sets sharing it connect without an edge, even on different floors.
keep the end lookKeeps an animated effect's final appearance on frames after its range.
keyboard shortcut (keyboard shortcuts)Keys that run a command directly; you can change them in Preferences.
language folderThe Languages folder in the settings folder where added languages and edited text are stored.
lasso select (lasso)A selection tool that selects the inside of an outline you draw.
layer (layers)One sheet you paint on. It is a timeline row; upper layers cover lower ones.
layer atlasA PNG that packs the drawings of the layers in the layer tree, one cell per layer × frame.
layer colorsThe colors used in every frame of the current layer, most used first.
layer effect (layer effects)Effects such as outline, shadow or flash that are layered on without changing the pixels.
layer pivot (layer pivots)The pivot each layer has (measured against the canvas for a normal layer, against its own area for a layer with its own size); that layer's effects, transform rotation center, symmetry axis and layer atlas sprite use it.
layer treeThe structure formed by nesting layers under other layers as children, several levels deep.
level reward (level rewards)A profile decoration unlocked at each level from 2 to 100 (title, level badge, frame, effect, card or nickname effect); every slot is on Auto by default.
linked cels (cel link)A link that makes several cels share one drawing; editing one changes them all.
linked copy (linked copies)Copying timeline cels together with their link groups, so cels pasted into the same row join the original group.
local cell correctionRe-fits the image-wide cell lines band by band to the real color edges.
locked layer (locked layers)A layer locked with the timeline padlock so that drawing and AI edits can't change it.
magic wandA selection tool that selects the area of similar color around the click (same settings as Fill).
magic wand previewA magic wand feature that tints blue the pixels a click under the mouse would select, and red the ones that would leave the selection.
MCPModel Context Protocol, the connection the AI CLI uses to call Chickxel's editor tools.
merge filesCombines several images selected in the file browser into one .chickxel.
meta file (meta files)The Unity settings file (.meta) next to an exported image, holding sprite cells, pivots and the guid.
middle fillChooses whether the middle is stretched (Stretch) or its pattern repeated (Tile) when stretching with 9-slice.
MixamoAdobe's free web service that rigs humanoid 3D characters and lets you download animations for them.
move pivot alongMoves the center (pivot) of later effects by as much as a moving effect moved the image.
Move toolThe tool that moves the selection or the whole cel; Ctrl turns any tool into it while held.
Movement testA window (F5) where you move a character as in a top-down or platformer game using its tag animations.
my assetsAssets made on this computer or published with your account; you edit them in place.
named group thereA condition matching a neighboring auto tile on any floor whose join group matches the rule’s Group field.
nearest neighborA scaling and rotation method that uses the nearest original pixel without blending colors.
New project dialogThe dialog that creates a new document from a canvas size and a background setting.
number field (number fields)A field you change by clicking to type, using the up/down buttons at its right end, or picking from its right-click list.
official effect (official effects)The 23 script effects the app installs by default (Outline, Shadow, Hit flash and so on).
onion skinA view option that overlays neighboring frames translucently.
Optimize criteriaThe per-preset budget (trials, seconds) and scoring rules for Clean up and Auto optimize.
paletteA list of colors to paint with. You can stack several and switch between them.
palette web (Lospec)A window for browsing and importing palettes from lospec.com.
parent layer (parent layers)A layer that has child layers. Hiding, locking, opacity and moving apply to its children as well.
peek previous frameShows the previous frame on the canvas while the key is held.
pencilThe basic drawing tool that paints pixels in the brush shape.
per-frame pivotA pivot (and for a layer with its own size, position) kept separately from a given frame to the end with 'From current frame on'.
per-frame position (per-frame positions)A layer placement that differs from the base position in a particular frame.
pick cardThe card in the tool options showing the tile or auto tile set about to be placed and its target floor.
pick from screenA color popup button that picks a color anywhere on the screen, other apps included.
ping-pongA tag direction that plays to the end and back.
pinned color (pinned colors)A color picked from a source cell and added to the result palette; only pixels closer to it change.
pivotThe reference point for rotation and placement, exported as the Unity sprite pivot.
Pivot toolThe tool for setting the pivot by dragging on the canvas or with buttons and numbers.
pixel font (pixel fonts)A font drawn on a pixel grid; Galmuri, Unifont and others come with the app.
pixel gridA view option that outlines every pixel when zoomed in.
pixel perfectAn option that removes the doubled L-shaped pixels at stair corners when drawing at size 1, leaving a one-pixel line.
Pixel stepsA setting that joins the pixels of shape lines and curves, or of pencil, gradient pencil and eraser strokes, diagonally (Diagonal) or only sideways and up/down (Orthogonal); the Shape tool and the pencil remember it separately.
Pixelate AI image (Pixelate)The window that turns AI-made or large images into clean pixel art.
pixels outside the canvasPixels pushed past the canvas edge. They are kept and can be brought back.
Prefer shade familyPrefers a palette color of the cell's own hue family over a different hue of similar lightness.
preset parameter (preset parameters)A value in an effect preset that changes settings of several effects inside it together.
preview boardThe 12×9 board in the auto tile set editor for trying the set you are editing; it isn't saved.
preview chickThe 20 numbered chick pictures that effect and preset previews apply the effect to.
preview position and pivotDeclaration values that move the end chick and each frame's pivot in effect previews.
preview sceneA small drawing used with the chick to preview effects that read other layers.
preview tabA green-tinted tab that plays the document being edited, enlarged, in the other pane.
primary color (Primary)The color painted with the left mouse button.
project file (project files)The .chickxel file that holds everything you edit: layers, frames, effects, export settings and more.
publish onlineThe window that publishes one of your assets to the Store and chickxel.com with your Chickxel account.
question cardThe card with options that appears above the input box when the AI asks you to decide.
received assets (received asset)Assets by others from the Store or files; copy one to My assets to change it.
recent colorsThe last 16 colors you painted with, newest first.
recent filesThe list of the last 10 opened or saved files, in the File menu and on the start screen.
recovery file (recovery files)A .chickxel saved separately for an unsaved document when an error forces the app to quit.
Replace color brush (color replace brush)A brush that, where you rub, changes only the color of the pixel where the stroke started.
representative layerThe layer whose pivot the canvas pivot follows in every frame (crown before its name).
resamplingHow rotated or scaled pixels take their colors from the original (RotSprite / Nearest).
Revert to savedA command that discards unsaved edits and rereads the file from disk.
Right button = eraserAn option that makes the right button of the pencil, gradient pencil, shape and fill tools erase instead of using the secondary color.
rotation centerThe point transform mode and Transform whole layer rotate around; it starts at the current layer's pivot.
RotSpriteA pixel-art rotation method that samples an 8× enlarged copy so rotated lines break less (no new colors).
score term (score breakdown)The terms, such as Fidelity and 1-px outline, and their weights that make up a candidate's 0–100 score.
script APIThe declaration, settings, img, ctx and other names and rules used in a script effect's source.
script effect (script effects)An effect written in JavaScript.
secondary color (Secondary)The color painted with the right mouse button (it erases when Right button = eraser is on).
seedA number that fixes the look of a random effect; the same seed always gives the same look.
sel-outDrawing an outline with darkened versions of the colors beneath it instead of one color.
selectionThe area chosen with a selection tool; painting, erasing and moving stay inside it.
settings folder (app settings folder)The Chickxel folder holding preferences, undo history, the app trash and logs (%AppData%\Chickxel on Windows).
shape pen (shape set)A drawing tool that stamps small pictures (leaves, flowers, sparkles, chick characters and more) from the chosen set. Eight built-in sets come with it; you can make your own sets, edit them on the canvas, and get or publish sets in the Store.
Shape toolA tool that drags out 11 shapes such as lines, curves, rectangles, ellipses and stars.
show effects on canvasA view option that decides whether layer effects are shown on the editing canvas.
skin (skins)One set of drawings for a layer (outfit, body type and so on). Each layer can keep several and swap between them.
skin chip (skin chips)The small label after the timeline name of a layer with more than one skin, showing its current skin; click it for the skin list and Skin settings….
skin preset (skin presets)A named set of skins, one per layer, for several layers. Applying it changes only the layers it contains.
skin rowThe row below the preview where you pick a skin preset and each layer's skin.
Skin settings windowThe window that shows the skin presets and each layer's skins as cards for editing.
something thereAn auto tile condition (orange triangle) that matches when the neighbor holds the same group or any tile on the chosen floors.
split viewThe canvas divided into left and right panes showing two documents.
Sprite LibraryThe .spriteLib asset Unity uses to swap skins.
start screenThe screen shown with no document open, where you choose a folder, recent file or Unity Hub project.
status barThe bottom line showing coordinates, color, size and save state.
style rulesThe pixel-art rules the AI follows when drawing: the built-in rules plus your own rules.md.
symmetry drawing (horizontal symmetry, vertical symmetry)An option that also draws on the opposite side of the canvas center (or pivot) axis.
tag (tags)A named frame range that groups an animation (for example idle or run).
target colorsA color count above which (or, when Strict, below which) the score is reduced.
temporary MCP configThe MCP server address and token Chickxel passes straight to the CLI on each run, separate from your CLI settings.
text boxA text area dragged out with the Text tool; lines wrap within its width.
the Store (Store tab)The Asset Manager tab for finding and installing effects, gradients and shape pen sets published on chickxel.com.
tile floor (tile floors)An ordered set of cells inside a tilemap layer; the same floor overwrites, different floors stack.
tile gridA view option that draws a line every tile of the chosen size.
tile tools (tile brush, auto tile brush)The tools used on tilemap layers instead of the pixel tools: tile brush, auto tile brush, eraser, rectangle, fill, picker and cell select.
tiled modeA view option that repeats the image around itself to check seamless patterns.
tilemap layer (tilemap layers)A layer that builds a map by placing tileset tiles on a grid of cells; every frame uses the same map.
tileset (tilesets)A collection of tiles, floors and auto tile sets for tilemap layers, stored as a copy in the project.
tileset file (tileset files)A .chickxel-tileset file holding one tileset, so several projects can share it.
Tileset panelThe panel on the left, shown for tilemap layers, where you pick tiles and auto tile sets to place.
Tileset settings windowThe window that edits one tileset's tiles, floors, auto tile sets, file and view; changes apply right away.
timelineThe bottom panel that shows frames (columns) and layers (rows) as cels.
toleranceHow different a color may be and still count as the same for fill and magic wand.
tool line (tool lines)A chat line showing an editor tool the AI called, its result and how long it took.
Tool options (tool options)The right-dock panel where you change the current tool's settings.
toolbarThe vertical bar on the far left for picking tools.
top barThe bar below the menus with the tool name, view options and zoom.
transform modeA mode for rotating, scaling and moving the selected pixels with handles.
transform whole layerA mode that rotates, scales, moves and distorts every frame of one layer (every skin of a part) together with one orange box.
translation CSVA spreadsheet file with every UI string as a key and one column per language; add a column to make a new language.
turntable (turntables)A frame mode that turns a 3D model one full circle and draws one image per evenly spaced direction, used for 8-direction sprites.
tutorial (tutorials)An in-app guide that teaches a feature step by step with the spot to use lit up on screen and a bubble next to it (Help > Tutorial…).
undo historyUndo steps that survive closing and reopening a file.
Undo this stepThe button under an AI reply that undoes everything that reply changed.
update dialog (automatic update)The dialog that shows a new version and its changes, then downloads, installs and restarts.
Upload to galleryThe window that uploads a picture to the chickxel.com gallery.
Use palette colors onlyA drawing setting that snaps the colors the AI paints to the nearest color in the current palette.
Use the source outlineA 7. Cleanup setting that makes Fit outline to 1 px peel only the cells detected as outline on the source.
wrap aroundA Move tool option that brings pixels pushed off one side back on the other side.
XPPoints from gallery pictures, shared assets, likes, daily check-ins and app time that raise your account level.
zoom levelHow much the canvas is magnified (1–64×, whole steps).
